# THREAD -- the book's argument so far


> Status: parts Introduction and Foundations are APPROVED (see `part-verdict.md`). The `Owed` lists below are the work remaining for the Simulation part onward.
One entry per approved chapter, in book order.

## 00-hybrid-programme -- The hybrid breeding programme, and the population genetics it rests on
- Question: what the selection problem of the preface is a part of -- one cycle of a two-pool hybrid programme, its four stages and the steps at which diversity can be lost -- and which population-genetic quantities, each defined with the identity later chapters lean on, the book assumes. It follows the preface because the preface states the optimisation problem and this chapter states the programme around it (00:8-16).
- Establishes:
  - A1-A8 restated for use before @sec-era-programme declares them; neutrality holds only in this chapter's diversity examples, not in the book's data (`simulate_data()` traits have effects at every marker) (00:18-42).
  - Selfing series $1 - F^{(t)} = (1/2)^t$, superscript counting generations of the mating system under discussion; DH reaches $F = 1$ in one step; the F1 of two homozygous lines is determined without sampling (00:78-96).
  - @eq-hp-gca-sca, the GCA/SCA model on cross means $\bar y_{ij}$ (Design II), defined relative to a reference set of crosses; the dominance part of GCA is an average effect at the opposite pool's frequencies, so under dominance GCA is a property of a line against a tester pool (00:98-132); illustration, dominance part alone, one draw: correlation 0.859 between two tester halves, 3 of top 5 shared (00:153-171).
  - S1-S4 and @tbl-hp-stages, distinct from the package's stage 1/2 and from trial stages; testing in cross and recycling are not stages; S4 is the preface's problem (00:173-197).
  - Recycling within pool only (A3); the scheme is what @kadoumi2025 call tester-based or modified RRS, as their report, not the book's attribution (00:199-211); `run_cycles()` is a reduced form of the loop (no trials, no S3, index known; 11b C7, C8) (00:213-219); @fig-hp-cycle (00:221-265).
  - Random-subset bias: a random subset of $n_{sel}$ of $N$ has expected $\theta_S$ larger by $(1/n_{sel} - 1/N)(\bar f_d - \bar f_o)$; a selected subset has no such guarantee (00:267-282); measured: candidates 0.3281, random 0.3209 (expected 0.3210), diversity greedy 0.3296 (00:304-309); hence $\alpha = (GD_{\text{ref}} - GD_S)/GD_{\text{ref}}$ with $GD_{\text{ref}}$ the mean GD of random subsets of the same size; the algebra covers S3 and S4, not S2 (00:310-321).
  - Where the largest loss falls depends on the S2 cross-selection strategy; the ledger is 08's, cached, S2-S4 only (00:322-331).
  - Under drift alone in two closed pools of equal weight, between-pool divergence rises while within-pool diversity falls; not an argument for selection; the rise of $GD_{BS}$ under `run_cycles()` is a simulation result with drift and selection not separated (00:333-341). @kadoumi2025 numbers and limits (00:343-360).
  - Local symbols $p_k$, $q_k$, $p_{Ak}$, $p_{Bk}$, $\bar p_k$, $p_{0,k}$ (00:364-368); Hardy-Weinberg used only as a reference, never as a description of a pool of lines or a set of single crosses (00:370-383).
  - @eq-hp-gd, $GD_S = 1 - \theta_S = H_e$ (plug-in Nei, own frequencies); $GD_T$ is $GD_S$ for a set spanning the pools, partitioned over usage-weighted parent lines, $GD_{WI} = 0$ with inbred parents; $H_o$ defined (00:385-406); $H_o = GD_T + GD_{BS}$ on the complete factorial, 0.3617 = 0.3281 + 0.0336 at the teaching scale (00:435-447).
  - IBD/IBS; pedigree coancestry $A_{ij}/2$; molecular $f_{ij}$; @eq-hp-ibs-ibd with base $p_{b,k}$ (distinct from the frozen $p_{0,k}$): $f$ is a floor set by base frequencies plus scaled pedigree coancestry, so unrelated pairs are not at 0 and $\mathbf f$ is read as a comparison between sets on the same markers (00:449-525).
  - $s_i = (1 + F_i)/2$; $\theta_S$ includes the diagonal (00:527-533); molecular $F_i$; $F_{(ab)} = f^L_{ab}$; $\theta_S = \mathbf w'\mathbf f^L\mathbf w$ in line usage (00:537-559).
  - The letter $F$: $F_{drift}$ (against the frozen base, never negative), $F_{hom}$ (can be negative), both on merged hybrid frequencies; $F_{ST} = GD_{BS}/GD_T$, which 08 calls $G_{ST}$, against $(\theta_w - \theta_{AB})/(1 - \theta_{AB})$, near twice it; $F_{BN}$ a simulation setting (00:561-585).
  - @eq-hp-drift ($\Delta F = 1/n_{gam}$, $N_e = 1/(2\Delta F)$); in a DH pool $\Delta F$ is the rate of rise of group coancestry (00:587-614); a crossing block of $n$ passes $2n$ copies ($N_e = n$, approximate without selfing), a pool of $n$ DH lines passes $n$ ($N_e = n/2$); simulated on individuals: 0.597 (0.599), 0.612 (0.613), 0.346 (0.349) (00:616-695). Selection is outside @eq-hp-drift, and 11b's rates are on another level (00:696-700). $N_s$ is static and lies in $[1/2, 1]$ on molecular $\mathbf f$ (00:701-707).
  - The three departures from the animal-breeding setting (00:709-732); reading map (00:734-754).
- Assumes: A2 (inbred parents) for $GD_{WI} = 0$, $F_{(ab)} = f^L_{ab}$, $\theta_S = \mathbf w'\mathbf f^L\mathbf w$; the complete A x B factorial for $H_o = GD_T + GD_{BS}$ and the GCA row-effect check; two pools; neutral, unlinked markers, a known base and HW parents for @eq-hp-ibs-ibd; neutrality, discrete generations, no selection, mutation or migration for @eq-hp-drift; equal pool weights and closed pools for the drift trade-off; the dominance-only GCA example ($a_k \equiv 0$, gamma $d_k$, illustrative); the teaching scale (A8) and one seed for every number.
- Takes from: index: the problem statement, $n_{sel}$ of $N$ under $\alpha_{\max}$ against a random-subset reference (index:18-47), and the three departures (index:48-66). Everything else is a forward pointer, declared as such (00:15-16): `05`: A1-A8, $F_{drift}$, $F_{hom}$; `01`: @eq-x-freq, @eq-hybrid-dosage, @eq-f-molecular; `02`: @eq-gd-partition; `05b`: GCA dominance part, $H_o$ identity (now @eq-het-wi-hyb in @sec-heterosis-hybrid-identity); `04e`: the GCA model; `06`: @sec-baseline, @sec-identity, $N_s$; `07`: @sec-collapse; `08`: stages, ledger, $G_{ST}$ form; `11b`: C7, C8, $GD_{BS}$ trajectories.

## 01-coancestry -- Coancestry, and the two matrices
- Question: how the hybrid marker matrix $\mathbf X$ is built from the line genotypes, why the VanRaden $\mathbf G$ cannot measure diversity (the total of its elements is zero by construction), and what the molecular coancestry $\mathbf f$ used instead is, including what its scale is and is not, and the two published treatments that arrive at it. It follows 00 because 00 has already defined IBD/IBS, coancestry, $s_i$, $F_i$ and $GD_S = 1 - \theta_S$ in words and points here for @eq-x-freq, @eq-hybrid-dosage and @eq-f-molecular; 01 supplies that computation and the contrast with $\mathbf G$ (01:8-22).
- Establishes:
  - Hybrid dosage $M^H_{(ab)k} = (M^L_{ak} + M^L_{bk})/2 \in \{0,1,2\}$ from homozygous parents, a deterministic genotype (@eq-hybrid-dosage, 01:24-45); F1 heterosis = sum of $d_k$ over the loci where the parents differ, no epistasis, $d_k$ defined at first use as the departure of the heterozygote from the homozygote midpoint (01:41-46, pointing to @eq-het-value); $x_{ik} = M^H_{ik}/2 \in \{0, 0.5, 1\}$ and $\mathbf X = \mathbf M^H/2$ (@eq-x-freq, 01:48-61).
  - VanRaden $\mathbf G = \mathbf Z\mathbf Z'/(2\sum_k p_k(1-p_k))$ with $p_k$ in the hybrid population (@eq-vanraden-g, 01:71-80); `vanraden_G()` takes the 0/1/2 matrix $\mathbf M^H$ and returns a list `G, Z, p`, while `molecular_coancestry()` takes 0/0.5/1 $\mathbf X$ (01:81-90).
  - $\mathbf 1'\mathbf G\mathbf 1 = 0$ (@eq-g-sum-zero, 01:92-102), so $\theta_S = (2n^2)^{-1}\mathbf 1'\mathbf G_{SS}\mathbf 1 = 0$ and $N_s = 1/(2\theta_S)$ diverges on $\mathbf G$: `sum_G` $-4.179 \times 10^{-11}$, `Ns_on_G` $-9.349 \times 10^{15}$ (round-off reciprocal, no information), $\min \mathbf G = -0.1389$ (@tbl-g-sums, 01:103-134). $\mathbf G\mathbf 1 = \mathbf 0$, so $\mathbf G$ is singular (01:412-416).
  - On $\mathbf f$ (biallelic markers) $\theta_S \in [1/2, 1]$ and $N_s \in [1/2, 1]$ for every set: whole population 0.7442, 1000 random subsets 0.6943-0.7445 (@tbl-ns-on-f, 01:111-114, 01:137-150); a monotone rescaling, not a head count (@sec-ns).
  - Subset sum under random sampling without replacement: $\mathrm E[\mathbf 1_S'\mathbf G\mathbf 1_S] = n\frac{N-n}{N-1}\overline G_{ii}$, not a function of $n/N$ alone, zero at $n = N$; Monte Carlo 75.7129 against formula 75.5192, s.e. 0.1065 (n = 100, 20000 draws, @tbl-subset-sum-mc), and exactly $n\overline G_{ii} + n(n-1)(\sum G - \operatorname{tr}G)/(N(N-1))$ (01:152-195). A subset sum is a well-defined distance from the population centroid, not a coancestry of the subset (01:165-168).
  - The failure of $\mathbf G$ is that its centring constant $2\mathbf p$ is estimated from the set being measured, so the reference moves with the set (callout, 01:198-208).
  - Molecular coancestry $f_{ij} = m^{-1}\sum_k [x_{ik}x_{jk} + (1-x_{ik})(1-x_{jk})]$, identity in state, $f_{ij} \in [0, 1]$ (@eq-f-molecular, 01:210-234); matrix form $\mathbf f = m^{-1}(2\mathbf X\mathbf X' - \mathbf X\mathbf 1\mathbf 1' - \mathbf 1\mathbf 1'\mathbf X' + m\mathbf J)$, equal to `tcrossprod(cbind(X, 1 - X))/m`, a Gram matrix (@eq-f-matrix, 01:258-284); book data: min 0.6448, max 0.8318, diagonal mean 0.8192, off-diagonal mean 0.6717 (@tbl-f-summary).
  - $s_i = f_{ii} = (1+F_i)/2$ with $F_i$ numerically the fraction of markers at which $i$ is homozygous, an individual quantity and not $F_{hom}$ (01:229-234, 01:347-359); the minimum of the diagonal is 0.5, reached by a hybrid heterozygous at every marker (01:338-344).
  - What the scale of $\mathbf f$ is: IBS, not an unbiased IBD estimate; under the assumptions of @eq-hp-ibs-ibd, $\mathbb E[f_{ij}] = A_{ij}/2 + (1 - A_{ij}/2)\times$ base-population floor, with floor $m^{-1}\sum_k(p_{b,k}^2 + q_{b,k}^2) \ge 1/2$; $\mathbf f$ is read as a comparison between sets on the same markers, not an absolute coancestry; no constant is estimated from the set measured (01:235-256).
  - Two floors, two names: the base-population floor (base frequencies $p_{b,k}$, 01:248-250, 01:443-446) and the panel floor $m^{-1}\sum_k(p_k^2 + q_k^2)$ with $p_k$ the frequency in the $N$ hybrids, an exact identity of the set: $\operatorname{mean}(\mathbf f) =$ panel floor (asserted at $10^{-12}$), off-diagonal mean from it and the diagonal; panel floor 0.6719, off-diagonal 0.6717; same hybrids on MAF $> 0.30$ markers 0.5271, on MAF $< 0.15$ markers 0.8710 (@tbl-panel-floor, 01:305-336).
  - Heterozygous fraction: $2(1 - \bar s) = 1 - \bar F = 1 - \bar f^L_{AB} = 0.3617$ on the complete A x B factorial of inbred lines, an identity of the realised factorial through $F_{(ab)} = f^L_{ab}$; independent of $F_{BN}$ under neutral Balding-Nichols divergence because the F1 is heterozygous with expected probability $2p^{*}_k(1-p^{*}_k)$ ($p^{*}_k$ the ancestral frequency of the draw, @eq-balding-nichols in 03, not the frozen base $p_{0,k}$); nothing claimed for non-neutral divergence (@tbl-diagonal, 01:360-380). Agrees with 00's $H_o = GD_T + GD_{BS} = 0.3617$ (00:435-447).
  - Published treatments, reported with what each says: Nejati-Javaremi allelic similarity (as reproduced by Gomez-Romano 2016, p. 3) is the bracket of @eq-f-molecular at a biallelic locus; Gomez-Romano 2016 on the range of cross-product relationships, the minor-allele penalty, non-invertibility of centred matrices, the "natural" interpretation of allelic similarity, and the choice depending on the objective; the guarantee $\mathbf f \in [0,1]$ is the book's own inference (01:382-417).
  - $\mathbf G_{0.5} = (\mathbf M^H - \mathbf 1\mathbf 1')(\mathbf M^H - \mathbf 1\mathbf 1')'/(m/2) = 4\mathbf f - 2\mathbf J$ (@eq-g05, 01:418-451), a derivation of this chapter (the paper gives no formula); entries in $[-2, 2]$, range 0.579-1.327 on the book data (@tbl-g05-range); for $\mathbf 1'\mathbf c = 1$, $\mathbf c'\mathbf G_{0.5}\mathbf c = 4\mathbf c'\mathbf f\mathbf c - 2$, so the two order selections identically; the removed 1/2 is the base-population floor at $p_{b,k} = 0.5$, a convention fixed in advance and not the panel floor (01:443-466). The two routes agree up to the affine map; this is consistency, not a proof that $\mathbf f$ is the right matrix to constrain (01:467-473).
  - A limit of constraining $\mathbf f$: with $\mathbf G_{0.5}$ in optimal contributions there is pressure to move allele frequencies toward 0.5 (Meuwissen et al. 2020, abstract and Methods; covariance with the base frequency negative); a constraint on $\theta_S$ pins the homozygosity lens only, not $F_{drift}$; the consequence for this book's selections is measured where those metrics are reported, not in 01 (01:475-491).
  - Scope of the package split: the coancestry-based metrics (`theta_group()` and those built on it, `gene_diversity()`, `status_number()`) take `ctx[["f"]]`; the frequency-based ones (`theta_from_freq()`, `he_nei()`, `He`, `alleles_lost`, `F_hom`, `F_drift` of `freq_metrics()`) read `colMeans(ctx[["X"]][idx, ])` and the frozen `ctx[["p0"]]` and cannot be reached by an f/G switch; `test-metrics.R` covers `theta_group()` only, not metrics reading `ctx[["f"]]` directly (@tbl-matrix-roles, 01:493-531). `R/00_data.R` roxygen and the `R/01_metrics.R` header comment say the same.
- Assumes: A2 (inbred, homozygous lines) and biallelic markers throughout; no epistasis for the heterosis sentence (01:41-46); the complete A x B factorial for the heterozygous-fraction identity; neutral divergence from a common ancestor (Balding-Nichols) for its independence from $F_{BN}$; neutral, unlinked loci, no mutation and a base population with frequencies $p_{b,k}$ for $\mathbb E[f_{ij}]$ (@eq-hp-ibs-ibd, from 00); sampling without replacement for the subset-sum expectation; the teaching scale (A8) and the seeds 1, 2, 3, 11 for every printed number. The published quotations are reports of what Gomez-Romano 2016, Meuwissen et al. 2020 and Caballero & Toro 2002 (p. 292) state, checked against the local PDFs; the rest of the sources are not reread (below).
- Takes from: `00`: @sec-hp-popgen, @sec-hp-ibd (IBD/IBS, coancestry, $s_i = (1+F_i)/2$, $GD_S = 1 - \theta_S$, @eq-hp-ibs-ibd and its floor, the scope sentence that frequency-based metrics are not computed on $\mathbf f$, 00:463-465), @sec-hp-inbreeding ($F_i$ against $F_{hom}$, $F_{(ab)} = f^L_{ab}$), @sec-hp-cycle (the cycle producing the F1), A2 of @sec-hybrid-programme, the 0.3617 of 00:435-447, and the pointers 00 made to @eq-x-freq, @eq-hybrid-dosage, @eq-f-molecular (all resolved). Earlier chapter changed by this review: 00:524 now cites @sec-f-molecular as well as @sec-why-not-g for why $\mathbf f$ is not centred. Forward, declared and not needed to read 01: `05b` (@sec-heterosis, @eq-het-value, @sec-heterosis-flat), `03` (@sec-simulating-crosses), `06` (@sec-ns), `05` (@sec-genomic-era), `02` (@sec-subdivided).
- Owed:

  - [settled in 02] -> `02`: `02:536` writes bare $\theta$ for the group coancestry of @sec-coancestry; write $\theta_S$ (01-C9). -- settled in 02: $\theta_S$ in the $N_s$ paragraph and in @tbl-keystone (02:827-833, 02:1023-1040).

The Terms entry of 90 stays owed (03 entry).

## 02-subdivided -- Diversity in a subdivided population
- Question: how the gene diversity of a metapopulation splits into within-individual, between-individual and between-subpopulation terms (Caballero & Toro 2002), what the F statistics, the per-subpopulation metrics and the effective-size result say about it, and which chapter of this book manages each term. It follows 01 because 01 defines the molecular coancestry $\mathbf f$ between single individuals; 02 averages the same quantity over the individuals of subpopulations, states the partition in those terms, and then makes the step to hybrids partitioned over parent lines (02:5-24, @sec-keystone).
- Establishes:
  - Notation (02:32-68): $n$, $N_i$, $N_T$ count subpopulations and individuals here, not $n_{sel}$, $N$ of the index; tilde = weighted mean within subpopulations ($\tilde f$, $\tilde F$, $\tilde s$, $\tilde p_{k,i}$), bar = mean over all pairs ($\bar f$, $\bar D = \tilde f - \bar f$), $\bar f \le \tilde f \le \tilde s$; sums and means per locus then averaged, ratios ($\alpha_i$, $G_i$, $G$, $F_{IS}$, $F_{ST}$, $F_{IT}$) formed from locus-averaged numerators and denominators (ratio of means); subscripts $i$, $ij$ are subpopulation quantities and $N_i = 1$ gives the individual quantities of 00 and 01; $k$ is an allele of one locus in the formulas and a biallelic locus in the first bullet of @sec-ct-oc (stated at both places).
  - $f_{ij}$ between subpopulations is identity in state; it equals pedigree coancestry only for a base with all alleles different (Caballero & Toro 2002, p. 292), which a biallelic panel cannot meet; unrelated lines sit at the base-population floor $m^{-1}\sum_k(p_{b,k}^2 + q_{b,k}^2) \ge 1/2$ and the observed level is the panel floor, so no threshold of $f_{ij}$ marks redundancy; ranges on the teaching scale, one draw: between-pool lines 0.6075-0.6635, within-pool 0.668-0.719 (02:84-127).
  - $s_i$, $F_i = 2s_i - 1$, $2(1 - s_i) = 1 - F_i = H_{o,i}$ (@eq-self-coancestry, 02:140-155); $F_i$ is molecular, measured against no base, not $F_{IS}$, and not comparable across marker panels (callout, 02:157-170).
  - $\alpha_i = (F_i - f_{ii})/(1 - f_{ii})$ and $G_i = (1 + \alpha_i)/2$ (@eq-alpha-g, 02:174-186), the subpopulation analogue of $F_{IS}$, not the diversity-loss constraint $\alpha$ and not a genomic matrix $\mathbf G$. Baseline under random mating: ratio of locus-averaged expectations $-1/(2N_i - 1)$, $G_i \to (N_i - 1)/(2N_i - 1) < 1/2$, $\alpha_i = -1/7$ ($N = 4$), $-1/23$ ($N = 12$) (@eq-alpha-baseline, 02:187-212), exact for the ratio of expectations and first order for the expectation of the ratio; checked by exact enumeration (deviation $4 \times 10^{-15}$) and by simulation (`alpha-baseline-check`, 02:213-270); @tbl-mating-meter (02:272-295) is read against the baseline, not against 0.
  - Nei distance $D_{ij}$ (@eq-nei-distance, 02:296-309); no double sum over individuals needed (02:310-329); the decomposition of $\bar f$ (@eq-fbar-decomp, 02:330-347).
  - F statistics (@eq-f-stats, 02:348-362): $F_{IS}$, $F_{ST} = (\tilde f - \bar f)/(1 - \bar f) = \bar D/(1 - \bar f)$, $F_{IT}$, $(1 - F_{IT}) = (1 - F_{IS})(1 - F_{ST})$; attribution reported as Caballero & Toro 2002 Eq. 6 (Wright 1969) and Toro & Caballero 2005 §3a (Cockerham 1969), originals not read. Heterozygosity form (@eq-f-stats-het, 02:363-372): $F_{ST} = (H_T - H_S)/H_T$, $F_{IS} = 1 - H_o/H_S$, $F_{IT} = 1 - H_o/H_T$, plug-in $H$, locus-averaged numerator and denominator, asserted at $10^{-12}$.
  - Two statistics are written $F_{ST}$ (02:404-427): the book's $F_{ST} = GD_{BS}/GD_T$ of @eq-f-stats, and the one referenced to between-population coancestry, $(\theta_w - \theta_{AB})/(1 - \theta_{AB})$. At one biallelic locus, equal weights, $\bar p = (p_A + p_B)/2$, $\Delta p = p_A - p_B$: $\Delta p^2/(4\bar p\bar q)$ against $\Delta p^2/(2\bar p\bar q + \Delta p^2/2)$, ratio $4\bar p\bar q/(2\bar p\bar q + \Delta p^2/2) \in [1, 2]$, $1/6$ and $2/7$ at $(0.2, 0.6)$; on the 30 random hybrids (seed 7) 0.126 against 0.224, ratio 1.78. 02 cites no external source for the second usage, does not use it, and always means @eq-f-stats. The symbol $G_{ST}$ does not occur in 02.
  - Partition (@eq-gd-partition, 02:430-436, @tbl-gd-partition): $GD_T = GD_{WI} + GD_{BI} + GD_{BS}$, $GD_{WS} = GD_{WI} + GD_{BI}$; identities $GD_{WI}/GD_T = (1-G)(1-F_{ST})$, $GD_{BI}/GD_T = G(1-F_{ST})$, $GD_{WS}/GD_T = 1-F_{ST}$, $GD_{BS}/GD_T = F_{ST}$ (@eq-ratio-identities, 02:455-472). $G$ is the mean of the $G_i$ weighted by $N_i(1-f_{ii})$, not the census mean; the two differ when both $G_i$ and $f_{ii}$ differ (toy draw, seed 1, @tbl-toy-partition: $G$ 0.6441, census mean 0.6490, $GD_T$ 0.4304 = 0.1097 + 0.1986 + 0.1221). Ratio of locus means 0.2836 against mean of per-locus ratios 0.2715 on the toy.
  - Reading by reproductive system (02:583-616), the book's own and not Caballero & Toro's: outcrossers (large $GD_{WI}$), selfers and DH collections ($GD_{WI} \approx 0$), clonal crops ($GD_{WI}$ frozen). Drift in two closed pools of equal weight: $f_{AB}$ unchanged in expectation, $\tilde f$ up by $\Delta$, $\bar f$ up by $\Delta/2$, so $GD_{WS}$ falls $\Delta$, $GD_{BS}$ rises $\Delta/2$, $GD_T$ falls $\Delta/2$; $f_{AA}, f_{BB}, f_{AB}$ are $\theta_A, \theta_B, \theta_{AB}$ of `theta_pools()` (R/01_metrics.R:120-127), computed in 02's chunk `fst-two-forms` (02:375-390) and listed in the Symbols table of 90 (@sec-notation, 90:205, 226); 00 does not define them (it names $\theta_w$ and $\theta_{AB}$ once, 00:580-581) (02:596-616). Neutral drift and closed pools only; not the regime of @eq-ne-meta.
  - Worked example of Caballero & Toro 2002 (the authors' imaginary example, four subpopulations $N$ = 12, 4, 8, 6, multiallelic and so outside the book's biallelic setting; matrix typed from Table 2a) (02:617-691): $\bar f$ 0.3256, $\tilde f$ 0.4535, $GD_T$ 0.6744, $F_{ST}$ 0.1897 reproduced from $\mathbf f$ alone to half a unit of the 4th decimal; the $\tilde s$-dependent values agree by construction; $GD_{BS}$ is under a fifth of $GD_T$ (@tbl-ct-example-global). Provenance: the 2002 paper read in full, the 2005 review in excerpts, the other sources not read (02:1012-1021).
  - Removal (02:692-757, @tbl-ct-loss): removing subpopulation 2 raises $GD_T$ by 1.84 % (census weights) and 6.47 % (equal weights); the others lower it (census -6.65, -3.91, -0.81; equal -6.92, -6.94, -3.56). The arithmetic is $D_{2j} \ni f_{22}/2$ with $f_{22} = 0.875$; no cause of the near-fixation is claimed. Pig breeds (Laval et al. 2000, reanalysed in Table 5 col. 3): FRBA +0.67 % without effective-size weighting, -0.0 % in the weighted column 6; the paper's Weitzman losses use Reynolds' distance (-65.5 %, -63.3 %), the ranking by mean Nei distance is the book's. Stated open: the equal-weights column gives -6.92/-6.94 where the paper's col. 4 prints -6.8/-7.0, cause not established.
  - Optimal contributions of the example: $\mathbf c = (0.522, 0, 0.270, 0.208)$, the published value, asserted; $GD_T$ rises from $1 - \bar f$ to `GD_max` (02:767-826). In OCS selection $\mathbf c$, $\mathbf f$ change object ($N$ candidates, $N \times N$); this chapter is the case $\lambda \to \infty$. The genic-variance argument for a mixture base is developed here (02:781-799) but belongs to 02b (@eq-response-genic): among A x B hybrids genic variance holds only the within-pool part of gene diversity; neither $GD_T$ nor genic variance sees linkage disequilibrium.
  - $N_s = 1/(2\bar f)$; with census weights $\bar f = \theta_S$ of the whole set, so $GD_T = 1 - \theta_S$ (asserted, `tbl-keystone-check`); $N_s \in [1/2, 1]$ is a biallelic property, $N_s = 1.54$ for the 30 individuals of the multiallelic example, and never a head count; $N_s$ is static, $N_e$ is defined by a rate (02:827-846).
  - @eq-ne-meta ($N_e \approx 2Nn/[(V_W + V_B + 1)(1 - F_{ST})]$, 02:848): the case $F_{IS} = 0$ of Eq. 14 of Caballero & Toro 2002 (which has $1 - F_{IT}$); constant $N$, any constant migration, discrete generations, asymptote; $F_{ST}$ is the metapopulation's own and tends to 1 for closed pools; not for inbred or DH pools. Equalising contributions within subpopulations doubles $N_e$ at the same $F_{ST}$; equalising seeds only gives 4/3. Checked by literal gene dropping (`ne-gene-dropping`, $N$ = 100, 500 loci, 25 generations, 30 pedigrees): $N_e$ 98.2 ($\pm$ 1.6), 133.9 (1.7), 199.0 (2.5) against 100, 133.3, 200 (02:865-955), the single-population case only. $N_e = 4N/(2 + V_k)$ is reported from Falconer & Mackay with $V_W = V_k/2$.
  - Caveats (02:970-997): within-population criteria favour the largest populations; neutral markers do not measure adaptive value; allelic richness is more sensitive to bottlenecks; "use $\bar f$ for proportions and regeneration effort, never alone to decide discard". Correction published by the authors (Toro & Caballero 2005 Table 4 note a, DESH) (02:998-1010).
  - Keystone (@sec-keystone, @tbl-keystone, 02:1023-1110) says where each term is managed. Individuals are the parent-line slots of the hybrids, subpopulations the two pools, usage weights equal across pools; within a pool census and usage weights differ (30 line slots, unequal usage asserted). $GD_{WI}$ is exactly zero with inbred parents and $GD_{WI\text{-}hyb}$ carries the signal. $GD_{BI}$ is eroded by drift; within-pool diversity also falls in the simulated cycles of 11b, drift and selection not separated. $GD_{BS}$: under flat dominance ($d_k \equiv 1$), inbred parents, the complete A x B factorial and equal pool weights, expected F1 heterozygosity per locus is $GD_T + GD_{BS}$ (@eq-het-split, @tbl-het-partition-identity); neutral redraws from one ancestral frequency vector leave that sum unchanged, so every unit of $GD_{BS}$ gained is paid unit for unit by $GD_T$, while $GD_{WS}$ falls by twice what $GD_{BS}$ gains; false for non-flat dominance or divergence aligned with dominance (05b). Thirty random hybrids, seed 7, @tbl-keystone-check: $GD_{WI\text{-}hyb}$ 0.1806, $GD_T$ 0.3215 = $GD_{BI}$ 0.2809 + $GD_{BS}$ 0.0406, $GD_{WI}$ 0, $F_{ST}$ 0.1263.
- Assumes: biallelic markers and plug-in $H$ throughout, except the worked example (multiallelic, published matrix); locus-averaged numerators and denominators for every ratio; Hardy-Weinberg draws at independent loci for @eq-alpha-baseline; random mating ($F_{IS} = 0$), constant size, constant migration and the asymptote for @eq-ne-meta; neutral drift in two closed pools of equal weight for the drift paragraph; flat dominance, inbred parents, the complete A x B factorial, equal pool weights and neutral Balding-Nichols redraws from one ancestral frequency vector for the $GD_T$/$GD_{BS}$ trade-off; the teaching scale (A8) with seeds 1 (toy), 5 (baseline simulation), 7 (30 hybrids), 11, 101-103. Caballero & Toro 2002 read in full, Toro & Caballero 2005 in excerpts; Wright (1969), Cockerham (1967, 1969), Nei (1973), Lindgren et al. (1997), Falconer & Mackay (1996) and Meuwissen et al. (1997) not read, reported only as those two papers attribute them.
- Takes from: `00`: @sec-hp-not-animal (the second departure), @sec-hybrid-programme and @eq-hp-gd ($GD_S = 1 - \theta_S$, the partition for a set of hybrids), @sec-hp-popgen (DH sampling unit), @sec-hp-inbreeding (the book computes two statistics under the name $F_{ST}$ and they differ; $F_{(ab)} = f^L_{ab}$), @tbl-hp-stages (S1), A2. `01`: @sec-coancestry (individual $\mathbf f$, $\theta_S$), @sec-f-molecular and @tbl-panel-floor (base-population floor against panel floor; $\mathbf f$ read as a comparison on the same markers), $N_s \in [1/2, 1]$. Forward, declared and not needed to read 02: `06` (@eq-catalogue-alpha, @sec-catalogue, @sec-identity), `09` (@sec-ocs), `02b` (@sec-response, @eq-response-genic), `05b` (@eq-het-split, @tbl-het-partition-identity, @sec-heterosis-flat, @sec-heterosis-alignment), `08` (@sec-stages), `11b` (@sec-cycles), `11c` (@sec-limits), `07` (@sec-choosing). No earlier chapter changed by this review.
- Owed:
  - [settled in 08] -> `08`: Trap 2 (`08:86-106`) names $GD_{BS}/GD_T$ with a historical label and $G_{ST}$; adopt 02's name for the book's $F_{ST}$ and for the second statistic, or state the rename; attach no unverified label (02-C2, with the 00 item above). 02 is true without it. -- settled in 08: see the 00 item above (Trap 2, 08:147-205); the book's $F_{ST}$ keeps the name, the second statistic is $F^{\text{btw}}_{ST}$ with the ratio $2 - F^{\text{btw}}_{ST}$ asserted (08:182-193).
  - [settled in 05] -> `05`: `05:360-364` attributes the standardised-variance form of $F_{ST}$ to "the form @wright1951 introduced" and calls the book's $F_{ST}$ column the Nei $G_{ST}$ of `gd_partition()`, the opposite naming to 02 and 00; reconcile with 02:348-427 and withdraw the unverified attribution. The `wright1951` bib entry has a 1949 DOI against the year 1951 (00-B13); 02 no longer cites it. (This item was not written as a row in it1-response; recorded from it2-verdict.) -- settled in 05: the attribution to Wright is dropped, not softened ("The priority of that form is not claimed here, and no paper of Wright's is cited for it", 05:459-460), and the chapter's $F_{ST}$ is $GD_{BS}/GD_T$ = Nei's $G_{ST}$ = the standardised-variance form, asserted in-chunk (`gd_partition()` `F_ST` == `s1$Gst_Nei` == marker-mean ratio, 0.1024 at teaching scale), as in 02:348-427 (05:1772-1787, @tbl-era-s1-identity); `Fst_Wright` is "the code's name", 1.81 times larger, for which 02 records that no external source is cited; $F_{BN}$ is the third object (05:1782-1787). The unused `wright1951` bib entry is listed in the 05 entry, Owed.
  - -> `90`: `90:179` repeats "against a base with all alleles distinct", which 02:94-103 and 01 withdrew (02-C1).
  - -> `90`: `90:118` "the term recurrent selection erodes (@tbl-keystone)"; the table now says drift, with selection not separated (02-C9).
  - -> `90`: bare $\theta$ for the group coancestry at `90:569` and `90:627`; write $\theta_S$ (02-C4).
  - -> `90`: rows for $F_{IS}$, $F_{IT}$, $\bar D$ and for the second statistic written $F_{ST}$, with the one-sentence distinction from the book's (02-C2, 02-C14; the $F_{ST}$ row is also owed from 00 and 05); rows or a local declaration for $\tilde p_{k,i}$, $c_i$ and $\theta_w$ (02-E3). 02 defines each in its Notation and is true without them.

  - [settled in 08] -> `00`: re-read `00:575-581`, which describes 08's $F_{ST}$/$G_{ST}$ labels as 08 stands, once 08 changes (02-C2). 02 cites that passage only for the fact that two statistics exist and differ, which 00 states today. -- settled in 08: 00:576-582 reworded in the 08 review and read against 08 at its iteration 3 (the book's $F_{ST} = GD_{BS}/GD_T$, $F^{\text{btw}}_{ST}$ separate).

Keep `02b:229-234` consistent with 02:798-799 and 02:1100-1109. -- settled in 02b: 02b carries both cases after @eq-response-hybrid-var (mixture base as the $p_{Ak} = p_{Bk}$ limit, $m\beta^2 GD_T/4$; hybrids, $m\beta^2 GD_{WS}/4$, equal only when $GD_{BS} = 0$), with conditions, asserted (02b:474-489, chunk `response-hybrid-var`, deviations at most 1e-14); 02 keeps its three sentences and points to 02b (02:805, 02:1102), consistent with 02b:474-489.

Unverified, non-decisive: Falconer & Mackay "ch. 4" in the comment of `ne-equal-seed` (`book/02-subdivided.qmd:873`, and so in `book/downloads/02-subdivided.R`; the reviewer recalls $N_e = (4N - 2)/(V_k + 2)$ with $4N/(V_k + 2)$ as the large-$N$ form, a 0.5 % difference at $N = 100$, below the oracle's resolution) (02-M10); the year 1969 of Wright vol. 2 rests on Caballero & Toro's reference list; Toro & Caballero 2005 locators (§2, §3a, §3d, §3e, §5a, Table 4 note a) rest on PMC fragments; Wright, Cockerham, Nei 1973, Lindgren 1997 and Meuwissen 1997 not read. Note on `book/references.bib:1965` (02-M12): the reference list of Caballero & Toro 2002 prints the title of Reynolds, Weir & Cockerham (1983) for the Laval et al. (2000) entry; the book's entry follows Crossref ("Genetic diversity of eleven European pig breeds", pages 187-203, confirmed by Europe PMC), so a reader comparing the lists sees a different title; no claim depends on it (carry as a refcheck note at close). Quarto cross-reference resolution, the rendered tables and `docs/`, `_freeze/02-subdivided` wait for the batch render; `alpha-baseline-check`, `ne-equal-seed` and `ne-gene-dropping` are chunk labels and cannot be cross-referenced.

## 02b-response -- Response to selection: the other half of the objective
- Question: what selection buys, as against what it spends: the breeder's equation $R = i\,r\,\sigma_A$ on the hybrids of this book's generator, the split of additive variance into genic variance and a covariance term and what gene diversity can and cannot say about it, combining ability against a tester pool, what the default `weights = NULL` assumes, and the restricted selection index set beside the $\alpha$ constraint (as a comparison, not a proof). It follows 02 because 02 names $GD_{BI}$ as the raw material of the next cycle's response without saying how response depends on it, and defers the genic-variance argument for a mixture base; 02b carries both (02b:25-46). It predicts no crossover cycle.
- Establishes:
  - Data of the chapter: `simulate_pools()` draw (seed 1, 25 + 25 lines, 2000 markers, 300 QTL with N(0,1) effects, $h^2 = 0.5$, `fst` 0.15), 625 hybrids of the complete factorial, `X` in {0, 0.5, 1}, purely additive ($d_k \equiv 0$) (02b:48-82, 02b:94-99). Configuration read from `cfg`/`book_cfg`, identities asserted to a relative deviation of 1e-12 with the achieved deviation printed (02b:3-23).
  - Object of "response": the genetic selection differential on the hybrids that were selected (mean genetic value of the selected minus mean over candidates), not a transmitted change; hybrids are not recycled, lines are (A3, 00:26), so a cycle transmits a line's value against a tester pool, a different object. The comparison tests only whether @eq-response-breeders reproduces that differential on this generator (02b:105-114, 02b:288-296).
  - @eq-response-breeders $R = i\,r\,\sigma_A$ in accuracy form, $r$ the correlation of criterion and genetic value; Falconer & Mackay (1996) cited, not read, footnote says no locator is given (02b:86-99). One draw (@tbl-response-breeders, 02b:121-134): $i$ 1.774, $r$ 0.702, $\sigma_A$ 4.742, predicted 5.908, realised 5.116 (about 13 percent overprediction). Split (@tbl-response-gap, 02b:144-157): $i_{real}$ 1.701, response with realised intensity 5.662, response from regression 5.662, realised 5.116, index skewness -0.170. Two identities, asserted: (1) $b\,S_I = i_{real}\,r\,\sigma_A$ (deviation exactly 0); (2) realised minus regression response $= \bar e_{sel}$, the mean regression residual over the selected set (1.1e-16; value -0.546) (02b:159-184).
  - Replicated over seeds 1-30 (@tbl-response-replicated, 02b:196-296): mean relative gap 0.006, s.e. 0.016, within 2 s.e. of zero; mean realised intensity against the finite-$N$ normal expectation 1.771 (s.d. 0.044) and the infinite-population 1.774. Error rates of the render-time assertion $|\bar g| < 2\,\mathrm{s.e.}$ computed: false alarm $2\,\mathrm{pt}(-2, 29)$ = 5.5 percent (about 1 seed set in 18); the rule flags a true mean gap with power 0.8 at 0.046 (4.6 percent of the predicted response, this sample's per-seed s.d. 0.088); 2 s.e. of the observed mean gap = 0.032; the two are a power and a threshold, not two bounds (ratio 1.42 normal approximation, 1.43 non-central $t$) (02b:237-287). The result is the absence of a detectable gap above that size, not a proof of zero; teaching scale only, the draw of seed 1 is one of the 30, and the replicated sweep of @sec-alphasimr is a different object (progeny mean, $r$ against the breeding value because that trait has dominance) not reproduced here (02b:288-296).
  - Genic variance $V_{genic} = \sum_k \beta_k^2\operatorname{Var}(x_k)$ (@eq-response-genic, the chapter's own definition) and the covariance-term identity @eq-response-ld, $\operatorname{Var}(\mathbf X\boldsymbol\beta) - V_{genic} = 2\sum_{k<l}\beta_k\beta_l\operatorname{Cov}(x_k, x_l)$, an identity of the covariance matrix (02b:298-316). Bulmer (1971) reported for the mechanism by which selection makes the term negative, primary text not read (02b:318-321). Seed-1 draw (@tbl-response-genic, 02b:338-361): realised variance 22.4837, genic 25.2051, term -2.7214 (deviations 1.4e-14 and 4.2e-16 against the oracle `ref_genic_var()`).
  - The term of the draw is not a Bulmer effect: under (i) loci independent given pool frequencies in `simulate_pools()`, (ii) no selection, (iii) finite lines, the factorial identity $\operatorname{Cov}(x_k, x_l) = [\operatorname{Cov}_A + \operatorname{Cov}_B]/4$ (no between-pool term; asserted, 1.5e-15) makes the term the sampling covariance of 25 + 25 founder lines, expectation zero. Seeds 1-30: mean -0.138, s.e. 0.835, negative in 18; disjoint seeds 31-230: mean -0.212, s.e. 0.326, $z$ = -0.65 (asserted $|z| < 3$, false alarm 0.3 percent); share of genic variance mean -0.009, s.d. 0.205; the seed-1 share -0.108 is 0.53 s.d. from zero. Measuring a selection-induced term needs a random-selection control, which @sec-limits supplies (02b:318-336, 02b:363-407).
  - Per-locus variance among hybrids @eq-response-hybrid-var, $\operatorname{Var}(x_k) = (p_{Ak}q_{Ak} + p_{Bk}q_{Bk})/4 = \bar p_k\bar q_k/2 - (p_{Ak} - p_{Bk})^2/8$, population-variance scale (divisor $N$), against `genic_var()` and `var(gv)` on divisor $N - 1$: `genic_var` = $N/(N-1)\times$ the population-scale sum (ratio 1.0016, 5.6e-16) (02b:409-461). Gene diversity of the hybrid pool is $GD_T$ of @eq-hp-gd for the complete factorial, equal pool weights; $V_{genic}$ among hybrids sees only $GD_{WS}$, weighted by squared effect, so gene diversity is a proxy for genic variance only through $GD_{WS}$ and only if effects are unrelated to which loci lose diversity (not tested) (02b:462-472).
  - Two special cases, each under stated conditions: (a) mixture base $p_{Ak} = p_{Bk}$, $V_{genic} = \sum\beta_k^2\bar p_k\bar q_k/2$, equal effects $m\beta^2 GD_T/4$ (3.1e-15); (b) hybrids of two pools never intermated, equal effects $m\beta^2 GD_{WS}/4$ (2.5e-15), equal to (a) only when $GD_{BS} = 0$; $GD_T = GD_{WS} + GD_{BS}$ (5.6e-17). The chapter's trait satisfies neither equal-effects condition, so (a) and (b) are statements about the proxy, not about `gv`. Neither $GD_T$ nor $V_{genic}$ sees linkage disequilibrium (02b:474-489). This settles Owed 02-M9: 02b owns the hybrid derivation, 02 keeps three sentences and cites it.
  - Callout (02b:496-516): $\theta_S$, $GD_S$/$GD_T$, $N_s$, allelic richness, $F_{hom}$, $F_{drift}$ (including $\theta_S$, the only metric the DE constrains) are functions of allele frequencies one locus at a time and do not see the covariance term; `eff_dim()` is the exception, responds to disequilibrium and weights loci by nothing. Two separate reasons a flat gene diversity does not imply flat additive variance: $GD_T = GD_{WS} + GD_{BS}$ with only $GD_{WS}$ entering $V_{genic}$; and the invisible covariance term. @sec-limits tests the second reason only.
  - Combining ability against a tester pool (02b:518-626), the dominance assumption declared where each statement is made. Without dominance: the effect on the hybrid value of one allele substitution at locus $k$ is $\beta_k/2$ whatever the pool (one parental allele moves $x_k$ by 1/2), $\mathrm{gv}_{ij} = a_i + b_j$ with $a_i = \sum_k\beta_k u_{ik}/2$, GCAs are $a_i$, $b_j$ centred, SCA zero, $\operatorname{Var}(\mathrm{gv}) = \operatorname{Var}(\mathrm{GCA}_A) + \operatorname{Var}(\mathrm{GCA}_B)$ = 22.4837, so the whole $\sigma_A^2$ is GCA variance; GCA of pool-A lines against five testers equals that against all 25 (deviations 7.8e-16, 5.9e-16, 1.1e-14, 7.9e-16, 4.9e-16). With dominance: the dominance part of GCA is an average effect at the opposite pool's frequencies (@sec-hybrid-programme), so combining ability is a property of a line against a tester pool and an SCA component appears. Illustration on a dominance-only $H$ with $d_k \sim N(0.3, 0.1^2)$ (seed 7; not 05b's gamma draw, seed 4; 05b owns the decomposition): variances GCA_A 9.064, GCA_B 8.673, SCA 20.376, shares 0.238, 0.228, 0.535, shares of the illustrative heterosis and not of the genetic variance of `gv` (@tbl-response-gca-sca). How the shares respond to recurrent selection is not measured.
  - What `weights = NULL` assumes (02b:628-729): the criteria of `stage2_select()` are predicted values $\hat{\mathbf g}$, standardised by `build_ctx()`, so the default index is $\sum_j\hat g_j/\operatorname{sd}(\hat g_j)$, economic weights $a_j = 1/\operatorname{sd}(\hat g_j)$, an economic statement and not a neutral one. Weights maximising the correlation with $W = \mathbf a'\mathbf g$ are $\operatorname{Var}(\boldsymbol\xi)^{-1}\operatorname{Cov}(\boldsymbol\xi, \mathbf g')\mathbf a$; for multi-trait BLUP $\hat{\mathbf g}$, $\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{\mathbf g}, \mathbf g') = \operatorname{Var}(\hat{\mathbf g})$, so $\mathbf b = \mathbf a$ (property of BLUP, uncited, verified: 1.1e-16 to 2.2e-16); for phenotypic records $\mathbf y$ the same expression is Smith-Hazel, @eq-response-smith-hazel $\mathbf b = \boldsymbol\Sigma_P^{-1}\boldsymbol\Sigma_G\mathbf a$ (Smith 1936, Hazel 1943 cited, not read; matrix form modern). Example (@tbl-response-smith-hazel): weights 0.6172 (yield), -0.1406 (moisture) for $\mathbf a = (1, 1)$; $\mathbf b \propto \mathbf a$ iff $\mathbf a$ is an eigenvector of $\boldsymbol\Sigma_P^{-1}\boldsymbol\Sigma_G$, sufficient $\boldsymbol\Sigma_G = c\boldsymbol\Sigma_P$. The gap between $\mathbf b$ and $\mathbf a$ is a property of an index on phenotypes, not a defect of `weights = NULL`, whose criteria are predictions; the default assumes the economic weighting and that predictions behave as multi-trait BLUP, neither tested against a real objective.
  - Restricted index @eq-response-restricted $\mathbf b = \boldsymbol\Sigma_P^{-1}[\boldsymbol\Sigma_G\mathbf a - \mathbf C(\mathbf C'\boldsymbol\Sigma_P^{-1}\mathbf C)^{-1}\mathbf C'\boldsymbol\Sigma_P^{-1}\boldsymbol\Sigma_G\mathbf a]$, $\mathbf C = \boldsymbol\Sigma_G\mathbf R$: the book's own statement of the matrix form (`index_restricted()`), Kempthorne & Nordskog (1959) the reference the book cites, not read, content and notation not claimed; only $\mathbf C'\mathbf b = \mathbf 0$ is claimed (by substitution and the chunk); the derivation as the maximiser is not given (checked outside the chapter by reviewer only) (02b:742-760). @tbl-response-restricted (02b:781-808): Smith-Hazel weights 0.6172/-0.1406, covariance with moisture 0.143, expected gain in moisture per round 0.2195; restricted weights 0.6538/-0.6538, covariance 0.0000, gain 0.0000 (4.4e-17); Monte Carlo (4e5 draws, seed 21): Smith-Hazel 0.217 (s.e. 0.003), restricted -0.002 (s.e. 0.003), within the 4 s.e. asserted. Zero is an EXPECTED gain for known matrices and a linear regression of genetic value on the index; the realised change in a finite selected sample, or with estimated matrices, is not zero (02b:810-853).
  - Lineage (02b:733-779, 02b:880-891): constraining a group coancestry while maximising gain is in Meuwissen et al. (2020), read (OCS maximises $\bar g = \mathbf c'\hat{\mathbf g}$ with $K = \tfrac12\mathbf c'\mathbf G\mathbf c$ constrained; Table 1 compares molecular matrices, no formula for $\mathbf G_{0.5}$); Meuwissen (1997) reported through the 2020 Introduction, not read; the affine map from $\mathbf f$ to $\mathbf G_{0.5}$ is 01's @eq-g05 and the two order selections identically. So restricting a group coancestry while maximising gain is not new to the book and the restricted direction is not its contribution; what the book adds is stated in @sec-baseline and @sec-ocs and not checked against the wider literature. Four differences from the $\alpha$ constraint: (1) equality, linear in index weights, closed form, against an inequality $\alpha(S) \le \alpha_{max}$ on a quadratic form in a discrete selection, solved by search; (2) expectation against realisation ($\alpha(S)$ is deterministic given its reference); (3) the quantity restricted: $GD_T$ contains $GD_{BS}$, which does not enter $V_{genic}$ among hybrids, so the restricted direction is not the one that carries response (item 3 of the Preface); (4) equality against inequality is not worked out in 02b or in @sec-ocs. Neither lineage is a proof that the $\alpha$ constraint is the right one; whether to prefer a budget to a price remains the argument of @sec-ocs.
  - Where this connects (02b:893-913): 05 and 06 report the frequency-based metrics and compute no $V_{genic}$ (grep); 08b measures accuracy of a different object (02b:898-901); Finding 5 of 07 on assumed accuracy; 09 states the $\alpha$ constraint as an inequality and does not derive it from the restricted index; 11c finds a term significant against a random-selection control, largest early and decaying with recombination, and argues from the decay that constraining allele frequencies while ignoring the term is justified, ranking that cost below the permanent cost of changed frequencies on the condition that the decay is fast.
- Assumes: A3 of 00 (hybrids not recycled, lines are) and A2 (inbred lines); biallelic markers coded 0/0.5/1; no dominance for the breeder's-equation check, the genic variance and the first GCA statement ($d_k \equiv 0$), with dominance declared where used (the second GCA statement and the $H$ illustration); the complete A x B factorial, independent loci given pool frequencies, no selection before the evaluation and finite lines for the covariance term; equal pool weights and inbred parents for the link to $GD_T$; normal-theory intensity for $i$, known and unestimated covariance matrices and a linear regression of genetic value on the index for the restricted-index zero; the teaching scale (A8) with seeds 1 (draw), 1-30 (replicated), 31-230 (disjoint block), 7 (illustrative $d$), 11 (finite-$N$ intensity), 21 (gain oracle). Primary text read: Meuwissen et al. (2020) (Introduction, Methods "Optimum Contribution Selection", Table 1). Reported and not read: Kempthorne & Nordskog (1959), Meuwissen (1997), Smith (1936), Hazel (1943), Bulmer (1971), Sprague & Tatum (1942), Falconer & Mackay (1996, no locator). The Smith-Hazel formula and the BLUP property are checked by algebra and chunk, not against the papers.
- Takes from: `00`: A3 (00:26), @sec-hybrid-programme (GCA does not depend on the tester set without dominance; the dominance part of GCA at the opposite pool's frequencies, 00:122-130), @eq-hp-gca-sca, @eq-hp-gd ($GD_S = 1 - \theta_S$). `01`: @sec-coancestry, @eq-g05 ($\mathbf G_{0.5} = 4\mathbf f - 2\mathbf J$, so the group coancestries order selections identically), $\theta_S$. `02`: @sec-subdivided, @tbl-keystone (the $GD_T = GD_{WS} + GD_{BS}$ split; $GD_{BI}$ as the raw material of the next cycle; the genic-variance argument for a mixture base deferred to 02b, now settled). ## 05b-heterosis-dominance -- Heterosis, dominance, and what divergence buys
- Question: whether between-pool divergence is "the heterosis engine" that the book had asserted since 02, and what follows once the claim is derived: the expected heterosis of a pool pair, its exact split into a shared term ($GD_T$) and a divergence term ($GD_{BS}$) under stated conditions, why neutral divergence relocates expected heterosis from the one to the other without adding any, what does buy heterosis (divergence aligned with dominance), the combining-ability decomposition of heterosis (GCA/SCA) and how neutral divergence reshapes it, and eight steps to run this on a programme's data. It follows 02b because 00, 01, 02 and 02b each state a piece (the numerical identity, $d_k$ and $H_i$, the partition and the trade-off, the two GCA statements) and hand the derivation or the decomposition to it; it opens by listing those hand-overs (05b:26-41) and claims two halves, *maintain* ($GD_{BS}$ lost at fixed $GD_T$ is heterosis lost, one for one) and *do not maximise* (neutral divergence adds none) (05b:43-54).
- Establishes:
  - Premises (05b:73-113, @sec-heterosis-premises): inbred parents; dominance within loci and additivity between (no epistasis, which $d_k$ cannot absorb); the marker panel is the set of dominance loci. The chapter's $d_k$ is one gamma(2, 4) draw (seed 4), positive at every locus, an illustrative assumption, not varied: every number that depends on $d$ (correlation 0.78, alignment factor 1.55, SCA shares) is scoped to it.
  - $d_k$ is named the **dominance effect**, the functional departure of the heterozygote from the homozygote midpoint, a property of the locus; "dominance deviation" is reserved for Fisher's statistical object (05b:145-166, @sec-heterosis-dk-meaning). The convention is declared for 05b, 00, 01, 02, 02b and says that other chapters (05 among them) still use the older name (05b:152-155); it is true of those five chapters after this review. $a$ is a scale, never computed (05b:131-143). $G(\mathbf x)$ and $H_i = \sum_k d_k\mathbb 1\{x_{ik} = 0.5\}$ (@eq-het-genotypic-value, @eq-het-value, 05b:180-206); `heterosis_value()` takes no additive effect; oracle `ref_heterosis()` on 25 random hybrids, gap 0 (@tbl-het-oracle-gap).
  - $\mathbb E[H]$ (@eq-het-expected, 05b:239-265) and the split $p_A + p_B - 2p_Ap_B = 2\bar p(1-\bar p) + y^2/2$ (@eq-het-split, 05b:267-282). Under flat dominance ($d_k \equiv 1$), inbred parents and the complete A x B factorial the two terms are exactly $GD_T$ and $GD_{BS}$ of `gd_partition()` (asserted, $10^{-12}$ relative; 0.3281 and 0.0336, sum 0.3617 as in 00:435-447 and 01:360-380); for varying $d$, $GD_{BS}$ is the unweighted version and the identity holds for the $d$-weighted term only (05b:297-306). $GD_T = GD_{BI} + GD_{BS}$, so expected F1 heterozygosity is $GD_{BI} + 2GD_{BS}$; the random-mating textbook heterosis $\sum_k d_ky_k^2$ (Falconer & Mackay, locator UNVERIFIED) is twice the divergence term, checked (@tbl-het-falconer-ch14, 05b:308-345). $GD_T + GD_{BS} = 1 - \theta_{AB}$ (@eq-het-theta-ab, 05b:349-380; $\theta_{AB}$ identified with $f_{AB}$ of 02 and $\bar f^L_{AB}$ of 01) and $2\,GD_{WI\text{-}hyb} = GD_T + GD_{BS}$ with $GD_{WI} = 0$ (@eq-het-wi-hyb, 05b:382-418), asserted.
  - Inbreeding depression $ID = 2F\sum_k d_kp_kq_k$ (@eq-het-id, 05b:420-455): at $F = 1$ it is the shared term at $p_A = p_B$ (asserted); closed form 27.5856 against the mean of 8 simulated runs 27.5746 at $F = 0.6$ (300 loci, one pool), $z = -1.2$. An inbred line's dominance value is exactly 0 (vacuous on simulated 0/1 lines, informative on real genotypes). Therefore $ID$ is not the current-cycle price of the $\alpha$ budget, which acts on $GD_T = GD_{BI} + GD_{BS}$ (not $GD_{BI}$ alone); whether the cost arrives later in the additive variance is the conditional question of @sec-response-genic, not tested here. For $0 < F < 1$ **this book does not model depression** (04's P9; only use at $F = 1$ in @sec-alphasimr; the $F = 0.6$ evaluation is a formula check) (05b:505-542).
  - Genetic distance predicts heterosis exactly only under flat $d$, inbred parents, complete factorial: $H_{ij} = d\,m(1 - f^L_{ij}) = d\,m\,\mathrm{MRD}^2_{ij}$ (@eq-het-distance, asserted); under the gamma $d$ the correlation falls to 0.78 (05b:544-600).
  - **$F_{BN}$ against $F_{ST}$** (05b:617-628): $F_{BN}$ is the Balding-Nichols parameter (`fst` in the code), used in every expectation and equation; $F_{ST}$ only for the statistic $GD_{BS}/GD_T$ of @eq-f-stats (Caballero & Toro 2002 Eq. 8), which estimates about $F_{BN}/(2 - F_{BN})$. "A function of the frequencies only" means the statistic. Agrees with 00:561-585 and 90:150-161.
  - **Neutral divergence adds no expected heterosis** (05b:602-787): $\mathbb E[2\bar p(1-\bar p)] = 2p^{*}(1-p^{*})(1 - F_{BN}/2)$ and $\mathbb E[y^2/2] = F_{BN}p^{*}(1-p^{*})$, equal and opposite, exactly in expectation (@eq-het-bn-total, @eq-het-bn-halves); $GD_T$ pays unit for unit and $GD_{WS}$ falls by **twice** what $GD_{BS}$ gains (derived from $2\bar p\bar q = p_Aq_A + p_Bq_B + y^2/2$, not measured on the sweep). The sweep, one draw per row, seed 5, teaching scale (@tbl-het-sweep, @fig-heterosis-sweep): six totals 0.3620-0.3664 (predicted level 0.365, mean 0.3646); divergence rises 0.0107 -> 0.0846 (gain 0.0739), shared falls 0.3549 -> 0.2792 (loss 0.0756), 2 % apart; sample-frequency offset $\propto (1 - F_{BN})$ explained and compared. The sweep is consistent with the expectation, it does not measure it.
  - **What buys heterosis is divergence aligned with dominance** (05b:789-900, @eq-het-alignment): divergence term $= \tfrac m2[\bar d\,\overline{y^2} + \mathrm{cov}(d, y^2)]$; same pools, same $F_{ST}$, same set of dominance effects, three placements: 0.79 / 1.00 / 1.23 of the independent total (factor 1.55, one pool pair, one $d$); stratified within groups of equal shared weight isolates the divergence channel. `mean_sq_delta_p` is twice the divergence term (@tbl-het-msdp, 05b:901-930).
  - **GCA/SCA of heterosis** (05b:932-1130): $H_{ij} = \mu + g^A_i + g^B_j + s_{ij}$ (@eq-het-gca-sca, @eq-het-gca-sca-terms), exact and orthogonal, reconstruction and `lm()` equivalence asserted (7.96e-13 and 6.97e-12 against a tolerance of 3.9e-7). **Declaration** (05b:961-973): these symbols denote the decomposition of $H_{ij}$, not of the cross mean $\bar y_{ij}$ of @eq-hp-gca-sca; the chapter's $g^A_i$ is the **dominance part** of 00's $g^A_i$, the additive part $\sum_k a_k\delta u_{ik}$ having cancelled; the qualifier "GCA for heterosis" for this term and "GCA" unqualified for 00's; $s_{ij}$ is the same object in both, $\mu$ and $g$ are not. GCA is an average effect at the **opposite** pool's frequencies ($p_B = 0.5$ contributes zero to $g^A$), so combining ability is a property of a line against a tester pool (@sec-heterosis-gca-average, 05b:1033-1086; agrees with 00:120-132 and 02b). $s_{ij} = -d\,m\,\mathrm{DC}(f^L)_{ij}$ under flat $d$ (@eq-het-sca-dc); variance components @eq-het-var-components (closed forms for independent-Bernoulli lines).
  - Tester-based mean of $H$ (the book's own derivation, 05b:1059-1086): over all $n_B$ testers the mean is exactly $\mu + g^A_i$; over a sample of $k$ it carries $\bar g^B_T$ (level, not ranking) and $\bar s_{i\cdot T}$ (in the ranking, mean zero in expectation); a testcross mean also carries the candidate's additive part; lines only, no mapping onto any published scheme. Comstock et al. (1949) is cited for existence and title only ("to make maximum use of both general and specific combining ability"); the chapter reports nothing else of the paper (05b:1025-1031, 05b:1062).
  - **Divergence destroys SCA faster than it builds GCA** (05b:1177-1366): $\sigma^2_{SCA,k}$ in expectation $\propto (1 - F_{BN})^2$; GCA a concave parabola in $F_{BN}$ with turning point $F^\star_k = (8p^{*}_k(1-p^{*}_k) - 1)/(8p^{*}_k(1-p^{*}_k))$ (@eq-het-var-bn, @eq-het-gca-peak, @eq-het-sca-gca-ratio). Sweep (seed 5 pools, seed 4 $d$, one draw per row): SCA variance 91.8 -> 27.8, GCA 60.0 -> 77.9 (+30 %, recovers 28 % of what SCA lost), total 151.9 -> 105.7 (-30 %), SCA share 0.605 -> 0.263; grid maximum over pool seeds 1-20 "0.20 in 1, 0.30 in 10, 0.45 in 9", expected peak on the $F_{BN}$ axis 0.36. Only the direction is seed-free; magnitudes are one realisation. The share result is Reif et al. (2007) in direction (formula-level definition not read), the quantity differs; Technow et al. (2014) is reported as refcheck read it and is "consistent with the mechanism", not a test. A smaller SCA share is not a better programme (05b:1368-1420).
  - Protocol, eight steps (05b:1437-2066): Step 1 non-inbred parents overstate the gain by a factor of about 5.3 on the chapter's configuration; Step 4 the identity holds for any weighting if both sides use the same one, and `gd_partition()` weights by usage while `pool_freqs()` does not; Step 6 correlation of a line's $k$-tester mean with $g^A_i$, $\rho_k$ (@eq-het-rho-k, $k$ = testers, not loci): 0.5516, 0.6930, 0.7699, 0.8534, 0.9368 for $k$ = 1, 2, 3, 5, 10 against 0.5516, 0.6907, 0.7672, 0.8509, 0.9354 (4000 tester sets per $k$; the simulated mean exceeds the closed form by 1.6-4.5 s.e. for $k \ge 2$, reasons stated as untested); layout rank rules.
  - **Step 7 (05b:1878-2044): no transferable threshold for the variance components.** The $\sqrt{2/n}$ band is one relative s.d. of the variance of independent observations, not a tolerance. Over 1000 recycled pairs (six founders, DH, free recombination, seeds 7001-8000) against 1000 independent Bernoulli sets (seeds 8001-9000), the ratio of s.d. of the per cent deviation is **1.15** (1.07-1.22) for GCA_A, **1.22** (1.14-1.31) for GCA_B and **1.65** (1.54-1.77) for SCA; recycled pairs fall outside the central 95 % range of the independent ones in 9, 13 and 23 % of replicates. **The mechanism is untested:** LD from descent, relatedness and loss of segregating loci are three candidates, not results; limits (six founders, one $d$, one pool pair, comparison against independent Bernoulli lines not a pedigree, physical linkage unmeasured) stated; the tolerance for a real pool must be recalibrated on its own pedigree.
  - Scope of the objective (05b:2068-2127): the diversity constraint stays additive; two plans with the same $\alpha$ and $GD_{BS}$ differ in the divergence term by $m/2$ times the difference in $\mathrm{cov}(d, y^2)$, how large between attainable plans is not measured; $\lambda$ needs two covariances ($\mathrm{cov}(d, y^2)$, $\mathrm{cov}(d, H_S)$), not $d$; no SCA is identifiable against a single tester (@sec-heterosis-step6).
  - Forward, inference not result (05b:2155-2167): selection on cross performance is the only process considered that can move frequencies depending on where the dominance is; the dominance loop is @sec-alphasimr, and @sec-cycles has no dominance in its traits and does not test it.
- Assumes: inbred parents (A2) and the complete A x B factorial with equal pool weights for the $GD_T$/$GD_{BS}$ identities; flat dominance ($d_k \equiv 1$) for the reading of @eq-het-split as $GD_T$ and $GD_{BS}$, @eq-het-distance and @eq-het-sca-dc, with the varying-$d$ forms stated; dominance within loci, additivity between, no epistasis; the marker panel is the set of dominance loci; neutral Balding-Nichols redraws from one ancestral frequency vector (ancestral $p^{*}$ uniform on [0.05, 0.95], as in `R/00_data.R:40`) for the expectations, all stated in expectation over the draw and checked against one draw per row; independent-Bernoulli sampling of lines (unrelated, linkage equilibrium) for the closed-form variance components; random mating for @eq-het-id; the gamma(2, 4) $d$, seed 4, positive at every locus, not varied; the teaching scale (A8) with seeds 4 ($d$), 5 (sweep), 7001-9000 (Step 7), 55 + 1e5 k + i (Step 6). No primary text was read for 05b itself (Caballero & Toro 2002 enters through 02). Cited and not read: Falconer & Mackay (Ch. 7, 14 locators UNVERIFIED), Fisher (1918), Sprague & Tatum (1942), Griffing (1956), Reif et al. (2007, formula level), Balding & Nichols (1995, beta form), Labroo et al. (2021, beyond refcheck), Mackay et al. (2020) and White et al. (2020) (abstracts), Technow et al. (2014, refcheck's reading), Comstock et al. (1949, title only), Moll et al. (1962) and Melchinger & Gumber (1998, existence only; not used).
- Takes from: `00`: A2, A3 (@sec-hp-cycle), @eq-hp-gca-sca (the model whose dominance part 05b isolates; 00:122-130), @sec-hp-popgen and the $H_o$ identity (0.3617, 00:435-447), @sec-hp-inbreeding ($F_{BN}$ against $F_{ST}$, 00:561-585), @tbl-hp-stages (S1, S2). `01`: $d_k$, $H_i$ and @eq-het-value first used (01:41-46), @tbl-diagonal and $\bar f^L_{AB}$ (01:360-380), $f^L$. `02`: @tbl-keystone and @sec-keystone (the conditions of the $GD_{BS}$ row: flat dominance, inbred parents, complete factorial, equal weights, neutral redraws), @eq-f-stats ($F_{ST} = GD_{BS}/GD_T$), @eq-gd-partition. `02b`: @sec-response-genic (gene diversity stands in for genic variance only through $GD_{WS}$ and only if effects are unrelated to which loci lose diversity), the two GCA statements and the illustrative $d \sim N(0.3, 0.1^2)$ draw (seed 7, not 05b's). Forward, declared: `03` (generator), `04` (P9), `04c`, `04e` (@sec-mating-estimation for the GCA of the cross mean), `05`, `06` (@eq-catalogue-lambda-d), `08` (Traps 1, 3), `08b`, `11b`, `12`. Earlier chapters and files changed by this review (wording, d_k -> "dominance effect"): `00` (00:40, 00:125, 00:138, 00:165), `01` (01:44, 01:344), `02` (02:1097-1098), `02b` (02b:578, 02b:586, 02b:592), `index.qmd` (identity paragraph now states its conditions), roxygen of `R/13_dominance.R`, `R/09_reference.R`, `R/05_pipeline_metrics.R` (`pool_complementarity()` no longer "the quantity that actually generates heterosis").
- Owed:
  - [settled in 04c] -> `04c`: `04c:333` calls $d_k$ the "dominance deviation"; the chapter now says dominance effect (05b:152-155). -- settled in 04c: "the dominance effect, the departure of the heterozygote from that midpoint (a property of the locus, as in @sec-heterosis-dk-meaning; not Fisher's statistical dominance deviation ... what `varD` and `genicVarD` measure)" (04c:392-396).
  - -> `04e`: `04e:176` same wording.
  - [settled in 05] -> `05`: `05:325-326` same wording ("$d_k$ the dominance / deviation at locus $k$", line break inside the phrase). -- settled in 05: "the dominance effect at locus $k$" (05:421-423).
  - [settled in 06] -> `06`: `06:364` and `06:479` same wording. -- settled in 06: 06:680 "dominance effects $d_k$" and 06:472-479 (no "dominance deviation" left in 06).

## 03-simulating-crosses -- Simulating lines, pools and crosses
- Question: how the stage-1 objects the rest of the book consumes (`X`, `f`, `hybrids`, optional `fL`, and the `ctx` built from them) are generated, which assumptions the generator makes, what its divergence knob `fst` does to the heterozygous fraction and the gene-diversity components, and what its traits do and do not model. It follows 05b because the Foundations chapters drew their data from `simulate_pools()` (02b) and `simulate_lines()`/`simulate_data()` (05b) without deriving them, and 02b and 05b both announce 03 as the next step (03:19-47). It is a generator chapter: it derives no new population-genetic result and claims no heterosis (sec-sim-divergence, 03:361-376).
- Establishes:
  - Opening (03:19-47): `simulate_pools()` and `simulate_data()` consume R's random stream in the same order up to the lines, so at `book_cfg`'s values they return the same lines and the same hybrid matrix `X` (asserted in the chunk of @tbl-line-genotypes, @sec-sim-lines, 03:122-134), and only the traits differ (`n_qtl` loci with independent effects plus noise against correlated effects at every marker and no noise). `simulate_pools()` clamps pool frequencies to $[10^{-6}, 1 - 10^{-6}]$ and `simulate_lines()` does not, so the pool frequencies agree to within $10^{-6}$ and the lines exactly at those arguments. 02b's chunk asserts `cfg$fst == book_cfg$fst` and `cfg$seed == book_cfg$seed` (02b:63-64).
  - The stage-1 contract (@tbl-stage1-contract, 03:56-75): `X` N x m in {0, 0.5, 1}, `f` N x N, `hybrids` N x (3 + n_traits), optional `fL` L x L (coancestry between lines); stage 2 runs without `fL` and loses only the heterotic-group decomposition.
  - Generator (@sec-sim-lines, @eq-balding-nichols, 03:76-137): Balding-Nichols-type model with the name and the one-parameter idea taken from @balding1995 and the Beta densities and the reading of $F_{BN}$ declared the book's own specification, not quoted from the paper. $p^{*}_k \sim U(0.05, 0.95)$, $q^{*}_k = 1 - p^{*}_k$; $p_{Ak}, p_{Bk}$ independent Beta draws with mean $p^{*}_k$ and variance $F_{BN}\,p^{*}_k q^{*}_k$ (derived from $ab/[(a+b)^2(a+b+1)]$, $a + b = (1 - F_{BN})/F_{BN}$): $F_{BN}$ is the divergence of each pool from the ancestral frequency, $\mathrm{Var}(p_{Ak} - p_{Bk}) = 2F_{BN}p^{*}_kq^{*}_k$, and the statistic $F_{ST} = GD_{BS}/GD_T$ on two pools of unbounded size estimates about $F_{BN}/(2 - F_{BN})$ = 0.081 at 0.15. Lines are $2 \times$ Bernoulli, so every line is homozygous (0 or 2 only, @tbl-line-genotypes).
  - **$p^{*}_k$ against `ctx$p0`** (03:93-97, 03:558-567): $p^{*}_k$ is a parameter of the draw, never observed in the data, and is neither $p_{Ak}$, $p_{Bk}$ nor `ctx$p0`; `ctx$p0` is `colMeans(X)` of the hybrids, a sample quantity, equal for equal pools and the complete factorial to the mean of the two pools' sampled line frequencies (asserted, relative deviation 1.1e-16); `maf_pop = pmin(p0, 1 - p0)`; `p0` is the frozen base of $F_{hom}$ and $F_{drift}$ (@sec-genomic-era), never recomputed per scenario.
  - **The generator's declared assumptions** (@sec-sim-assumptions, 03:141-176): biallelic, neutral, unlinked markers (drift alone from one ancestral frequency vector, no selection, mutation or migration after the split; $p^{*}_k$, $p_{Ak}$, $p_{Bk}$ and every genotype independent across $k$; no map, no recombination, no linkage disequilibrium beyond sampling noise); unrelated lines drawn independently given the pool frequency and inbred by fiat at fixation (no doubled-haploid or selfing path, @sec-hp-cycle); equal pool sizes ($n_A = n_B$) and the complete A x B factorial with equal weights, an assumption and not a property of hybrid programmes; additive traits with effects at every marker, known without error. The reported numbers are conditional on these; the exact identities (per-hybrid heterozygous fraction $= 1 - f^L_{ab}$ for any inbred parents, $\mathbf f$ = `tcrossprod(cbind(X, 1 - X))/m` for any 0/0.5/1 matrix, $\sum\mathbf G = 0$ for any sample-centred panel) do not need them all; independence across loci enters the dispersion of the replicates, not the expectations. Relaxations are pointed to: linkage, outbred founders, directional dominance, several generations in @sec-alphasimr; which crosses in @sec-mating and @sec-joint; dominance in @sec-heterosis and @sec-alphasimr; prediction error in @sec-genomic-prediction.
  - **The divergence knob, $F_{BN}$ grid at $m$ = 2000** (@sec-sim-divergence, @fig-divergence-het, @tbl-divergence-grid, @tbl-divergence-trade, 03:178-335): teaching lines (25 + 25), $m$ = `book_cfg$m` = 2000, six $F_{BN}$ from 0.02 to 0.40, one draw per point; the 0.15 row is the F1 of the chapter's own `L` and the value of @tbl-diagonal: **heterozygous fraction 0.3617**, $GD_{BS}$ 0.0336, $GD_T$ 0.3281, $GD_{WS}$ 0.2945 (asserted against `L`, deviation 1.1e-16). Heterozygous fraction over the grid 0.3617-0.3663 against an expectation of 0.3650 that contains no $F_{BN}$. **Replicate numbers**, 200 seeds, the two ends of the grid only: **0.3651 (s.d. 0.0033)** at $F_{BN}$ = 0.02 and **0.3651 (s.d. 0.0059)** at 0.40; paired change between the ends 2.2e-6 (s.e. 4.0e-4), 0.01 s.e. from zero; a single draw per point scatters by about 0.006, so the figure alone cannot separate a flat line from a small trend and the expectation and the replicate means carry the flatness. Between the ends $GD_{WS}$ changes by $-0.1323$, $GD_{BS}$ by $+0.0674$, $GD_T$ by $-0.0649$: **single-draw ratios 1.04** ($GD_{BS}$ gain / $GD_T$ loss) **and 1.96** ($GD_{WS}$ loss / $GD_{BS}$ gain) against the expectations 1 and 2 of @sec-subdivided under drift in two equal pools; over the 200 replicate grids 1.005 and 2.003 (s.d. 0.086 each), so this draw is within ordinary sampling variation.
  - The expectation (03:337-360, under the assumptions of @sec-sim-assumptions): per hybrid the heterozygous fraction is exactly $1 - f^L_{ab}$ (asserted in the chunk of @tbl-ctx-check); its mean over the complete factorial is $1 - \bar f^{L}_{AB}$, with $\bar f^{L}_{AB}$ = `between_pool_f` (written $f_{AB}$ in @sec-subdivided and $\theta_{AB}$ in @sec-notation, identified on the complete factorial in @sec-heterosis); with equal pool sizes $1 - \bar f^L_{AB} = GD_T + GD_{BS}$ exactly. For $n$ lines per pool the sample frequencies add binomial variance, $\mathbb E[GD_{BS}] = F_{BN}p^{*}q^{*} + (1 - F_{BN})p^{*}q^{*}/n$ per marker; $GD_T$ loses and $GD_{BS}$ gains $F_{BN}p^{*}q^{*}(1 - 1/n)$, the factor cancelling in the ratios; expected change 0.0666 against 0.0694 for unbounded pools. @sec-heterosis-flat derives the same expectation and offset on another grid (seed 5); the formulas agree and the numbers here are a different draw. 03 keeps its copy because it compares it with numbers computed here (it1-M8, ruled acceptable).
  - **No heterosis is claimed** (03:361-376): the data carry no dominance, so the heterozygous fraction is not heterosis here; it is the F1 heterosis per locus only at flat dominance ($d_k \equiv 1$, @eq-het-value); its identification with $GD_T + GD_{BS}$ needs in addition inbred parents, the complete A x B factorial and equal pool weights, the conditions of the $GD_{BS}$ row of @tbl-keystone. Under them neutral divergence relocates diversity from $GD_T$ to $GD_{BS}$ and creates no expected heterosis; outside them 03 says nothing about heterosis. That $GD_{BS}$ is to be preserved is not a consequence of the flat line: @tbl-keystone states it for its conditions and @sec-heterosis-alignment treats what the divergence must satisfy.
  - Hybrids and traits (@sec-sim-hybrids, @sec-sim-traits, 03:376-450): every A x B combination is formed (625 at the teaching scale, $71^2$ at the production scale of `R/00_data.R`); `R`, `B`, `L` are names of code objects local to the chapter, not $\mathbf R$, $\mathbf B$, $L$ of @sec-notation, and $n_A$, $n_B$ count lines of pool A and pool B, not the female and male lines of @sec-mating. Marker effects are correlated through a Cholesky factor of a fixed `R`; the realised trait correlations depart from `R` by up to the value printed in @tbl-trait-cor; rank of $\mathbf Z$ is $(n_A - 1) + (n_B - 1)$; how closely the realised correlations approach `R` on average over draws is not measured.
  - **The callout is limited to maximality** (03:452-477): the predicted values are built from known marker effects and carry no prediction error; for a given candidate set and diversity constraint no selection made on estimated values has a higher mean true index than the optimum computed on the known values, because the optimum maximises that index over every feasible selection; the book's gains come from greedy, DE and local search, which at book scale are not certified optima (@sec-bound; `ocs_bound()` is the valid bound, `sel_exact()` does not converge there); a feasible selection is not a bound, so a gain in `ctx$index` does not bound the gain a selection on estimates could achieve; the statement concerns the index only, not the diversity metrics, which do not depend on the traits. The wording "optimistic ceiling" is not 03's.
  - The two matrices (@sec-ctx-built, @tbl-ctx-check, 03:479-567): $\sum\mathbf G = -4.18 \times 10^{-11}$, relative to $\sum|\mathbf G|$ 1.5e-15, zero up to rounding because `vanraden_G()` centres on the **sample** frequencies of this panel, `colMeans(M)/2`, so $\mathbf 1'\mathbf G\mathbf 1 = 0$ (@eq-g-sum-zero); **$\sum\mathbf G = 0$ belongs to sample centring** and need not vanish at other reference frequencies (a constant 0.4 gives 309640.6). `f` equals `tcrossprod(cbind(X, 1 - X))/m` to 0 and lies in $[0, 1]$; `min(f) >= 0` is the weak property, the informative one is the range, 0.64475-0.83175 (printed 0.6448 and 0.8317 in @tbl-ctx-check, 0.6448 and 0.8318 in 01's table: the same value rounded two ways); `f` is identity in state, unrelated individuals are not at 0, its level is set by the marker panel (@sec-coancestry). `simulate_data(book_cfg)` builds this chapter's `ctx` element by element (asserted, 1e-12), apart from the column names of `traits`. Exact identities asserted at relative 1e-12 with the achieved deviation printed.
  - Stage-1 call and coding (03:569-660): `stage1_simulate()` passes `X`, `ped`, `traits`, `fL` to `stage1_build()`, the entry for real data (@tbl-st1-components, @tbl-st1-hybrids); `coding = NULL` infers 0/1/2 from any value above 1, so a 0/1 matrix without a dosage-2 call is ambiguous and is read as 0/0.5/1 with a warning, demonstrated with a two-hybrid example (@tbl-coding-ambiguous); declaring `coding` removes the assumption.
  - Candidate set (03:663-723, @fig-candidate-pool, @tbl-gd-baseline): the whole set sits above the distribution of 400 random subsets of 60 (0.32809 against 0.32586, Monte Carlo s.e. 3.8e-5, gap 59 s.e., apparent loss 0.681 %): sampling, not selection, and the subject of @sec-baseline, which owns the explanation and measures the same gap with its own subsets.
- Assumes: the generator's assumptions above (biallelic, neutral, unlinked markers; unrelated, fully inbred lines; equal pool sizes and the complete A x B factorial with equal weights; additive traits with effects at every marker known without error); for the heterozygous fraction to be $GD_T + GD_{BS}$ exactly, inbred parents, the complete factorial and equal pool weights; for it to be heterosis, in addition flat dominance $d_k \equiv 1$, which the data of 03 do not carry; neutral Balding-Nichols redraws from one ancestral vector for the expectations; the teaching scale (A8: 25 + 25 lines, 2000 markers, 625 hybrids) with `book_cfg`'s seed and, for the replicates, seeds 1 to 200 (subsets with seed `cfg$seed + 3`, traits with `cfg$seed + 99`). Read against sources: @balding1995 for the name and the one-parameter idea only (full text unread; the abstract does not support the Beta form, which is the book's own), @vanraden2008 for the matrix of @eq-vanraden-g by name (code and algebra checked, paper unread).
- Takes from: `00`: A2 (inbred parents), A5/A8 (independent loci, teaching scale), @sec-hp-cycle (DH and selfing paths the generator does not model), $F_{BN}$ against $F_{ST}$ (00:561-585), the 0.3617 (00:435-447). `01`: @eq-g-sum-zero, @eq-vanraden-g, @eq-f-molecular, $\mathbf f \in [0,1]$ and its level set by the panel, @tbl-diagonal (the 0.3617 and the flat heterozygous fraction). `02`: @eq-f-stats, @eq-gd-partition, drift ratios 1 and 2 in two equal pools (02:596-616), @tbl-keystone and the conditions of its $GD_{BS}$ row (02:1039, 02:1085-1100). `02b`: the `simulate_pools()` call and its description (02b:48-59, 02b:63-64). `05b`: @sec-heterosis-flat (the expectation and the sample-frequency offset), @eq-het-value, @sec-heterosis-alignment, @sec-heterosis-gca-sca. Forward, declared and not needed to read 03: `05` (@sec-genomic-era), `06` (@sec-baseline), `07` (@sec-collapse), `04c`/`04e`/`04g`, `08b` (@sec-genomic-prediction), `10b` (@sec-bound), `12` (@sec-case-study). Earlier chapters and files changed by this review: `01` (01:374-378, the ancestral frequency $p_{0,k}$ -> $p^{*}_k$, "never observed, not the frozen base"; 01:420-446 keeps $p_{0,k}$ for the base), `05b` (plain $p$ -> $p^{*}$, $q^{*} = 1 - p^{*}$ declared once at 05b:620, in the equations of @sec-heterosis-flat, @eq-het-var-bn, @eq-het-gca-peak, @eq-het-sca-gca-ratio; the hybrid-set $p_kq_k$ at 05b:480, 05b:501 left alone; also the 05b-M28/M29/M30 wording and comment fixes), `02b` (the `stopifnot` at 02b:63-64 and the `identical` check against `simulate_lines()`).
- Owed:
