## 04-population-sizing -- Sizing a segregating population, F1 to F4
- Question: how many plants a selfing programme must carry through F1 -> F2 -> F3 -> F4 for one biparental cross, for each objective a breeder may size for (diversity retained, the worst region of the genome, precision of the panel's allele frequency, recovery of a P2 homozygote at $k$ loci), on a simulator of ancestry along a genetic map. It follows 03 because 03 draws parent lines "inbred by fiat" and does not model the selfing path (03:165, now pointing to @sec-sizing); 04 models that path. What is sized is the F4 line panel (a line-level object), not the hybrid candidate set, which is formed from lines afterwards (04:15-23). Selfing is the alternative to DH for deriving lines from a within-pool cross (S2, A2); a cross between two heterotic groups would give a derived population outside A1-A8 / A3, which enters no number because the simulator labels ancestry only (04:24-35).
- Establishes:
  - Sizing assumptions SA1-SA9 (04:50-229; parents are P1, P2): SA1 ancestry not alleles (loci fixed for different alleles in the parents, "allele frequency" = frequency of P1 ancestry, 04:40-48); SA2 homozygous parents, so the F1 is one genotype and its sizing is logistical; SA3 map of `maize_chr_len`, own model input, not an IBM map, and the intermating junction recursion $(2 + g/2)L$ ($4L$ at $g = 4$, $4.5L$ at $g = 5$, against $L$ for an F2 haplotype and $2L$ for selfing to fixation); SA4 Haldane, no interference (expected crossover number fixed; observed junction count and drag not measured); SA5 distortion by rejection sampling, three distorters all against P2 (@tbl-sizing-distorters, placeholder labels), a model input, "plausible, not measured", so every distortion statement is for this one configuration (04:114-152); SA6 five schemes (`ssd`, `ssd_attr`, `bulk` at $\beta = 6$, `syn1`, `syn2`), all ending at $H = 0.125$; SA7 the F4 panel is not a RIL panel, $H = 0.125$; SA8 s.e. between replicates, not between markers; SA9 not modelled (artificial selection, QTL architecture, phenotypic inbreeding depression, mutation, structural variation) (04:220-224).
  - Symbols (04:164-167, 90-notation edited): $H$ here is observed heterozygosity $H_o$ of @sec-hp-popgen, averaged over plants and loci, not $H_T$/$H_S$ of 02; $h_t$ is the heterozygosity of the parent whose meiosis produces generation $t + 1$ (recursion symbol, not the $H_t$ of @eq-hp-drift); $D$ = diversity retained, $m^{-1}\sum_j 2p_j(1-p_j)/0.5$, with the panel itself as frequency base; $n_{rep}$ replicate count. F convention: $F^{(2)} = 0.75$ for an F3 plant, F1 as base (00 convention, 04:1045-1056).
  - @eq-sizing-div, $\mathrm E[D] = 1 - (1 - H)/N$ for neutral SSD (04:197-216): not derived in full; sketch at $p_0 = 1/2$ ($\mathrm E[D] = 1 - 4\mathrm{Var}(\hat p)$, $\mathrm{Var}(\hat p) = (1-H)/(4N)$), equal to the $1/n$ loss of @eq-hp-drift at $H = 0$ and to $n_{gam} = N/(1 - H)$; a sampling-variance equivalence, not a Wright $N_e$. $N_{eq} = (1 - H)/(1 - D)$ is a communication device, not an $N_e$ (04:1057-1065).
  - Live checks of the identities (@sec-sizing-verify, 04:231-397): per-plant heterozygosity F2 0.497, F3 0.254, F4 0.127 against $(1/2)^{t-1}$; neutral $D$ against @eq-sizing-div on 100 replicates per $N$; expected junctions against the cached grid; the junction-count deficit from two junctions in one marker interval, replicated on 20 neutral panels of 500 lines per spacing: 2 cM -4.2% (s.e. 0.19), 1 cM -2.2% (0.16), 20 of 20 negative.
  - Results on the 20-size grid, $N$ = 10 to 390 (04:399-927; 2 cM, distortion active, SSD, seed 20260828 cached; recovery replicated live, 20 populations per $N$, seeds 20261108 + $N$): diversity stays below the distortion-free curve at every $N$ tested, and the gap is attributed to distortion by a neutral twin at $N$ = 110 and 390 only (gap 5.3e-4 and 1.2e-4 neutral against 1.0e-2 and 9.1e-3 active; @tbl-sizing-twin, 04:484-548); `ssd_attr` deficit is its smaller panel (70-75% of $N$ lines; @eq-sizing-div at $n_{final}$ accounts for 103% of the summed difference); the bulk (selection for P1 ancestry) retains the least $D$ at every grid size, margin 6.7 to 20.9 s.e. over the 20 sizes against the lowest other scheme; the one cross-size contrast (bulk at 390 against SSD at 30: 0.0074, 2.2 s.e.) is exploratory and not offered as a finding (04:576-636); block length changes little with $N$ over this grid (junction slope at most 1.3% of the mean count over the grid, all five schemes), so linkage drag is moved by an intercrossing cycle, not by field area, for this grid (04:637-691).
  - The metrics saturate at different $N$ (@tbl-sizing-saturation, 04:692-927): $N_{90}$ defined on the grid (relative to its upper end 390) with 2-s.e. brackets; $D$ bracket 70-110, $\min(p, 1-p)$ bracket 30-350 (the grid does not locate it, so the contrast rests on $D$ and recovery); recovery of a P2 homozygote at $k$ unlinked loci against @eq-sizing-n95 ($N_{95}(k) = \log 0.05/\log[1 - ((1-H)/2)^k]$, Sedcole): replicated first crossing of 0.95 at $k = 3$: $N$ = 50 (bracket 30-50), $k = 5$: 270 (230-290; closed form 185 understates it), $k = 8, 10$ not reached (0.293, 0.057 at 390); closed form against neutral simulation at $k = 5$, $N = 110$, 10 cM: 0.825 against 0.831, with three distorters 0.752 (s.e. 0.013, seed 9), 6.1 s.e. below; 59 of 60 points ($k \ge 5$) more than 2 s.e. below the closed form (not independent). Gains past $N_{90}$ are within 2 s.e. of zero step by step (@tbl-sizing-steps).
  - F1 sizing (@sec-sizing-f1, @tbl-sizing-f1, 04:929-1006): with fully homozygous parents the F1 is one genotype; with residual heterozygosity $h_{res}$ the expected parental alleles lost is $2h_{res}n_{loc}\cdot 2(1/2)^n$ (`f1_sizing()`) and the expected loci made monomorphic is $n_{loc}[2h_{res}(1-h_{res})(1/2)^n + h_{res}^2\cdot 2(1/4)^n]$ (enumeration oracle `p_mono()` asserted against the closed form, $n = 1..20$); scenario values, not estimates for any germplasm. Working numbers table (@tbl-sizing-working-numbers, 04:1007-1056).
  - Bridge to the later kernel (@sec-sizing-connects, 04:1067-1170): $GD = D/2$ exactly and $N_s = 1/(2 - D)$ (asserted); the bound $GD_{subset} \le (1 - \text{fixed})/2$ is not binding on three 10 cM panels (fixed fraction 0 at N = 30, `bound` column of the table, GD 0.482-0.494 whole panel, greedy 10-line subset 0.479-0.488 not above its panel, random 0.451-0.454, $N_s$ 0.966-0.988); only the conditional "no selection of lines restores an allele the panel does not carry" is claimed, not a ceiling; $N_s \in [1/2, 1]$ for every set of lines (a monotone rescaling of $\theta$, not a head count, no pooling of crosses would make it one), so $D$ and $GD$ compare sizing scenarios (04:1157-1172).
- Assumes: the SA1-SA9 above, in particular: loci on distinct chromosomes for recovery, one distorter configuration (SA5), 2 cM map unless a 10 cM or 4 cM map is stated; a single uniform F1 and inbred parents (A2) for @eq-sizing-div and @eq-sizing-n95; independent heterozygosity in the two parents and independent loci for the F1 argument; biallelic loci (SA1). Scope of the bridge (04:1075-1084): the later chapters budget the $GD$ of hybrids against a frozen base $p_0$ and a random-subset reference under A2, A5, A8; the panel departs from each (lines carry $H = 0.125$, so $GD_{WI} > 0$ at line level; linked markers, SA4; its own frequency base; $D$ divides by 0.5, not by a frozen base), so what is established is the line-level quantity of one derived panel, not the later hybrid budget. Reported numbers are for the simulated cross at the grid's reduced scale, not for any germplasm.
- Takes from: `00`: A2 and A3 (@sec-hp-cycle, @sec-era-programme), A1-A8 and the stage table @tbl-hp-stages (S2), @sec-hp-popgen ($H_o$, the DH sampling unit), @sec-hp-inbreeding ($F^{(t)}$ convention), @eq-hp-drift ($H_t$, the $1/n$ loss), @eq-hp-gd. `01`: @sec-coancestry ($\mathbf f$ only), @sec-identity (GD). `02`: @sec-subdivided ($H_T$, $H_S$, to say $H$ is not those). `02b`/`06`: @sec-baseline (the finite-set bias, same sampling factor), alpha < 0 as a definition (see M18). `03`: @sec-simulating-crosses (lines inbred by fiat, the candidate set). `05b`: none (04 does not cite it; 05b cites 04's SA9). External, not in the repo: Lee et al. 2002 abstract, Winkler et al. 2003 abstract only, Falque 2009, Lu 2002, Zhang 2018, Sedcole 1977, Fisher 1954, Haldane 1919.
- Owed:
  - -> `index`: `index.qmd:116-118` "a panel cannot be selected back up to variation it never sampled (@sec-sizing)" is true only of alleles the panel lacks, which is all 04 claims (04:1154-1155 "no selection of lines restores an allele that the panel does not carry"); restate with that scope or keep it conditional (04-C6).
  - -> `04d`: `04d:376-379` "That is the same arithmetic @sec-sizing explains for a biparental cross" (diagonal dominance, $N_s$ near or below 1 on inbred lines); 04 now explains $N_s \in [1/2, 1]$ for every set of lines as a monotone function of $D$ (04:1157-1172), not the diagonal argument; align 04d (04-C3, 04-M20).
  - -> `04e`: `04e:35-37` says @sec-simulating-crosses "derived populations from crosses already chosen"; 03 derives none and the derived-population chapter is @sec-sizing (03-C10, also in the 03 entry).
  - [settled in 08] -> `08`: first half of 04-C10: 08 does not vary plants per cross and does not cite @sec-sizing; 04 now says so ("what more plants would cost against what it buys is not established here", 04:1173-1177). Check that 08 neither claims nor implies the measurement. -- settled in 08: 08 does not vary plants per cross, says so (08:852-856: the file does not vary crosses or DH lines per cross) and cites @sec-sizing only for genetic maps (08:416).
  - -> `11c`: second half of 04-C10: 11c's "same argument" about what is recoverable from a panel; 04 points to @sec-limits for the question asked about the variance, with "a different conclusion about what is recoverable" (04:1174-1177); keep both true.
  - -> `90`: `$n_{rep}$` row added in 04's it3 pass; the row says "the n_rep column of the cached grid" while the symbol also names live arms (`n_rep_chk`, 20-population arms); widen the wording (non-decisive).
  - -> quant-Rwrite: `regenerate.R` could cache the replicated recovery grid; contested and not done, since the chapter computes it live (~35 s) and a cached copy is a second source of truth. `R/06_f2size.R` roxygen of `f1_sizing()` was fixed (formula 0.9278 against the "about half" 0.9766 at $n$ = 9).
  - -> render batch: Quarto render, `book/_freeze/04-population-sizing` and `docs/` refresh (live re-execution ~35 s); the legend overlap in @fig-sizing-ancestry and the Typst wrapping of @tbl-sizing-saturation and @tbl-sizing-working-numbers were never inspected (04-M22, E4, E6).
  - Not a chapter, non-decisive: 04-M18 (04:1151-1152 says the book's results "show" a selection with $\alpha < 0$ at @sec-baseline, which defines $\alpha < 0$ but does not display one; peripheral to the bridge); 04-M19 (the distortion arm reports one seed, 0.752 (0.013); other 20-population arms gave 0.708 (0.011) and 0.747 (0.014), arm-to-arm spread about 0.04, larger than the within-arm s.e.; the direction holds, 3 to 11 s.e. below 0.831, but the "6.1 s.e." is a one-seed figure). Unmeasured: sensitivity to the strengths, positions or number of distorters (SA5, 04-M17); interference (SA4); bridge on one subset size and one heuristic. UNVERIFIED sources (not read in full): Lee et al. 2002 (which baseline the "four-fold increase" uses), Winkler et al. 2003 (abstract only), Sedcole 1977, Fisher 1954, Haldane 1919 and the other maize citations, by name and abstract level only; refcheck marked them non-decisive.

## 04b-backcross-introgression -- Introgressing several genes by backcrossing
- Question: how many plants a marker-assisted backcross (MABC) conversion needs to move $k \le 4$ unlinked recessive targets from one donor into an elite line by BC3, and which of its three success criteria (not losing the genes, recovering the recurrent parent, linkage drag) binds; then what a panel of such conversions does to gene diversity. It follows 04 as the other half of the sizing question: 04 sized a segregating population for four objectives, and 04b's fixation section asks 04's fourth kind (a homozygote at $k$ unlinked loci) from a BC3 rather than an F2-F4 scheme, the rest being the mirror image (discard donor genome except at $k$ loci) (04b:53-64). It keeps 04's promise (04:30) to treat donor introgression "on its own terms" outside A1-A8, and states where it stands: A2 kept, A3 not met (donor outside the pool), A5 dropped in the main body and restored in @sec-bc-connects, A1/A4/A6/A7/A8 unused; conversion is a pre-breeding operation, not one of S1-S4 (04b:65-80).
- Establishes:
  - Model (@sec-bc-model, 04b:182-273): one inbred recurrent parent, only the donor haplotype tracked as a binary vector on a 5 cM marker grid, ten maize chromosomes; Haldane meiosis per interval, @eq-bc-haldane (04b:192), asserted against `haldane_r()` (04b:210-212); "exact for the marker chain", not the chromosome: two crossovers inside one 5 cM interval cancel (04's 2 cM deficit 4.2% is cited, not measured here at 5 cM; direction for drag not shown) and drag has a floor of one cell, 5 cM per target, on a 5 cM lattice, ceiling = 30/5 = 6 cells (04b:197-209). $s$ = fraction of the genome donor and elite share by descent; $D$ = donor dosage; $\mathrm{IBD}_E = 1 - (1-s)D$, @eq-bc-ibd (04b:222): the donor-specific complement is exact per plant, the proportionality only in expectation (04b:215-229). Coupling phase, so drag is the union of donor segments and $P_{\text{same}}$ of @eq-bc-psame (04b:241) applies to linked targets only; every run puts the targets on different chromosomes, so $P_{\text{same}}$ is unused in any reported number (04b:243-247); $d$ in @eq-bc-psame is the distance between two targets, not the marker interval of @eq-bc-haldane (04b:247). Repulsion (two donors) is outside the model (04b:267-273).
  - Three criteria, at $s = 0.60$ only (section "Three criteria; at $s = 0.60$ one of them binds", 04b:275-441): foreground, $q = (1/2)^k$, exact (@tbl-bc-foreground, 04b:291); at $k = 4$ the 47 plants of the table are per generation, lineage survival $(1-(15/16)^{47})^3 = 0.862$, 64 plants per generation for 0.95 over three generations; scan $p_{lost}$ 0.193 (N = 40, 300 replicates) and 0.050 (N = 60, 200) under `rec_drag` (04b:314-329). Recovery of the elite is free by BC3: $\mathrm{IBD}_E = 1 - (1-s)(1/2)^4 = 0.975$ for an unselected plant, mean simulated 0.946-0.964 under selection, conditional on kept targets (@tbl-bc-ibd, 04b:330-374). Only linkage drag binds at $s = 0.60$.
  - At $s = 0$ the IBD criterion binds too (@tbl-bc-s0, 04b:376-441; `rec_drag`, $k$ = 4, uniform schedules, 500 replicates per row, own seeds, Clopper-Pearson, the two $s$ unpaired): P(IBD $\ge 0.90$) 0.532 [0.487, 0.576] (N = 60), 0.782 [0.743, 0.817] (200), 0.822 [0.786, 0.855] (400), upper limits all below 0.95; $s = 0.60$: 0.932, 1.000, 1.000; $p_{lost}$ 0.088/0/0 and 0.068/0/0. Drag does not stop binding at $s = 0$: the table measures IBD alone, so IBD is added to the drag criterion, not substituted for it (04b:1078-1084). Not run, and said so (04b:432-441): N > 400 at $s = 0$, a background-priority stage, the joint drag-and-IBD criterion at $s = 0$, any $k \ne 4$, other drag ceiling, IBD target or grid. The three statements of the section hold at $s = 0.60$ and not in general.
  - The corrected index (@sec-bc-index, 04b:453-554): `rec_bg` (background decides among the recombinant-selected survivors) discards stage 1; `rec_drag` (drag decides, background breaks ties) is the default of `mabc_opt$main_strategy`. Paired on common random numbers, four genes certified (Clopper-Pearson lower bound >= 0.95) at N = 400 plants per generation, next size tested 250 not certified (lower bound 0.901); under `rec_bg` the same cell fails, which produced the retracted "BC4-BC5 or split in two" conclusion. "At $s = 0.60$, in this generating model, the wall was a property of the index, not of the cross simulated"; compared at $s = 0.60$ only (04b:551-554). Policies and "how long to keep selecting recombinants" (04b:556-593): three unpaired estimates in one cell (3 genes, N = 200), no dispersion, so the first step is not resolved. Allocation across BC1/BC2/BC3 (04b:595-737): the coarse scan is under `rec_bg`, before the correction; the paired re-run under both indices (@tbl-bc-recheck, 04b:655) at 120/120/120 gives P(both) 0.969 [0.956, 0.979], not separated from 40/120/200 (0.955); the two 20-plant-generation schedules lose targets (20/150/190: $p_{lost}$ 0.063); five schedules, one budget, one gene number, one $s$.
  - Fixation (@sec-bc-fixation, 04b:739-818): BC3F2 $q = (1/4)^k$, DH $q = (1/2)^k$, requirement $n_{keep} = 3$ homozygotes as a cumulative binomial; four genes 1610 BC3F2 plants against 99 DH lines (factor 16.3); same derivation as 04's @eq-sizing-n95 for a different event, 04 owns it (04b:752-762); both assume independent targets and no distortion, and 04's closed form understated the simulated cross at $k = 5$ (185 against 270), while the BC3F2 binomial was checked against a distortion-free simulation only (`mabc_bcnf2.csv`).
  - Running (@sec-bc-running, 04b:820-955): `run_bc_program()`, one illustrative programme (@tbl-bc-traj, 04b:830, seeds 5 and 6, `rec_drag`, BC3 drag 60.0 cM) and one paired index comparison on seed 7 (@tbl-bc-dragcmp, 04b:868, `rec_bg` 70, `rec_drag` 45 cM; one replicate, illustrative); `sizing_table()` reproduces `mabc_analytical_sizing.csv` exactly (04b:889-893). Provenance of the six cached tables (04b:913-940): `mabc_index_comparison.csv` and `mabc_allocation_recheck.csv` written into `book/data` by the current `run_mabc.R --only=...`, one seed per cell; `mabc_analytical_sizing.csv` is the full run's, with no random component; `mabc_uniform.csv`, `mabc_allocation.csv`, `mabc_bcnf2.csv` and the two figures `mabc_policies.png`, `mabc_sizing.png` come from the predecessor of the current driver, never rewritten by it and never diffed against a re-run; replicate counts of the allocation and BC3F2 scans are typed (`MB_alloc`, `MB_f2`, 04b:20-34) and checked for consistency with the CSVs, not derived. `MB` is asserted equal to `mabc_opt` (04b:22-26).
  - Assumptions and what is not modelled (04b:957-1037): no interference (Haldane; every close-in population size a lower bound, by how much not computed; @falque2009), one value of each criterion (drag ceiling 30 cM per gene, IBD target 0.90, 5 cM grid, $s$ except in @tbl-bc-s0), one plant advanced per generation (single-lineage probabilities), uniform recombination rate (the 1000-fold figure is @rodgersmelnick2015's background statement; their measurement is ~136 000 breakpoints, hotspots three- to over 100-fold), no distortion (@lu2002 at abstract level), triploid endosperm a deduction with no source checked, the gene set illustrative (@holding2008 for QPM), IBM map lengths not used and 04's "four-fold" baseline-dependence UNVERIFIED for @lee2002, no rescaling applied.
  - Conversion cost (@sec-bc-connects, 04b:1068-1209, @tbl-bc-conversion-cost, 04b:1122): the model is inbred 0/1 lines at independent biallelic markers with allele frequency 1/2 (A5 restored, A7 holds); the donor is one line common to the eight conversions and shared by descent with the recurrent parent with probability $s$ per marker, else an unrelated line matching only by state; a conversion carries donor alleles with probability $D = (1/2)^4$. Ratio of gene diversities $GD$ of eight BC3 conversions off one recurrent parent to eight unrelated lines: expectation $2D(1-D)(1-s)$ = 0.047 at $s = 0.60$ and $2D(1-D)$ = 0.117 at $s = 0$ (200 seeds: mean 0.0470, s.d. 0.0037; 0.1172, s.d. 0.0057; asserted at 4 s.e.); seed-11 draw $GD$ 0.0231 ($s = 0.60$), 0.0548 ($s = 0$), 0.4383 (unrelated), ratios 0.053 and 0.125. The diagonal of $\theta$ scales both $GD$ by the same factor. A conversion adds $k$ loci to an elite line and makes it a near-copy of the recurrent parent; whether converted lines enter a pool is a breeding decision outside the chapter (A3). The measurement is a ratio of gene diversities, "in expectation and over replicate draws"; no optimiser is run or bounded (04b:1094-1099). Consequences limited to what was measured: $\alpha$ of @sec-ocs is against a same-candidate-set random reference (@sec-baseline), so conversions inside the set lower the $GD$ of the set and of the reference alike and the budget does not charge their cost; $\theta$ separates the conversions from one line entered eight times only by the small ratio (eight copies have $GD = 0$); recognising near-copies when the candidate set is built is a recommendation, not tested (04b:1185-1197).
  - Symbols (`90-notation.qmd:387-422`): $k$, $s$, $D$ (donor dosage, not 04's diversity retained $D$, disclaimed at 90:415-421), $\mathrm{IBD}_E$, $d$, $r$, $L$ (an interval between two targets), $P_{\text{same}}$, $q$ (per-plant probability of the wanted genotype), $n$, $N$, $n_{\text{keep}}$ (row added in the 04b review, 90:405), $P$, $\nu$.
- Assumes: inbred recurrent parent and donor-derived haplotype only (A2); donor outside the pool (A3 not met); Haldane meiosis on a 5 cM marker grid, no interference, uniform rate, no distortion, no genotyping error; coupling phase from one donor; targets on different chromosomes (independent segregation for $q$, $(1-q)^n$ and the cumulative binomial); one plant advanced per generation; $s$ = 0.60 for every statement about which criterion binds, except @tbl-bc-s0; one drag ceiling, IBD target, grid, $k$ per statement. In the last section: independent unlinked markers at allele frequency 1/2, one donor common to the conversions, inbred 0/1 coding with the unrelated panel drawn without sharing among its lines.
- Takes from: `00`: A1-A8 and @tbl-hp-stages (S1-S4) (@sec-era-programme, @sec-hp-cycle); @sec-hp-ibd (marker against pedigree $\theta$). `01`: @sec-coancestry (the kernel $\mathbf f$, $\theta$, $GD$ for the conversion panel). `04`: @sec-sizing (the four objectives, @tbl-sizing-saturation, @eq-sizing-n95, @sec-sizing-verify's junction-count deficit, SA4 Haldane/no interference, 04's closed-form understatement at $k = 5$, the IBM-expansion UNVERIFIED), 04:30 (promise to treat introgression on its own terms). `06`: @sec-baseline (the random-subset reference behind $\alpha$). `07`/`10`: `max_use`. `09`: @sec-ocs ($\alpha$ budget, the continuous `upper` cap, 09:362-366). External, not in the repo: Hospital 2001 (context only, a different problem: one gene, number of generations), Falque 2009, Holding 2008, Lu 2002 (abstract level), Rodgers-Melnick 2015, Haldane 1919 (see Owed).
- Owed:
  - -> `11b`: `11b:344-346` ("@sec-backcross is the machinery that performs [injection] ... introgression is how new material enters an elite pool without dragging the rest of the genome with it") is not contradicted outright but overreads 04b: 04b models the transfer of $k \le 4$ loci into one elite line, its last section shows the product is a near-copy of the recurrent parent (0.047 of the gene diversity of unrelated lines at $s = 0.60$, 0.117 at $s = 0$), and whether a converted line enters a pool is "a breeding decision outside this chapter" (04b:1086-1088). 11b's experiment replaces six of twenty-five lines per pool with founding-generation material (11b:348-); say that 04b adds loci, not a line (04b-C9). Also `11b:77-79` ("@sec-sizing is where linkage is modelled properly"): 04b models linkage too, with Haldane meiosis and no interference.
  - -> `index`: kept true, edited in this review: `index.qmd:116-119` item 5 now says the measurement is a ratio of gene diversities for BC3 conversions and that no optimiser is run or bounded (04b-C3), in agreement with 04b:1094-1099. Its first clause ("a panel cannot be selected back up to variation it never sampled (@sec-sizing)") is still owed from the 04 entry (04-C6). `index.qmd:188-192` (three tables and both figures cached from the predecessor driver; no full re-run compared) agrees with 04b:913-940; keep true.
  - -> `90`: (a) $d$ has two meanings in 04b: marker-interval length at @eq-bc-haldane (04b:188-194; 90:398 "interval length, cM") and distance between two targets at @eq-bc-psame (04b:243; 90:400 gives $L = d/100$ for that distance); declare the second use in the row or the note (04b-C12). (b) the local-symbol note (`90:415-421`) disclaims $s$, $D$, $N$/$n$, $L$, $\nu$, $k$ but not $q$ (per-plant probability; $q^*_k$, $q_{b,k}$ are allele frequencies elsewhere in 90), $d$ (dominance deviation $d_k$), $r$ (plots per cross in the mating block) or $P$ (a different meaning in 04's sizing block, 90:343-348); $\theta$ and $GD$ are written unsubscripted for a set of eight lines at 04b:1100-1170 where 90 has $\theta_S$ (04b-C13). Not edited here.
  - -> `92` (existing item above, `92:364`): 04b agrees that `regenerate.R` does not write any `mabc_*` file (no match for `mabc` in `book/scripts/regenerate.R`); the only writer is `inst/scripts/run_mabc.R`, and `mabc_uniform.csv`, `mabc_allocation.csv`, `mabc_bcnf2.csv`, `mabc_policies.png`, `mabc_sizing.png` come from its predecessor and were never diffed against a re-run (04b:913-940, `index.qmd:188-192`, `92-verification.qmd:309`). "The script is the single place where the expensive computations live" stays false; keep 92:309 true.
  - -> `04c` (in progress): keep true: `04c:2036` describes the MABC scans as cached (true: the six `mabc_*` tables; 04b states which are cached from the predecessor driver). -- kept true in 04c: the MABC scans are the cached example of what AlphaSimR does not need (04c:2284-2286, "unlike the marker-assisted backcrossing (MABC) scans in @sec-backcross", line moved from 04c:2036).
  - [settled in 07] -> `07` (keep true, `09`, `10` still owe their side): 07 defines `max_use` once, as the largest number of times one line may be a parent, in the preview paragraph of Finding 4 (07:531-538) and says the full treatment is in @sec-combinatorial / @sec-advanced. Remaining here -> `09`, `10`: `max_use` is in `07` and `10` only (07:531-538, 07:545-546, 07:949-951; 10:79 and later); `09:362-366` has the continuous `upper` cap, described there as the continuous analogue of the usage cap that 10 enforces; 04b cites them as such (04b:1193-1197) and says capping the near-copies of one parent jointly needs them identified as a group first, not run (04b-M11, closed). -- 09 side settled in 09: `max_use` defined in the fitness paragraph (09:96-106), `upper` = `max_use`/(2n) and "same in kind, not the same constraint" (09:717-730); 10 still owes its side.
  - -> `04b` itself, non-decisive, next touch (04b-M12): the caption of @tbl-bc-dragcmp (04b:869) says "drag in cM at BC3 of the programme above", which invites a match with @tbl-bc-traj (04b:830) but the two use different meiosis seeds (6 and 7): `rec_drag` BC3 drag is 60.0 cM in one and 45 cM in the other (`rec_bg` 70). Both captions state their seed and "one replicate, illustrative", so no claim is wrong; the shared label "the programme" is loose.

Genet. 8:299-309 (04b:195-199). The paper files are not in `reference/`.

Editing the qmd forces re-execution (@tbl-bc-s0 runs 6 x 500 programmes live); refresh `book/_freeze/04b-backcross-introgression`, `docs/`, then `Rscript book/scripts/make_downloads.R`.
  - Inbound `@sec-` citations of 04b (self-citations discarded; first continuity count): `sec-backcross` 7 (`00:746`, `04:30`, `04c:2036`, `11b:345`, `91:63`, `90:414`, `index:118`), `sec-bc-running` 2 (`index:191`, `92:309`); `sec-bc-index`, `sec-bc-assumptions`, `sec-bc-model`, `sec-bc-fixation`, `sec-bc-connects` are cited only from 04b itself. `04e`, `04f`, `04g`, `10`, `10b`, `12` do not cite it; no chapter depends on 04b's numbers (none quotes one).


## 04c-alphasimr-pipeline -- Simulating a hybrid breeding programme: AlphaSimR from the ground up
- Question: how a two-pool hybrid programme (DH lines, testcross against the other pool, reciprocal recurrent selection, a commercial single cross) is built from AlphaSimR's primitives, one measured step at a time (Steps 1-7), then assembled (Step 8) into RRS replicated against a paired no-selection control, with the stage-1 contract coming out of it so that `stage2_select()` runs on it unchanged (04c:46-70, 105-111). It follows 04 and 04b by returning to the contract of @sec-simulating-crosses and replacing its generator; the two chapters between (04, 04b) run on the package's own map-and-meiosis simulators, not AlphaSimR, and are not prerequisites (04c:46-55); the programme is the one of @sec-hybrid-programme (04c:64-66). It closes the Simulation part and hands on to @sec-genomic-era (04c:2331-2333).
- Establishes:
  - Founder engines (Step 1, 04c:113-330): `quickHaplo()` is exactly reproducible, with adjacent-site $r^2$ on the $1/(n-1)$ sampling floor (no founder LD); `runMacs()` carries local LD (adjacent $r^2$ a multiple of the floor, overall barely above it; @tbl-alphasimr-ld, 04c:259-295). Reproducibility is stated at its measured scope only: ONE pair of default-threaded `runMacs()` calls per render (@tbl-alphasimr-engines, 04c:162-216), read inline as `differed` or `identical`, with `.Random.seed` asserted identical after both calls; NOT established: a rate for `runMacs()`, anything on `runMacs2()` (never called in Step 1, used in Step 8), a mechanism (`nThreads` is a labelled untested hypothesis, 04c:212-216). The three rules hold either way (callout, 04c:221-251): found once and branch both arms; `set.seed()` immediately before each founder draw; `saveRDS` what cannot be reseeded. Policy: `quickHaplo(inbred = TRUE)` for Steps 2-7 and all of @sec-programme; `runMacs2(split = )` in Step 8, every result replicated over founder draws, verdicts near a critical `t` written as the render's (04c:313-330).
  - The trait model (Step 2, 04c:331-453): AlphaSimR's `TraitAD` genetic value, @eq-alphasimr-gv (04c:381), on the 0/1/2 dosage ($\mathbf M^H$, not $\mathbf X$); $d_k$ is the dominance EFFECT (a property of the locus, @sec-heterosis-dk-meaning), not Fisher's dominance deviation, which `varD`/`genicVarD` measure (04c:392-396); $\delta_k = d_k/|a_k|$ drawn from $N(\texttt{meanDD}, \texttt{varDD})$, positive mean = directional dominance (04c:409-420); every segregating site a QTL, so there is no marker-QTL gap (04c:431-436). The `reps` argument of `setPheno()` is the locations idiom (04c:448-453).
  - Variance components (Step 3, 04c:455-607): realised, not requested, heritability; `varA/genicVarA` on inbred founders splits into inbreeding (exactly 2 at linkage equilibrium only if $d_k(q_k - p_k) = 0$, close to 2 otherwise) and gametic-phase disequilibrium (04c:534-554); `varD` on inbred founders is zero to rounding (asserted `< 1e-10`, 04c:556-565); @eq-alphasimr-mhw (04c:572) and $\alpha_k = a_k + d_k(q_k - p_k)$ asserted against `genParam()`; two definitions of "genic variance" stated (`genic_var()` observed per-locus variance, @eq-response-genic, against AlphaSimR's HW expectation; 04c:520-532).
  - Heterosis and the lottery (Step 4, 04c:609-760): the F1 of two inbred parents has `f1_sd` = 0 (asserted) and $H_{ij} = \sum_k d_k\mathbb 1[u_{ik}\ne u_{jk}]$, @eq-alphasimr-hij (04c:657), $=$ @eq-het-value on AlphaSimR's coding, asserted against `heterosis_value()`; $\bar H$ over the factorial, @eq-alphasimr-hbar (04c:689) $=$ `heterosis_expected()`; reconciled with Falconer & Mackay's $H_{F1} = \sum d_k y_k^2$ (a different mid-parent, 04c:721-729); transgressive fraction ordered by the gap in DH s.d. (04c:738-760).
  - Selfing and DH (Step 5, 04c:762-874): $\mathbb E[M_{F_t}] = \tfrac12(G_i+G_j) + H_{ij}(1/2)^{t-1}$, @eq-alphasimr-selfing (04c:785), $t$ counting from the F1 ($t=1$; 00's superscript counts selfing generations, index shifted by one, 04c:787-790); checked on $n_{f1}$ parallel lineages at seven generations, $|z| < 4$ asserted, rows correlated, not independent (04c:846-854); DH reaches homozygosity 1.0 in one step.
  - Selection (Step 6, 04c:876-1260): breeder's equation checked with $r$ against `bv()`, not the $r$ of @eq-response-breeders unless $d = 0$, and `R_observed` is the transmitted change in genetic mean, not the genetic differential on selected hybrids that 02b checks (04c:929-940); GCA of a pool-A line against pool B, @eq-alphasimr-gca (04c:1019), $\alpha^{(B)}_k = a_k + d_k(1 - 2p_{Bk})$, with its dominance part $=$ `gca_A` of `heterosis_gca_sca()`; the two $g^A_i$ (whole GCA here, dominance part in @eq-het-gca-sca-terms) are separated (04c:1029-1034); with `quickHaplo()` frequencies at one half the dominance weights are small and per se value ranks GCA closely, a property of the founder spectrum and not of "undifferentiated halves" (04c:1059-1078); first-order accuracy @eq-alphasimr-accuracy (04c:1102) with finite-tester-pool and sample-variance corrections, tied to @eq-het-rho-k at $\sigma^2_e = 0$ and to @tbl-mating-quantities as $n_B \to \infty$ (04c:1122-1129); the Jensen term measured, not asserted, at most a few percent of the smallest accuracy (@tbl-alphasimr-jensen, 04c:1192-1231); $t$ here is the tester count (04e's/05b's $k$, renamed because $k$ indexes QTL).
  - Index on phenotypes (Step 7, 04c:1262-1412): requested $h^2$ 0.6 and 0.1 against realised 0.47 and 0.072 with the cause stated (04c:1291-1301); the default `weights = NULL` is $a_j = 1/\mathrm{sd}$ on predicted criteria and Step 7 is the phenotypic case, NOT the default (04c:1304-1326); equal weights against Smith-Hazel: trait-1 gain 4.1x smaller (0.0108 against 0.0437 per unit intensity) at 0.2 percent of attainable correlation; restricted index expected gain in trait 2 at zero (6.6e-17), a model expectation, at a cost of 15 percent of the aggregate (04c:1357-1394); `optim` agrees with Smith-Hazel to 1.1e-15 (04c:1396-1407, the chapter's own addition to @sec-response).
  - RRS assembled (Step 8, 04c:1414-2175): founders from `runMacs2(split = )`, OUTBRED, every site a QTL, `alphasimr_gd()` $= 1 - \overline{\mathbf f}$ (04c:1451-1467); the partition over the two pools is @eq-gd-partition of @sec-subdivided via `ct_partition()` on the founder individuals ($\bar f$ census-weighted over all pairs, self pairs included, no hybrid, 04c:1480-1492; $GD_{WI}$ = half a plant's observed heterozygosity), $F_{ST} = GD_{BS}/GD_T$ the statistic of @eq-f-stats. Split sweep (20 founder draws per split time, 04c:1507-1780): $F_{ST}$, $GD_{BS}$ and the divergence share of expected heterosis rise with `split`; $GD_T$ rises too, entirely from sites fixed for opposite alleles (ascertainment of the founder sites by `runMacs2()`), while $GD_T$ at every other site falls (04c:1637-1679); the dominance share of var(GCA) and the per se-GCA correlation are contrasted between split 0 and 1000 (render-dependent `t`, two of the 7 sweep tests beyond Bonferroni 3.01 in the checks; 04c:1681-1780); sweep multiplicity declared as 7 tests (2 contrasts, the `GCA_var_dom` contrast, the covariance term at 4 splits), unadjusted 2.09 called permissive for a difference and strict for "not distinguishable", ascertainment bookkeeping, `gca_only()` and the $F_{ST}$/$GD_{BS}$/$GD_T$ movements declared descriptive/exploratory (04c:1720-1736). One cycle in six steps (04c:1782-1840): testers are the first `n_testers` individuals of the CURRENT opposite pool, outbred, a different set each cycle, so the estimand is the value against outbred testers, not the inbred-B GCA of @eq-alphasimr-gca (04c:1817-1826, 2257-2264); only trait 1 enters; response is measured pre-selection and diversity post-selection (04c:1833-1840). Replicated trajectory, 48 paired founder draws, each founder set drawn once and BOTH arms branched off it, same seed before each arm, cycle 1 identical between arms (asserted), the arms' streams part at the elite pick (04c:1842-1906); paired $t$ over 11 declared tests (unadjusted 2.01, Bonferroni 2.98); every diversity conclusion exploratory (the pooled gene-diversity means are constructions); `mean_tc_A`/`mean_tc_B` are not per-pool responses (both estimate the A x B level; 04c:2080-2124). What is inferred (conditional on the render's `t`): the A x B level responds to RRS; whether it spends within-pool diversity faster than the bottleneck alone is exploratory; `GD_BS` is read as a divergence statistic with 05b's conditions (flat $d$, inbred parents, complete factorial, equal weights) NOT met (here $d_k = \delta_k|a_k|$ varies, pools outbred), "not a measure of heterosis" (04c:2131-2170, 2224-2235).
  - The control is a separation of selection from the bottleneck WITHIN this design; it is not a replication of another chapter's control: @sec-cycles has a budget $\alpha$ against the frozen base and no random-selection arm, and @sec-programme measures against the programme's own start "rather than against a random-selection baseline of the same size" (04c:2152-2158); Step 8 has no diversity budget and cross-checks nothing of 11b's $\alpha$ result (04c:2316-2324).
  - Stage-2 passthrough (04c:2177-2201): `alphasimr_pipeline()` and `alphasimr_to_stage1()` give the `{X, f, hybrids, fL}` contract (joint $\mathbf f^L$ over both pools' elite lines, one numbering); `stage2_select()` runs unchanged on the phenotypic trait columns (`hybrid_pop@pheno`, one plot); the attainable $\alpha$ ceiling can be at or below zero at this size, so `DE_alpha` rows coincide with `ref_truncation`.
  - Scale and limits (04c:2237-2303): Step 8 runs live at 12 + 12 founders, 5 x 150 sites, 4 cycles, 3 DH per parent, 6 elites, 2 testers, 48 paired draws (04c:5-13), its own scale, not `book_cfg` (25 + 25, 2000 markers) nor `rrs_config`'s default; Steps 2-6 use one founder set, Step 7 a separate additive draw; no GxE (@sec-programme adds it); DH instantaneous; the split is a stylised divergence, not calibrated to a named pool pair; "book scale" is gone. The heavier driver is `inst/scripts/run_alphasimr.R` (04c:2290-2299).
  - Symbols (`90-notation.qmd:545-567`, local to @sec-alphasimr): $x_{ik}$ (0/1/2 dosage), $G_i$/$G_{ij}$ (not the Caballero-Toro $G_i$), $\mu_0$, $\delta_k$, $M_{HW}$/$M_{F_t}$, $\bar H$ ($\mu$ of 05b renamed), $\alpha_k$/$\alpha^{(B)}_k$ (average effect, NOT the diversity budget $\alpha$), $\hat g^A_i$, $t$/$r$/$\rho(t, r)$ ($r$ the accuracy against `bv()` only in the breeder's-equation subsection, 90:561), $v_g$/$v_s$/$v_e$, $n_A$/$n_B$.
- Assumes: Steps 2-6: inbred `quickHaplo()` founders, every site a QTL, directional dominance at the requested `meanDD`/`varDD`, "pools" = two undifferentiated halves of one founder set (no divergence), one phenotyping environment, independent loci in the closed forms (HW and linkage equilibrium for @eq-alphasimr-mhw and genic variance), AlphaSimR's real map and meiosis for descendants. Step 7: a separate ADDITIVE founder draw, phenotypic records, requested against realised heritabilities differ. Step 8: OUTBRED `runMacs2()` founders (stylised split, equal-size demes), DH lines fully inbred, testers outbred and chosen by index, one location, selection on trait 1 only, measured against the same run's cycle 0 and the paired random-elite control, not a random-subset null of the same size (so not the $\alpha$ of 09/10); no founder repeatability assumed (not established for `runMacs2()`). Every verdict near a critical $t$ conditional on the render; the 11 paired tests and the 7 sweep tests each Bonferroni-reported, the diversity conclusions exploratory.

## 05-genomic-era -- Diversity metrics in the genomic era
- Question: which inbreeding lens a diversity constraint pins, and what a programme must measure to see the lens it does not pin: $F_{hom}$ (loss of heterozygosity against a frozen base, @eq-era-fhom) and $F_{drift}$ (squared frequency displacement, @eq-era-fdrift) are the same quantity under pure drift and differ by the covariance @eq-era-cov when management itself moves frequencies (@meuwissen2020's result, reported and reproduced on the book's data), followed by a seven-step monitoring protocol (@sec-era-protocol, 05:833-2051) with Call / Check / Warning sign / Threshold per step. It opens the part "Metrics and prediction" and follows 04c, which closes Simulation: the opening names what it takes from the Simulation part (the lines and hybrids of `simulate_lines()` / `simulate_data()`, @sec-simulating-crosses) and writes the one drift model it adds, `pool_traj()` (05:12-17); says the F4 panels of @sec-sizing, the backcross populations of @sec-backcross and the AlphaSimR cycles of @sec-alphasimr are NOT measured in this chapter (they depart from A2 and A5) and that A1-A8 are this chapter's list, which @sec-sizing and @sec-backcross map their departures onto and @sec-simulating-crosses and @sec-alphasimr do not use (05:17-22). 04c's hand-over (04c:2331-2333) is true of this opening.
- Establishes:
  - A1-A8 as this chapter's list (@sec-era-programme, 05:47-156): A1 allogamous, F1 product; A2 fully inbred parents (asserted, @tbl-era-inbred-check: line heterozygosity 0, line self-coancestry 1, F1 heterozygous fraction 0.3617 $= 2\,GD_{WI\text{-}hyb}$, 05:62-87); A3 recycling within pool, `run_cycles()` only "in reduced form" (no trials, no S3, index known without error; Comstock et al. 1949 cited for existence and title only, 05:89-100); A4 discrete cycles, overlap neither modelled nor measured and no later chapter treats it, "a limit of every rate reported here" (05:102-107); A5 biallelic 0/0.5/1 markers, neutrality stated per simulator and per row (three cases, 05:109-126), marker panel = causal set wherever `simulate_data()` is the source, the marker-versus-QTL gap "not modelled and not measured here" (05:128-133), linkage equilibrium in the founding draw only (05:135-139); A6 frozen base `ctx$p0` (05:141-143); A7 no dominance in any metric (05:145-148); A8 teaching scale 25 + 25 lines, 2000 markers, N hybrids, with the two other scales named and not run (05:150-156).
  - Definitions (05:191-501): $\Delta F = (F_t - F_{t-1})/(1 - F_{t-1})$, $N_e = 1/(2\Delta F)$, @eq-era-rate (05:198), and $\Delta F = 1 - e^{\text{slope}}$, $-\text{slope}$ only its first-order approximation (05:205-207, 1462-1466); $F_{hom}$ (@eq-era-fhom, 05:230) summed over markers polymorphic in the base, can be negative (05:243-246), computed by `freq_metrics()` on the MERGED hybrid frequencies $\bar p = (\tilde p_A + \tilde p_B)/2$, so its $He$ column is $GD_T = 1 - \bar f$ of @eq-gd-partition identically (asserted), NOT the heterozygosity of the product, which is $2\,GD_{WI\text{-}hyb} = GD_T + GD_{BS}$ on the full factorial and differs from it by at most the printed amount on a selected subset (@tbl-era-hom-two-levels, @tbl-era-hom-two-levels-het, 05:254-340); $F_{drift}$ (@eq-era-fdrift, 05:450) never negative, "the algebraic form of the standardised-variance definition of $F_{ST}$" and the same form only in time not in space, exactly what `G_VR2` constrains (05:448-467); $F_{drift}$ not to be read as this programme's selection intensity (05:473-495). The inbreeding-depression statement $ID = 2F\sum_k d_k p_k q_k$ is for the segregating crossing block and uses an $F$ against current frequencies, not the $F_t$ of the two lenses (05:416-446).
  - The central identity, @eq-era-cov (05:512-514): $F_{hom} - F_{drift} = 2\,\mathrm{cov}(\delta p/\sqrt{p_0(1-p_0)},\, (p_0 - 1/2)/\sqrt{p_0(1-p_0)})$, covariance with divisor $m$ (`2 * mean(x * y)`, not `stats::cov()`), `cov_diag` IS the gap (asserted to machine precision, @tbl-era-identity-residual; all three lenses read 0 on the whole candidate set, @tbl-era-base-zero, 05:530-579). Sign by matrix (@tbl-era-matrix-signs, 05:581-600). The MAF-spectrum callout (05:601-671): under a rule $\delta p = c(0.5 - p_0)$, $F_{drift} = c^2\kappa$ and `cov_diag` $= -2c\kappa$ exactly, $\kappa = \overline{(p_0 - 1/2)^2/[p_0(1-p_0)]}$; $\kappa$ 1.27 for the book's panel against 0.64 for the ancestral $U(0.05, 0.95)$ draw alone; the WGS-versus-chip comparison is an inference from the algebra, not a result of Meuwissen et al.'s experiment, and the plant translation is a stated expectation.
  - Reported, not reproduced (05:673-831): `G_LA`, `G_ROH`, the seven matrices side by side (@tbl-era-side-by-side), the Panel N results of Meuwissen et al.'s Tables 2 and 3 (@tbl-era-reference-results, `gain_per_dF` derived here and NOT their device; at equal averaged inbreeding `G_LA` beat $\mathbf A$ in 65 of 100 replicates, `G_ROH` in 62; their Results text says 40% overshoot on both rates, their Table 2 does not give two equal values).
  - Protocol, one claim per step (05:833-2051). Step 1: on the full factorial, and on any set with uniform line usage within each pool (complete with unequal pool sizes, balanced incomplete NCII shape), $\mathtt{colMeans}(X) = (p_A + p_B)/2$ exactly; a base rebuilt from the selected hybrids makes both lenses IDENTICALLY zero (@tbl-era-rebase); `range()` over a stored history cannot detect it, the per-cycle comparison with cycle 0 can; `build_ctx()` has no `p0` argument, so overwrite `ctx$p0` and `ctx$maf_pop` as `cycle_ctx()` does (05:838-967). Step 2 (05:969-1127): both lenses in one `freq_metrics()` sweep; the max-diversity plan (`w = 0`, `G_0.5` in miniature) drives $F_{hom}$ negative (seed 1 z = -4.79) and `cov_diag` strongly negative but pays no drift excess when optimised and read on the same full panel (seed 1 $F_{drift}$ z = -0.67; over 20 founder draws -1.6 to 1.5, above 0 on 11, above 2 on 0; $F_{hom}$ z -6.3 to -3.6); a single `random` row is no control (null s.d. against a value of the same order); the implied pull of this plan toward 0.5 is 0.013 against the stipulated 0.05 of Step 4; `w = 1` returns the truncation set exactly, the index s.d. being three orders of magnitude above the s.d. of $\theta$ across subsets. Step 3: report `cov_diag` and its sign (05:1129-1153). Step 4 (05:1155-1483): the drift model of @eq-hp-drift calibrates the $\log(1 - F)$ regression, $\Delta F = 1/n_{gam}$, crossing block $N_e = n$, DH pool $N_e = n/2$ (structural factor 2); with no selection at all $F_{drift}$ climbs, so it is not an accumulated intensity; the stipulated pull of 0.05 toward 0.5 makes the $F_{drift}$ fit non-constant ($R^2 < 0.995$) and the $F_{hom}$ fit return a negative rate; thresholds $R^2 \ge 0.999$ (neutral, minimum 0.99935, margin 0.00035) and $< 0.995$ (managed, maximum 0.99243, margin 0.00257) set on the data they are read on, one seed family, teaching scale only; the deviation statistic over 20 seed families exceeds the Bonferroni z (3.08) in 4 of 20, so the bound is a description, not a test. Step 5 (05:1485-1651): audit on a half panel never seen by the plan; the $F_{hom}$ gap of the max-diversity plan is 5.37 s.d. of the random-subset gap on seed 1 (2.28 to 6.38 over 20 draws), managed-half $F_{hom} < 0$ on 20 of 20, audit-half $F_{hom} > 0$ on 16 of 20; the `index` row is a selection response on the audit loci (audit-half $F_{drift}$ z 33.4, 23.0 to 34.3), not a noise reference; an UNEXPLAINED finding reported as such: on half panels the max-diversity plan has $F_{drift}$ z above 0 on 19 of 20 draws on both halves (above 2 on 16 managed, 17 audit), unlike the full panel of Step 2. Step 6 (05:1653-1809): pool-level reading; truncation costs 4.1% of gene diversity against `null60$gd_ref` and drives $F_{ST}$ and $GD_{BS}$ UP while $GD_T$ falls (a one-pool collapse and a divergence are indistinguishable in $F_{ST}$; by the algebra of the step, one selection on one draw, frequency across germplasm not measured); `GD`, `GD_T` and $He$ one number on this germplasm; the chapter's $F_{ST} = GD_{BS}/GD_T$ = Nei $G_{ST}$, `Fst_Wright` the code's between-referenced statistic (1.81 times larger), $F_{BN}$ the generating parameter, each stated once here (@tbl-era-s1-identity, 05:1772-1787); a per-line usage cap (`max_use = 4`) is the direct instrument. Step 7 (05:1841-2051): the matrix to invert, @eq-era-rank $\mathrm{rank}(\mathbf f) \le \mathrm{rank}(\mathbf B)$ for any selection and any line genotypes (a theorem, 05:1917-1941), with equality a measurement (@tbl-era-rank, 5 selections and `n_draw` random 20-hybrid draws, one scale), rank(f) = $L - 1$ on the full factorial, centring costing exactly one dimension of VanRaden's $\mathbf G$ and `G_0.5` $= 4\mathbf f - 2\mathbf J$ inheriting its rank from $\mathbf f$; the book's DE and `metrics_cheap()` never invert $\mathbf f$, the QP routes add `diag(1e-8, n)`, effect on the optimum not measured.
  - What not to do (05:2053-2107) labels each recommendation as measured here or as Meuwissen et al.'s argument; the transposition limits (@sec-era-transposition, 05:2109-2139); the operational checklist (05:2141-2167).
  - Symbols in 90: rows added by the 05 review for $\tilde p_A$, $\tilde p_B$, $\bar p$, $G_{ST}$, $\varepsilon$ (ridge, 0.01 in Meuwissen et al., written $\alpha$ there; distinct from `eps` of `freq_metrics()`), and the note that $m$ is $N_{SNP}$ there. $\alpha$ keeps its diversity-loss meaning (05:489-490, 1849).
- Assumes: A1-A8 above; the figures are one founder draw (`simulate_data(book_cfg)`, seed 1) unless a table says twenty (data seeds 1-20, null of 300 subsets, `set.seed(7)` per draw) or the null of 1000; hybrid subsets of size 60; teaching scale 25 + 25 lines, 2000 markers; marker = QTL, linkage equilibrium within pool in the founding draw, which makes Step 5 a best case; $\tilde p_A$, $\tilde p_B$ weighted by the usage a selection induces; no dominance in any metric; discrete cycles; Step 4 thresholds at 25-line scale, `n_rep` replicates, one seed family; nothing is run at production scale or on real germplasm, and the chapter says so. The Meuwissen et al. numbers are animal-breeding Panel N values, transcribed and reported, not reproduced.
- Takes from: `00`: @sec-hybrid-programme, @sec-hp-cycle (A3), @eq-hp-drift ($\Delta F = 1/n_{gam}$), the 0.3617. `01`: @sec-coancestry ($\mathbf f$, $GD = 1 - \theta$), the promise (01:487-491) that the $F_{hom}$/$F_{drift}$ gap is measured where reported. `02`: @sec-subdivided, @eq-gd-partition, @eq-f-stats (the $F_{ST}$ naming of 02:348-427). `02b`: @sec-response (the true genetic variance item of the checklist). `03`: @sec-simulating-crosses (the generator, $\tilde f$, `ctx$p0` as the mean of the pools' sampled line frequencies, `fst` as $F_{BN}$). `04`/`04b`/`04c`: @sec-sizing, @sec-backcross, @sec-alphasimr, named as not measured here. `05b`: @sec-heterosis (A7, @eq-het-wi-hyb in @sec-heterosis-hybrid-identity, @sec-heterosis-id, @sec-heterosis-gca-sca as the model for citing Comstock at title only, the $d_k$ name). By forward pointer (declared as such): `06` @sec-baseline, @sec-catalogue, @sec-catalogue-null; `08` @sec-stages (Traps 2, 3); `08b` @sec-g-singular; `09` @sec-ocs; `11b` @sec-cycles; `12` @sec-case-study. External, none in the repo: Meuwissen et al. 2020 (read in PDF for the sentences quoted), Holsinger & Weir 2009 (read, supports the "several other quantities" sentence), Caballero & Toro 2002, Nei 1973, VanRaden 2008 (form of $\mathbf G$ only; abstract-level support), Liu et al. 2010, Wood et al. 2014; UNVERIFIED: Wright 1922 ("this book has not read", 05:221-223), Falconer & Mackay 1996 locators (Ch. 3-4, 3, 7, 14; 05:203, 226, 421, 489), Toro 2014 ($\mathbf G_{0.5}$ attribution, only via Meuwissen et al.), Comstock et al. 1949 (title only).
- Owed:
  - -> user (OPEN, PENDING by the user's declaration): Comstock et al. (1949) is cited in A3 for existence and title only, "as @sec-heterosis-gca-sca does", nothing of it attributed (05:92-95). Same item as in the 00, 05b and 04c entries; the user decides whether the paper is read.
  - -> `11b`: $\Delta F$ read as $-$slope and $N_e = 1/(-2\,\text{slope})$ in the chunk at 11b:238-243, against the $1 - e^{\text{slope}}$ of 05:205-207 and 05:1462-1466 (00-C19, 05-R16). Nothing to change in 05 (the older locators 05:1045-1050, 05:1850 in the 00 entry are stale). The first-order error is stated by 05 as about $\Delta F/2$ relative.
  - -> `11b`: a half-panel audit across cycles (05-C5), and -> `11c`: overlapping cycles and line reuse (05-C6). 05 no longer points to either; it says no cycle-by-cycle audit is run anywhere in the book (05:1637-1639) and that no later chapter treats overlap (05:105-107). Both are owed only if wanted; if 11b or 11c adds one, those 05 sentences become false.
  - [settled in 08b] -> `08b`: cite @eq-era-rank (05:1935) in @sec-g-singular instead of rederiving the span/rank argument (08b:208-250: rank($\mathbf G$) = $L - 2$ against the $L - 1$ of $\mathbf f$ here, consistent; 05-C15). 05 owns it; 08b should cross-reference. -- settled in 08b: @sec-g-singular cites @eq-era-rank and carries the argument over to the centred $\mathbf G$ with one more step, states $L - 2$ as a bound and measures its attainment (08b:287-298, @tbl-genpred-rank).

If a later chapter or edit explains it, 05:1076-1078 and 05:1610-1612 must be reconciled.
  - -> `R/00_data.R` / `simulate_data()` (code, quant-Rwrite or the user): `ctx$traits` carry no per-marker effects, so the index used in Step 5 cannot be rebuilt from management-half effects only; the index row is therefore labelled a selection response on the audit loci (05:1492-1498, 1614-1620) and the cleaner design (index selected on the managed half alone) is not run.
  - -> bibliography: `wright1951` (references.bib:705) is cited by no chapter after this review (the text says no paper of Wright's is cited, 05:459); not deleted; its metadata notes (`number = {1}` against issue 4, a 1949 DOI against year 1951, 00-B13) stay for the bib pass.
  - UNVERIFIED sources, non-decisive (no claim rests on them): Wright 1951 and 1922 (05:221-223), Falconer & Mackay locators (Ch. 3-4, 3, 7, 14), Toro 2014 on $\mathbf G_{0.5}$ (05:165, via Meuwissen et al.), VanRaden 2008 wording on base frequencies (05:1861-1863, abstract only). The $ID = 2F\sum d_k p_k q_k$ formula is verified in 05b, not in Falconer.

Counts quoted from `era-full-panel-20`, `tbl-era-audit-null` and `tbl-era-seeds-z` are live and seeded, so a render reproduces them.

## 06-metric-catalogue -- A catalogue of diversity metrics
- Question: where the catalogue of diversity metrics is defined once, where the baseline of the random-subset null is derived (@sec-baseline), and where every metric, the two lenses of 05 included, is read against that null (@sec-catalogue-null); and what "no loss" means. It follows 05 because 05 separated $F_{hom}$ and $F_{drift}$ and read both against a resampled null before that null had been defined; the opening says so and names the three things this chapter does (06:12-17). It keeps four inputs apart under one signature `f(idx, ctx) -> scalar` (06:19-37). It hands on to @sec-choosing (06:312, 989-990), which asks which of the catalogue to keep (07:11).
- Establishes:
  - Four inputs, not one: the molecular coancestry `ctx[["f"]]` (`theta_group()`, `gene_diversity()`, `status_number()`, `mean_offdiag()`, `ene()`, `ane()`, `eff_dim()`, and `GD_WI_hyb` of `gd_partition()`); marker frequencies with the frozen base `ctx[["p0"]]` (`theta_from_freq()`, `he_nei()`, `alleles_lost()`, $F_{hom}$, $F_{drift}$); the pedigree (`ne_parents()`, `ne_lines_pool()`); the line coancestry `fL` weighted by usage (`theta_pools()`, rest of `gd_partition()`). Only the first group reads the hybrid-level $\mathbf f$ (06:19-37).
  - Group coancestry $\theta_S$ over all $n^2$ elements, diagonal included (@eq-catalogue-theta, 06:46-47), the split $\theta_S = \bar f_d/n + (n-1)\bar f_o/n$ (@eq-catalogue-split, 06:58; asserted on five selections); the off-diagonal mean ranks like $\theta_S$ on five selections (Spearman printed, "a check of the ordering, not an estimate", 06:92-94) but has no interpretable scale and no defensible baseline.
  - $\theta_S = m^{-1}\sum_k(\bar p_k^2 + \bar q_k^2)$ (@eq-catalogue-theta-freq, 06:115), so $GD_S = 1 - \theta_S = H_e$ of @eq-hp-gd, Nei's gene diversity in plug-in form (no $2n/(2n-1)$ correction); one quantity written two ways, asserted with `all.equal()` (06:117-140). Matrix route $O(n^2)$, frequency route $O(nm)$, timed once (@tbl-catalogue-timing, 06:148-170).
  - $N_s = 1/(2\theta_S)$ (@eq-catalogue-ns, 06:179); on molecular $\mathbf f$ (identity in state, floor set by the marker frequencies) $\theta_S \in [1/2, 1]$ and $N_s \in [1/2, 1]$, a monotone rescaling and not a head count (06:181-191, @tbl-catalogue-ns-edge); a descriptor of a set, not a drift projection, against the rate-based $N_e$ of 05 (callout, 06:208-223; Toro et al. 2020 on the abstract only).
  - $GD$ and $N_s$ describe the expected heterozygosity of the merged set, NOT the heterozygosity the F1s carry: expected F1 heterozygosity of the complete factorial is $GD_T + GD_{BS}$ (@eq-het-theta-ab), that of the pairs kept is $2\,GD_{WI\text{-}hyb}$, and a selection can move the two against each other (06:225-245; one draw, $GD$ 0.3259 to 0.3124 against `GD_WI_hyb` 0.1814 to 0.1823, random subset to truncation; 05's twenty draws show no consistent sign, @tbl-era-seeds-z). $GD$ is not a measure of next season's hybrid vigour.
  - $N_{e,par} = 1/\sum_i p_i^2$ (@eq-catalogue-ne-par, 06:253), bounded by the number of lines; $A_{lost}$ (@eq-catalogue-alleles-lost, 06:293), MAF threshold 0.05; "irreversible" only for a closed population (06:284-289). Lopez-Cortegano et al. (2019) reported for direction, not transfer (multiallelic haplotypes against biallelic markers): maximising $A_T$ retained more alleles and controlled molecular inbreeding better than maximising $H_T$ except at the all-within weight; the Fernandez et al. (2004) mechanism for the undivided case "as reported there"; no mechanism of this chapter's own for the subdivided case (06:330-359).
  - Redundancy analysis of @sec-choosing is CACHED EXTERNAL DATA, `metric_redundancy.csv`, no producer in this repository, never reproducible here (06:312-319, 06:413-415): Spearman with $\theta$ 0.932 (`alleles_lost_MAF`), -0.925 (`Ne_parents`), -0.967 (`eff_dim`); it does not include $F_{drift}$ or $F_{hom}$ (06:319-328).
  - E-NE and A-NE with $\eta_{ij} = 1 - f_{ij}$ (@eq-catalogue-ene-ane, 06:371), lower A-NE is better; `ane()` keeps the self-distance $1 - f_{ii} > 0$ against Odong et al. 2013's 0 so that every z-score and every optimisation chapter share one definition (06:404-415, ranking of five selections unchanged, asserted gap $\ge 0$).
  - $D_{eff} = (\sum\gamma_i)^2 / \sum\gamma_i^2$ on the DOUBLE-CENTERED submatrix, at most $n - 1$ (@eq-catalogue-deff, 06:421-429); an uncentered version re-measures $\theta$ (Rayleigh bound asserted, 06:449-456). On the chapter's random subset the package value is 26.19 and the uncentered ratio 1.47 (06:458-460).
  - $\theta_A, \theta_B, \theta_{AB}$ (@eq-catalogue-theta-pools, 06:482-484, usage weights from `line_weights()`); $GD_{WI} = 0$ exactly with inbred parents (asserted, 06:508-513), $GD_{BS}$ rises under truncation; the rise is the usage-concentration effect of @sec-era-step6 and not complementarity (the index is additive, $R^2 = 1$ asserted, 06:519-533). Share of the rise reproduced by concentration alone depends on the control arm: 81 % at 5 x 12 lines (effective numbers of lines, rounded up), 49 % at 7 x 17, 27 % at the 10 x 23 distinct lines of truncation (chunk `gdbs-matching`, seed 5, 300 draws; the last is a less matched control, $N_{e,lines}$ 4.5 and 11.8; the right control is NOT settled; one draw, one subset size; the remainder, selection on the index or unequal usage, is not isolated) (06:567-615).
  - Divergence under dominance: with inbred parents, complete factorial and flat $d_k \equiv 1$ the divergence term of expected heterosis is $GD_{BS}$ itself, otherwise the $d$-weighted version (06:472-479). @eq-catalogue-het-hs-dg: $p_A + p_B - 2p_Ap_B = H_T + D_G = H_S + 2D_G$ per locus (06:675), cited from @eq-het-split and not re-derived; $1 - \theta_{AB} = GD_T + GD_{BS}$ asserted on five selections, realised F1 heterozygosity $2\,GD_{WI\text{-}hyb}$ differs by at most 0.0043 (06:705-743). $H_\lambda = D_G + \lambda H_S$ (@eq-catalogue-lambda, 06:647; $D_G$ is Lopez-Cortegano's symbol; reading the two subpopulations as the two pools is this book's analogy). $\lambda = 1/2$ at flat $d$, $\lambda_d = \bar d_H/(2\bar d_D)$ otherwise (@eq-catalogue-lambda-d, 06:683-685), identity asserted to 1e-12 and equal to `heterosis_expected()`/$m$; $\lambda_d$ 0.505-0.512 for gamma(2, 4) $d_k$ (seed 8); a plan-local weight (06:665-782). Lopez-Cortegano's $\lambda$ values (1 mainly, 0, 0.5, 1000) and the 0.5 of Bennewitz & Meuwissen (2006), second-hand, "not read here"; the coincidence with $\lambda_d = 1/2$ is stated and the relation not examined (06:698-704).
  - The baseline: $\mathbb E[\theta_S] - \theta_N = (1/n - 1/N)(\bar f_d - \bar f_o) > 0$ for random subsets without replacement (@eq-catalogue-bias, 06:792; predicted 2.222e-3, observed 2.277e-3, Monte Carlo s.e. 4.1e-5, asserted within 4 s.e.); $\alpha = (GD_{ref} - GD_S)/GD_{ref}$, $GD_{ref}$ the mean $GD$ of random subsets of the same size (@eq-catalogue-alpha, 06:842-845; `gd_ref`). Baseline of the teaching pool ($n$ = 60, $N$ = 625, `B` = 2000, seed 7): `GD_population` 0.32809, `GD_ref` 0.32586, s.d. 7.58e-4, `gap_se` 1.70e-5, apparent loss 0.680 % if the whole pool is used as baseline, "entirely a sampling artefact" (@tbl-catalogue-gd-baseline, 06:851-877). The off-diagonal mean is unbiased for the population's but omits $\bar f_d/n$ (06:829-835).
  - How to set the $\alpha$ limit: truncation's $\alpha$ is the one at which the constraint stops binding; at the teaching scale 4.129 % (0.04128766), so a 5 % limit would be inoperative; the value at the production scale (71 + 71 lines, 25000 markers, A8 of @sec-era-programme) and at the `sim_config` default (50 + 50, 5000) is not computed here and does not transfer; the frontier is @sec-ocs, the recipe @sec-combinatorial (06:888-912).
  - Every metric against the null (@tbl-catalogue-z, 06:914-951): null of `null_distribution(ctx, 60, B = 2000, B_full = 1000, seed = 7)`, the same call as 05's @tbl-era-signature-z (truncation: GD z -17.06, $F_{hom}$ 9.41, $F_{drift}$ 35.08, `alleles_lost` 14.93; capped truncation: GD -1.36, ANE 16.41); relative s.e. of each s.d. $1/\sqrt{2(B_{full}-1)}$ = 2.2 %, normality of the null NOT checked. Over twenty founder draws (@tbl-catalogue-seeds-z, `B` = 200, `B_full` = 300, noisier): capped-truncation GD $|z| > 2$ on 12 of 20, ANE $z > 2$ on 20 of 20, $|z_{F_{drift}}| > |z_{GD}|$ for truncation on 20 of 20, $|z_{N_{e,par}}| < |z_{GD}|$ on 18 of 20; the robust statement is the GD/ANE contrast, which @sec-choosing turns into a recommendation (06:954-990).
- Assumes: the teaching scale throughout, 25 + 25 lines, 2000 markers, $N$ = 625 hybrids, $n$ = 60, simulation seed 1, five named selections (`random` seed 42, `trunc_cap` line cap 4, `greedy_w` $\omega$ = 0.001, `greedy_div`, `truncation`), under `stopifnot()`; one founder draw except @tbl-catalogue-seeds-z; biallelic 0/0.5/1 markers, A1-A8 of @sec-era-programme (A2 inbred parents is what makes $GD_{WI} = 0$ and the $GD_T + GD_{BS}$ identity exact); $\mathbf f$ identity in state; the baseline is random subsets of the SAME size, not the pool; the heterosis readings (@eq-catalogue-het-hs-dg, $\lambda_d$) need inbred parents, the complete factorial at usage weights and equal pool weight, and $\lambda_d$ also $\bar d_D > 0$, $\bar d_H \ge 0$; no dominance in the simulated index, so $GD_{BS}$ here is a partition component and not a heterosis measure; frozen base `p0`; normality of the null not checked; the production scale is not run.
- Takes from: `00`: @eq-hp-gd ($GD_S = 1 - \theta_S$), @eq-hp-ibs-ibd, @sec-hybrid-programme. `01`: @sec-coancestry (the convention, $\mathbf f$ against a centred matrix), @eq-f-molecular. `02`: @sec-subdivided, the partition $GD_T = GD_{WI} + GD_{BI} + GD_{BS}$ and $F_{ST}$ (Caballero & Toro 2002). `03`: @sec-simulating-crosses (additive index, `simulate_lines()`), @tbl-gd-baseline (the same baseline example on its own 400 subsets: 0.681 %). `05`: @sec-genomic-era ($F_{hom}$, $F_{drift}$, the null, @eq-era-rate, @tbl-era-signature-z, @tbl-era-seeds-z), @sec-era-step6 (usage-concentration algebra), @sec-era-programme (A8, the two scales), @tbl-era-hom-two-levels-het (`F1_het`). `05b`: @eq-het-theta-ab, @eq-het-split, @sec-heterosis (what $GD_{BS}$ buys, `heterosis_expected()`). `07`: the cached redundancy analysis (read, not reproduced).
- Owed:
  - [settled in 10b, 10b:1829-1832] -> `10b`: `10b:1245` says "@sec-catalogue's rule applies: one diversity lens, chosen deliberately, reported against a null"; 06 states no such rule (06-C13). Either state it in 06 or reword 10b; 06 is closed, so reword 10b.
  - [settled in 07] -> `07`: the cached `metric_redundancy.csv` `eff_dim` column has mean 1.486 (range 1.438-1.506) against the package's double-centered 26.19 and the uncentered ratio 1.47 on 06's random subset (06:458-467); 06 reads the column as uncentered ("a reading of the numbers, not a verified fact"; the cache was built at 12000 markers, 06 runs at 2000). 07 should say which form the cache used or recompute it with the package. The csv has no producer in the repository (06:312-319, 413-415): cached external data, never to be called reproducible; the same gap leaves open which definition of A-NE it used. 07's own figures (07:199-215: allelic richness 52 %, $N_{e,par}$ 36 % of rank s.d.) are used by 06 without typed numbers and stay for 07 to keep true. -- settled in 07: the `eff_dim` column (mean 1.486, range 1.438-1.506) is read as the UNCENTRED ratio and every claim about `eff_dim` is restricted to that form, "not the package's `eff_dim()`" (07:376-384, 07:403, 07:1012-1016); the column was not recomputed (no producer); the A-NE definition of the cache is declared unrecorded (07:385-386); the 12,000 is read as $2m$, $m = 6{,}000$, an inference (07:359-361); allelic richness 0.521 and $N_{e,par}$ 0.364 of the rank s.d. at fixed $\theta$ (07:427-440) replace the 52 % / 36 % of the earlier text; 06:312-319 now cross-references @tbl-redundancy-residual.
  - [partly settled in 07] -> `07`, `09`, `10`, `11b`: forward promises 06 makes, to keep true: @sec-choosing turns the GD/ANE contrast into a recommendation (06:989-990) and puts $N_{e,par}$ into the objective (06:279-280; 07:442); @sec-collapse "makes the matrix route cheaper still" (06:172-173); @sec-cycles demonstrates that within-pool diversity is the fuel for future progress (06:477); @sec-ocs shows the frontier and @sec-combinatorial gives the recipe (06:910-912). -- settled in 07: the GD/ANE contrast and the recommendation (07:982-1006: A-NE and E-NE are NOT redundant, kept out of the optimiser for cost and form, offered once on the returned plan; assembly from $\mathbf f^L$ an inference, not implemented or timed); $N_{e,par}$ is the second constraint (07:941-947); the matrix route is cheaper than the line route only for large $n$, slower at $N = 2{,}500$, $n = 50$ (07:300-310), so 06:172-173 "cheaper still" is true only with that qualifier (06 is closed: reword 06:173 if the cross-check is wanted). Remaining: -> `11b` (@sec-cycles, 06:477), `09`/`10` (@sec-ocs, @sec-combinatorial, 06:910-912). -- 09 side settled in 09: the frontier is @fig-ocs-frontier and @tbl-ocs-frontier (09:446-489); the recipe stays in @sec-combinatorial (09:93, 727-729). Remaining -> `11b`, `10`.
  - -> bibliography / `quant-refcheck`: Bennewitz & Meuwissen (2006), a proceedings paper (8th WCGALP, no DOI, no Crossref record), is cited in plain text at 06:698-701, second-hand through Lopez-Cortegano 2019, and has no `references.bib` entry (06-M22, 06-M25); the text names author and year and says it was not read. Bibliographic form is refcheck's.

(b) `tbl-catalogue-ns-edge` prints its data frame without `fmt()` (06:193-202, 06-M26); form only.

Counts quoted from the chunks are live and seeded, so a render reproduces them; the timing table is one unreplicated, machine-dependent run and no text number depends on it.
