## 07-choosing-metrics -- Choosing metrics: collapse, cost, redundancy
- Question: which of the catalogue to keep, answered by two questions the catalogue could not ask: what each metric costs per evaluation and how much of what it reports is already in $\theta$ (07:24-26). It follows 06 because 06 built the catalogue and read each metric against the random-subset null, and left the choice to this chapter (06:312, 989-990). Answer: **two** metrics inside the optimiser, **three** at validation, plus one monitoring item outside the analysis behind the other five (07:28-31, @tbl-choosing-recommendation 07:33-59, @sec-choosing-recommendation 07:925-1047). Five findings: 1 an algebraic identity (live), 2-4 cached external results, 5 a live simulation (07:61-64).
- Establishes:
  - The recommendation, inside the homozygosity lens only, for inbred parents and an index additive in the parents, on a cached redundancy analysis whose setting is unrecorded (07:927-929). Optimiser: proportional loss of gene diversity $\alpha = (GD_{ref} - GD_S)/GD_{ref}$ with $GD_S = 1 - \mathbf w^\top\mathbf f^L\mathbf w$ by the line route, never by scanning genotypes; and $N_{e,par} = (\sum_a k_a)^2/\sum_a k_a^2$ as second constraint, tabulated from the same count vector inside `make_fitness_fast()` (07:933-947). Validation on the returned plan: alleles retained (bitset), within/between pool decomposition, $N_s$ (free, a rescaling in $[1/2, 1]$, not a head count) (07:961-975). Monitoring: $F_{drift}$ and $F_{hom}$ against the random-subset null; the redundancy analysis has no drift column, so nothing says they are redundant with $\theta$ (07:976-980).
  - Finding 1, the collapse (@sec-collapse, 07:79-152). Assumptions (i)-(v) declared first: homozygous parents (A2), each hybrid one $A \times B$ cross and the $n$ distinct, $\mathbf f^L$ the molecular coancestry of the lines (@eq-f-molecular), $\theta_S$ over all $n^2$ ordered pairs diagonal included, $\mathbf G^L$ coded 0/1 (07:83-93). @eq-collapse-f $f_{(ab),(cd)} = \tfrac14(f^L_{ac} + f^L_{ad} + f^L_{bc} + f^L_{bd})$ (07:98); @eq-collapse-theta $\theta_S = \mathbf w^\top\mathbf f^L\mathbf w$ with $w_a = k_a/(2n)$, $\sum_a k_a = 2n$, each pool's weights summing to $\tfrac12$ (07:100-108); $\bar{\mathbf p} = \mathbf w^\top\mathbf G^L$ (07:110-113). The derivation is this chapter's own under (i)-(iv); the quadratic form is the identity @sec-hybrid-programme asserts numerically (07:114-116).
  - $\mathbf w$ is a sufficient statistic for the DIVERSITY quantities only (group coancestry, $GD$, $N_s$, $N_{e,par}$, selection frequencies, pool decomposition by usage weights); not for $GD_{WI\text{-}hyb}$ or realised F1 heterozygosity (they depend on pairing), nor the mean index unless additive in the parents (not under dominance, @sec-heterosis) (07:127-136). Memory $O(N^2) \to O(L^2)$ (07:120-125). A swap changes at most four entries of $\mathbf w$: four rank-1 updates in `theta_swap()`, $\theta$ updates in $O(L)$ (07:138-142). The diversity side of the discrete hybrid problem is OCS on lines under three further restrictions, the lattice $k_a/(2n)$, the margins and realisability as line degrees of $n$ distinct pairs; continuous OCS drops all three; stated for the diversity objective and an additive index only (07:144-152).
  - Verification: the cached identity sweep `theta_identity_check.csv` (all ten worst deviations below the asserted tolerance 1e-12; two of ten columns, `max_pairwise_f` and `max_alleles_lost`, have no producer anywhere; the comparators are the package's own slower routes except the pool decomposition and the bitset richness, checked against `ref_*`) (07:156-192); live identity and live swap on the teaching scale, asserted to 1e-12 (07:194-223). Two implementation traps: `ctx$f` partial-matches `ctx$fL`; R integers are 32-bit so the bitset packs 31 bits per word (07:225-236).
  - Scales named (07:251-255): the BENCHMARK scale of the cached cost study ($L = 300$, $N = 22{,}500$, $m = 10{,}000$, $n = 1{,}000$), distinct from the 71 + 71 lines and 25,000 markers of @sec-era-programme and from the TEACHING scale (25 + 25 lines, 2,000 markers). Hardware, R version and timer of the cost benchmark are not recorded (07:72-73, 255).
  - Finding 2, cost (@sec-choosing-cost, 07:238-325), from `cost_per_eval.csv`, cached external: genotype-scan $\theta$ 14.2 h per million evaluations against 106 us for the line route (482x) for an algebraically identical number; the incremental swap 5 us (5.0 s per million; the timer resolves about 1 us, so one significant figure); $N_{e,par}$ 7 us, "of the same order" and NOT ordered against the swap (at $n = 200$ the file gives other values) (07:268-287); bitset allelic richness 892 us, 5.7x faster than the float version, 8.4x the line route and 178x the swap, so it belongs at validation because it would make every evaluation several times dearer, not because it is infeasible (07:288-299); MAF-threshold allele loss 5,297 us = 1.47 h per million (07:1017-1019). The line route is cheaper than the matrix route only for large $n$: slower at $N = 2{,}500$, $n = 50$, cheaper at $N = 10{,}000$, $n = 1{,}000$, only the line route exists at $N = 22{,}500$ (07:300-310). The live table `cost_per_eval()` (seeded, repetitions multiplied by 4 until 0.2 s, no line-route, swap or bitset row, wall-clock of the render machine) supports no ordering across routes (07:312-325).
  - Finding 3, redundancy (@sec-choosing-redundancy, 07:327-529), `metric_redundancy.csv` cached external, 621 selections, no recorded setting (07:355-361). Scope: ONE lens (homozygosity; no $F_{drift}$ or $F_{hom}$); a MIX of strategies (300 random, 120 truncation, 113 restricted-line-pool, 88 greedy), so all rank statistics are properties of the mix (within-strategy $\rho(\theta, N_{e,par})$ -0.60 random, -0.95 truncation, against -0.925 overall); the `eff_dim` column is read as the UNCENTRED ratio (mean 1.486), claims about `eff_dim` restricted to that form and not about the package's (07:363-386). PC1 87.6 % and PC2 6.6 % of the rank variance of the 16 diversity columns, 85.4 % and 7.5 % without the four exact rescalings of $\theta$ (07:396-401); Spearman with $\theta$: `eff_dim` 0.967, MAF allele loss 0.932, $N_{e,par}$ 0.925 (07:402-405). The marginal correlation is "the wrong test"; the residual test (rank of the metric on the rank of $\theta$ and its square, residual s.d. over rank s.d., @tbl-redundancy-residual, 07:407-425) shows nine metrics keeping at least 0.35: between-pool coancestry 0.653, A-NE 0.556, E-NE 0.523, alleles retained 0.521, number of lines used 0.503, within-pool coancestries 0.391 and 0.373, $N_{e,par}$ 0.364, MAF allele loss 0.352; the uncentred `eff_dim` 0.250 (07:427-440). Allelic richness is ONE OF SEVERAL metrics that vary at fixed $\theta$, not "the one real second axis" (the cached figure's panel title is withdrawn, 07:391); the residual criterion is necessary, not sufficient (07:449-454). Inside the band $|\theta - \mathrm{median}(\theta)| < 0.002$: 399 selections, alleles retained span 171 alleles, $N_{e,par}$ 30.1 to 55.8 (07:441-447). Live teaching-scale reproduction of the $N_{e,par}$ half only, seed 77, with a log-spaced index-weight grid (a linear grid returned two distinct selections) (07:460-517).
  - Finding 4, searching on line weights, declared a PREVIEW of @sec-combinatorial and @sec-advanced, with its terms (DE, penalty/repair, `max_use`, decoder) introduced there (07:531-538). Cached `de_constraints.csv`, three seeds per configuration, 60,000 evaluations, four simultaneous restrictions (loss $\le 0.5\%$, $N_{e,par} \ge 50$, at most 6 uses per line, pool balance within 5%), $N = 3{,}600$ against $L = 120$ (07:540-552). Neither arm is `sel_de()`, both cold (07:549-552). Hybrid-key arm mean index 0.351, line-weight arm 1.988, difference 1.64, seed ranges not overlapping; hybrid arm $N_{e,par}$ and loss slack, maximum use 5, 6, 6 against a cap of 6; pool imbalance 0 in all nine rows, so the "four restrictions" bind on two at most; penalty and repair numerically identical in the hybrid arm; no line-weight arm with a penalty (07:579-600). A slack constraint at the end of a run is the sign of an unconverged search here, "not a general rule" (07:602-606). Limits: (a) two encodings, not two handlers, and the line decoder satisfies the cap by construction (07:607-612); (b) `search_benchmark.csv` (regenerated here): uncapped, `sel_de()` ahead of line-encoded DE at the book scale only; capped, an unjudged gap of 0.0041 at the book scale (1.1097 against 1.1056, sd 0 in every capped cell, "no standard error exists to judge it by") and none at the production scale (1.1223 both); the result is a verdict on the $N$-dimensional key encoding, not on `sel_de()` (07:613-646); (c) three seeds, `NP` unrecorded against the DEoptim guidance of $10 \times$ parameters (07:646-653). The earlier unconstrained-truncation comparison (2.337 / 1.62 % / 33.2 / 17 / 85 %) is withdrawn (07:653-658).
  - Finding 5, the index is not the truth and its error is not white (@sec-choosing-error, 07:660-923), live, teaching scale, $n = 60$ of $N = 625$. $\hat u = \mathrm{scale}(r\,u + \sqrt{1 - r^2}\,e)$, $e$ either $\mathbf T\mathbf z$ with $\mathbf T\mathbf T^\top = \mathbf f + 10^{-8}\mathbf I$ (correlated with relatedness) or $\mathbf z$ (independent); $r$ is a NOMINAL accuracy, the realised correlation differs in every seed (07:676-688); $\alpha$ against `gd_ref` with $B = 400$. `reported` exceeds `realised` and the gap opens as $r$ falls; nothing in the pipeline detects it without the truth (07:764-772). "$\alpha$ moves little with accuracy" holds for CORRELATED error only: truncation $\alpha$ 4.12, 4.16, 3.96, 3.79 % at $r$ = 1, 0.9, 0.7, 0.5 under correlated error, 4.12, 3.38, 1.83, 0.75 % under independent (twenty seeds, paired difference with its s.e.) (07:774-805). Greedy ($w = 3 \times 10^{-4}$) under independent error: $\alpha$ falls from 1.33 % at $r = 1$ to -0.66 % at $r = 0.5$ (s.e. 0.03 pp), monotone in $r$ in 20 of 20 seeds, more diverse than the random baseline, which the truncation reading (noisy selection tends to random) cannot account for; the reading that greedy penalises $\theta$ is marked NOT TESTED; under correlated error greedy stays positive, 0.92 % (07:806-821). Correlated against independent error at $r = 0.6$, sixty seeds, within seed: truncation loses more diversity under correlated error (paired difference in 60 of 60 seeds) and uses its most-used line more (52 of 60); the realised-index difference is NOT a result (block means of twenty seeds -0.074, 0.002, 0.001); that families are the mechanism was not tested (07:823-892). Callout: truncation on a perfect index loses 4.12 % (4.129 % at $B = 2000$, @sec-baseline) and delivers all it reports; the diversity price of aggressive selection can stay close to its full amount while the gain falls short; report $\alpha$ against the plan planted and treat a reported index as an upper bound (07:894-914, 916-923). Nothing changes in the metric set: Findings 1-3 are statements about $\mathbf f$ (07:918).
  - Core Hunter's E-NE and A-NE are NOT redundant, and are kept out of the optimiser on cost and form (`ane()` needs the $N \times N$ hybrid matrix, an $O(Nn)$ pass per evaluation, not timed), not redundancy; offered once on the returned plan; assembly of the $N \times n$ block from $\mathbf f^L$ via @eq-collapse-f is an inference, not implemented or timed (07:982-1006). Not recommended: Nei's $H_e$ as a separate metric (it is $1 - \theta$), effective dimensionality (cached column is uncentred, the package's double-centred form was not in the analysis), MAF-threshold allele loss (cost, and allelic richness stands for allele retention; whether the two carry the same information was not examined), E-NE and A-NE as optimiser metrics (07:1008-1027).
  - Caveats: the choice of $\mathbf f^L$ is open and the chapter relies on no ranking of matrices; the collapse is algebra for any symmetric $\mathbf f^L$ given @eq-collapse-f, whose adequacy for, e.g., a centred VanRaden matrix is not established; managing on molecular coancestry controls homozygosity, not drift; a predicted rate of coancestry is biased downward by roughly a factor of two in the simulations of Gomez-Romano 2016 (as reported in @sec-ocs); report realised $\theta$ on the plan (07:1029-1047). Drop-in code, not run, is `sel_de_fast()` with warm start, all four restrictions and `NP` $= 10 \times$ lines (07:1049-1077).
  - Notation introduced in 90: $\mathbf T$ (Cholesky factor of $\mathbf f + 10^{-8}\mathbf I$, 07:679-680; `\mathbf L` retired; the code variable `Lc` is unchanged), $\mathbf k$ and $\bar{\mathbf p} = \mathbf w^\top\mathbf G^L$, $e$, $z$, nominal accuracy $r$ (90:285, 297, 320, 29). "benchmark scale" and "teaching scale" are this chapter's names (07:251-255); `w` is the index weight of the greedy score, $\omega$ of @sec-notation, not the usage vector $\mathbf w$ (07:492-495).
- Assumes: inbred or DH parents (A2); each hybrid an $A \times B$ cross, the $n$ distinct; $\mathbf f^L$ molecular coancestry, identity in state; diversity objective and additive index for the equivalence with OCS on lines; the homozygosity lens only; for Findings 2-4 the cached external files at their own scales (benchmark scale for cost, 621 unrecorded-setting selections for redundancy, $N = 3{,}600$, $L = 120$ for the DE runs); the redundancy statistics are properties of one strategy mix; Finding 5 at the teaching scale only, $n = 60$ of 625, nominal accuracy, twenty seeds (60 for the comparison of the two kinds of error), $B = 400$, one scale measured.
- Takes from: `00`: A2 (@sec-era-programme), @sec-hybrid-programme (the quadratic form in line usage asserted numerically). `01`: @eq-f-molecular. `02`: Caballero & Toro 2002 (the decomposition, 07:91, 07:969). `03`: @sec-simulating-crosses (`ctx$index` from known marker effects, additive index, 07:135, 665-666). `05`: @sec-genomic-era ($F_{drift}$, $F_{hom}$, the sign of the bias of a drift constraint), @sec-era-programme (the programme scale). `05b`: @sec-heterosis (the index is not additive under dominance). `06`: @sec-catalogue (@eq-catalogue-theta, @eq-catalogue-alpha, @eq-catalogue-theta-pools, @eq-catalogue-ene-ane; the uncentred participation ratio 1.47 and the package's 26.19 on its subset; `ane()` keeps a nonzero self-distance; @tbl-catalogue-z, @tbl-catalogue-seeds-z; the 4.129 % of @sec-baseline), @sec-ns, @sec-richness, @sec-notation. Forward pointers only, none relied on for a result: @sec-combinatorial, @sec-advanced, @sec-ocs (07:536, 613, 1005, 1043), @sec-case-study (07:919), @sec-genomic-prediction (07:671), @sec-plant-literature (07:1031).

Equation labels `eq-collapse-f` and `eq-collapse-theta` are cited from no other chapter.

## 08-pipeline-stages -- Metrics at each pipeline stage, and five traps
- Question: where, at each of the four stages S1-S4 of @tbl-hp-stages, the metric set that 07 chose is applied, and which five readings of it look correct, return a number and are wrong (08:18-23). It follows 07 because 07 chose the set (07:28-31, 925-1047) and left its placement in the programme to this chapter; 07's @sec-collapse and @sec-choosing-cost are cited here, not rederived (08:31-39, 690-694, 912-916). Three rules first: every exact identity is asserted with `stopifnot()` in the chunk that shows it, an identity shown without an assertion is not claimed as verified (08:25-30); the metric set is 07's (08:31-39); the assumptions are declared once (08:40-49).
- Establishes:
  - Scale and provenance (08:50-61). Live chunks of S1 (except the Trap 3 sweep), S3 and S4 use one simulated world of 30 + 30 lines, 3,000 markers, 900 hybrids (`simulate_pools(30, 30, 3000, fst = 0.15, seed = 12)`); the Trap 3 sweep uses the teaching scale (25 + 25 lines, 2,000 markers). Five artefacts are cached results of an external study with no code in the repository and are never called reproducible: `fst_definitions.csv`, `dist_vs_variance.csv`, `pipeline_loss_ledger.csv`, `metric_pitfalls.png`, `pipeline_loss.png` (@sec-verification lists them); what is computed from the csv files is a re-analysis, not a reproduction.
  - Kernel (08:69-84): @eq-stages-fij $f_{ij} = 1 - m^{-1}\sum_k (x_{ik} - x_{jk})^2$ for inbred lines coded 0/1; $\theta$ includes the diagonal, so a single line has $\theta = 1$ and a pair $(1 + f_{ij})/2$.
  - Trap 1 (08:87-109): $f_{ij} = 1 - \mathrm{MRD}_{ij}^2$ by definition; checked against `dist(GL)/sqrt(m)`, not through `mrd_matrix()` (built from $\mathbf f$, so circular); deviation $2\times 10^{-16}$, asserted below $10^{-12}$; homozygous lines only, not claimed for hybrids.
  - S1 partition (08:111-145): @eq-stages-partition $\theta_T = \sum_p\sum_q \varpi_p\varpi_q\theta_{pq}$ with census weights $\varpi_p = n_p/L$; $\theta_w = \sum_p \varpi_p\theta_{pp}$, $GD_{WS} = 1 - \theta_w$ (code `GD_within`; $GD_{BI}$ for inbred lines), $\theta_{AB}$ between pools (`theta_B` of `theta_decompose()`, a name `theta_pools()` uses for within-pool B), $GD_{BS} = \theta_w - \theta_T$; asserted to $10^{-12}$. The opposition of within-pool erosion and between-pool inflation is 00's drift argument; it does not cover selection, and the rise of $GD_{BS}$ under `run_cycles()` is a simulation result (08:113-121).
  - Trap 2, @sec-stages-trap2 (08:147-205): two statistics written $F_{ST}$. The book's $F_{ST} = GD_{BS}/GD_T = (\theta_w - \theta_T)/(1 - \theta_T) = (H_T - H_S)/H_T$, Nei's $G_{ST}$ with plug-in diversities (code `Gst_Nei`), @eq-stages-fst; the other, @eq-stages-fst-btw $F^{\text{btw}}_{ST} = (\theta_w - \theta_{AB})/(1 - \theta_{AB})$ (code `Fst_Wright`, "Wright" the code's label, no claim about a paper). With equal pool weights, @eq-stages-fst-ratio $F^{\text{btw}}_{ST}/F_{ST} = 2 - F^{\text{btw}}_{ST} \in [1, 2]$, asserted live (0.1824 / 0.1004 = 1.817 on the seed-12 world) and on all 7 cached rows; @tbl-fst-definitions: ratio 1.96 to 1.59 across $F_{BN}$ (08:195-207). Which reference was used must be stated.
  - Trap 3 (08:208-278), @tbl-trap3-sweep, 4 levels of $F_{BN}$ x seeds 1 to 20 at the teaching scale: `mean_sq_delta_p` $= 2F_{ST}GD_T = 2GD_{BS}$ for equal pool sizes, asserted to $10^{-10}$ over the 80 draws, correlation with $F_{ST}$ 0.9984; $F_{ST}$ mean 0.0465 to 0.2697, $GD_{WS}$ 0.3325 to 0.2108; `frac_opposite_fixed` near zero except at the top level. So the frequency-side statistics do not decouple from $F_{ST}$ here; what decouples is expected heterosis, which is 05b's (@tbl-het-sweep, @sec-heterosis-flat). Prescription: hold $F_{ST}$ in a band, not at a maximum; the width is a programme decision and is not established (08:271-278). Pools whose divergence was produced by selection are not examined (08:256-270).
  - S1 monitoring panel (08:280-309), @tbl-s1-panel: the 11 columns of `s1_monitor()` (`cycle`, `GD_within`, `GD_total`, `Fst_Wright`, `Gst_Nei`, `Ns_total`, `GD_A`, `GD_B`, `frac_opposite_fixed`, `mean_abs_delta_p`, `mean_sq_delta_p`); `cycle = 12` is a label, the chunk is one draw, not a time series; `Ns_total` 0.76 is a rescaling, not three quarters of a line. @sec-cycles logs a different set of columns and does not show this panel as a time series (08:297-300). Kadoumi et al. (2025) is one programme with both movements, an abstract-level reading, not a test of the panel (08:304-309).
  - S2 identities (08:313-396), @tbl-dh-identities, for a DH family from two inbreds under the per-locus marginal 1/2 (holds with or without linkage): (a) $\mathbb E[f] = (1 + f_{ij})/2$ between sibs, (b) $(f_{ik} + f_{il} + f_{jk} + f_{jl})/4$ between families, (c) @eq-stages-dh-theta $\theta_S = \mathbf w^\top\mathbf f^L\mathbf w + n_{DH}^{-1}(1 - \sum_c (n_c/n_{DH})(1 + f_c)/2)$ with $w_a = k_a/(2n_{DH})$, the same construction as @sec-collapse for hybrids; the second term corrects the $n_{DH}$ diagonal pairs and is $O(1/n_{DH})$. (c) asserted against a literal pair-by-pair oracle (equal $n_c = 40$; the package test covers unequal $n_c$, tolerance 1e-12, and `dh_pool_theta()` now weights the correction by $n_c/n_{DH}$, 08-M8); (a), (b) asserted by gene dropping at free recombination (2,000 pairs, tolerance 0.002). Callout (08:397-403): the diversity of a DH pool is a quadratic form in which parents are used and how often; the lines need not be genotyped or exist.
  - Progeny variance (08:405-452): @eq-stages-sigma-dh $\sigma^2_{DH(a,b)} = \tfrac14\sum_{k:\,x_{ak} \ne x_{bk}}\beta_k^2$, additive model, inbred parents, FREE recombination only (with linkage the covariances enter and it is not the variance); usefulness criterion $UC_{ab} = \mu_{ab} + i\,h\,\sigma_{DH(a,b)}$, $\mu_{ab}$ the mid-parent value under additivity, $i$ the intensity of @eq-response-breeders, $h$ the "$r$ of that equation" (`h = 0.6` an illustration, not an estimate); matches gene dropping to the asserted 3 % (@tbl-sigma-uc). Schnell and Utz (1975) is not in the bibliography and was not checked (08:422-427).
  - Trap 4 (08:453-495), cached `dist_vs_variance.csv`, @tbl-trap4, @fig-metric-pitfalls: within a stratum the distance explains 1.1 % and 4.8 % of the variance of $\sigma_{DH}$ against 33.2 % pooled (cor 0.1029, 0.2188, 0.5760); within a stratum distance is near-linear in the number of segregating loci; the crosses share parents and are not independent; the consequence for a ranking of crosses was not computed. Rank on `sigma_dh_crosses()` from marker effects.
  - S3 (08:497-531): constrain within each pool is an argument from the partition, not demonstrated; the demonstration is pooled (top 20 by merit against 300 random sets of 20, `loss_vs_resampling()`, @tbl-s3-loss, @tbl-s3-pools).
  - Trap 5, @sec-stages-trap5 (08:532-686): the incremental greedy ("add the next-best line while $\theta$ stays below the ceiling") returns the empty set silently, because a single line has $\theta = 1$ and no pair meets the ceiling (size 0, @tbl-trap5-greedy); $\theta$ of a random set falls with set size, so the ceiling is meaningful only at the target size (@fig-theta-by-size, ceiling 0.6746 at $n = 20$ for $\alpha = 0.005$; `theta_ceiling()` $= 1 - GD_{ran}(1 - \alpha)$). The shipped route, `select_lines_constrained()`, was rewritten in this review (exact swap score, every accepted swap strictly lowers $\theta$, warns and sets `met`/`n_swap` when it stops short; R/05, four new tests): seed-12 world $\theta$ 0.67226 against ceiling 0.67464 (truncation 0.67554), 1 swap (@tbl-trap5-repair); 20 worlds (seeds 1 to 20), truncation violates in 10, repair meets in 20 (previously 13), returns the truncation set in the 10 non-violating worlds, at most 4 swaps, mean merit cost 0.041 where it swapped (max 0.148) (@tbl-trap5-worlds); stated as what these 20 worlds show, not a guarantee. Within pool, one call per pool with its own ceiling: both pools meet (1 and 0 swaps; truncation exceeds only in pool A), which shows the call runs, not that it preserves more between-pool structure (@tbl-trap5-within-pool). Keep the check `attr(sel, "met")`.
  - S4 (08:688-735): under 07's assumptions, $\theta_S = \mathbf k^\top\mathbf f^L\mathbf k/(2n)^2$ for 100 random hybrids equals the hybrid-matrix value (deviation below $10^{-12}$, asserted; @tbl-s4-usage); the saving is memory, $O(N^2) \to O(L^2)$, not time per evaluation unless $n$ is large; $H_e$ of the merged frequencies $= 1 - \theta_S$ (0.337915 against $\theta$ 0.662085, @tbl-s4-he), not the heterozygosity the F1s carry; $N_{e,\text{par}}$ 49.63.
  - The ledger, @sec-stages-ledger (08:737-909), cached `pipeline_loss_ledger.csv`, 8 founder pools, arms `UC only` (cross selection on the usefulness criterion alone, so including $\sigma_{DH}$, not on merit alone) and `UC + div` (the criterion plus a diversity term on parental coancestry). Loss is $100(GD_{before} - GD_{after})/GD_{before}$ against each stage's own re-based same-size baseline, so stage losses do not add and are not parts of one end-to-end loss (asserted on all 48 rows; exactly 2 negative rows, S3 `UC only`). `UC only`: loss 9.40, 0.32, 2.48 % at S2, S3, S4, shares 77.5, 2.5, 20.0 %; `UC + div`: 3.43, 2.98, 3.47 %, shares 35.5, 29.0, 35.5 % (@tbl-ledger). End-to-end change of $GD$ (S2 `GD_before` to S4 `GD_after`, a change including the baseline-sampling effect, not a loss against a random set): 13.069 % and 10.729 % (08:825-845). The term lowered the S2 loss in 8 of 8 pools (9.40 to 3.43 %) and raised the S3 and S4 losses in 8 of 8; "S2 is the cheapest stage to constrain" is not measured, the term moved loss rather than removing it; the largest share at S2 is a statement about the first arm at this scale with these 8 pools (08:846-860).
  - What the term buys (@tbl-endpoints, 08:861-909), paired by pool, 8 pools, paired $t$, normality unchecked, four outcomes unadjusted: merit cost 11.0 % (33.40 to 29.71, lower in 8 of 8, $p$ 0.0002); final-set $GD$ +0.0078 (CI 0.0021 to 0.0136, higher in 7 of 8, $p$ 0.015), a post hoc outcome added by this re-analysis and not significant at a Bonferroni level of 0.0125, "a signal to test, not a finding"; $N_e$ +1.13 (CI -0.97 to 3.22) and alleles retained +41 (CI -101 to 182): intervals contain no change and changes of the sizes shown, "not significant" is not "no benefit". That diversity spent at S2 is "reclaimed" later, and that a term carried through all four stages would hold the final diversity, are not tested.
  - Cost, @sec-stages-cost (08:910-941), @tbl-stage-cost: orders only, nothing timed; the timings the book reports are in @sec-choosing-cost, where the line route is slower than the matrix route at $N = 2{,}500$, $n = 50$. Checklist (08:942-966), eight items, each at the strength of its trap.
  - Notation introduced in 90 (new table "Pipeline stages", 90:306-330, 11 rows): $\varpi_p$ (census weight of pool $p$; `w_p` retired), $\theta_{pq}$, $\theta_T$ (the $\bar f$ of 90's partition table), $\theta_w$ (its $\tilde f$), $F^{\text{btw}}_{ST}$, $C$, $n_c$, $f_c$, $n_{DH} = \sum_c n_c$ (`D` retired), $\bar F_{cc'}$, $a$, $b$ (the parents of a cross, not the DH indices $(i, j)$), $i$, $h$, $q$ (of @eq-response-breeders), $\mu_{ab}$, $UC_{ab}$, $\sigma_{DH(a,b)}$. 90:154 and 90:270 (the $F_{ST}$ list and the $G_{ST}$ row) edited. $F_{BN}$ throughout for the simulation parameter `fst`; "Balding-Nichols" is the name only (03:79-84 bounds the attribution).
- Assumes: parents inbred or DH, coded 0/1 at biallelic markers (A2); $\mathbf f$ identity in state with no base population, so every loss is against a random subset of the same size from the set being selected from (no frozen `p0`); the simulated merit is additive, no dominance; the DH identities (a)-(c) need only the per-locus marginal 1/2, @eq-stages-sigma-dh needs free recombination; the $F^{\text{btw}}_{ST}/F_{ST}$ ratio and the `mean_sq_delta_p` identity need equal pool sizes/weights (live pools and, inferred from the identity holding to 4e-15 in all rows, the cached table); the S4 collapse needs 07's assumptions (each hybrid one $A \times B$ cross, the $n$ distinct); everything live is one world of 30 + 30 lines, 3,000 markers except the Trap 3 sweep (teaching scale, 20 seeds x 4 levels); Traps 2 and 4 and the ledger rest on cached external files of unrecorded scale (the ledger's description, 6,000 markers, $F_{BN}$ = 0.15, 20 crosses of 40 DH lines, is quoted and cannot be checked), the ledger on 8 founder pools; paired $t$ with normality unchecked; no run varies crosses or DH lines per cross; production scale not run.
- Takes from: `00`: A2 (@sec-era-programme), @tbl-hp-stages (@sec-hp-cycle; the opposition of the two terms, Kadoumi et al. with its limits), @sec-hp-popgen (the F1 heterozygosity), @sec-hp-inbreeding ($F_{ST}$ against $F_{BN}$). `01`: @eq-f-molecular (kernel). `02`: @sec-subdivided, @eq-gd-partition, @eq-f-stats. `02b`: @eq-response-breeders and @sec-response (`sel_intensity()`). `03`: @sec-simulating-crosses (`simulate_pools()`, `ctx_from_pools()`, `fst` as $F_{BN}$). `04`: @sec-sizing (genetic maps, named only). `05`: @sec-genomic-era (the code's "Wright" label). `05b`: @sec-heterosis, @sec-heterosis-flat, @tbl-het-sweep, "Reading the S1 panel" (05b:911). `06`: @eq-catalogue-theta, @sec-ns, @sec-baseline, @sec-catalogue. `07`: @sec-choosing (the metric set), @sec-collapse (@eq-collapse-theta), @sec-choosing-cost. ## 08b-genomic-prediction -- Predicting breeding values: GBLUP, BayesA, BayesB
- Question: what a fitted prediction model reaches on the simulated hybrids, where `ctx$traits` had until now been taken as the truth (GBLUP by the mixed model equations, BayesA and BayesB by Gibbs sampling over marker effects), what the accuracy depends on (which parents the training set had seen, training size, noise, the rank of $\mathbf G$), what the model's own reliability says without the truth, and how the predictions enter the `stage1_build()` contract. It follows 08 because 08 closes with the S4 selection taken as given (hybrids ranked on `ctx$index`) and the stage table of 00 says genomic prediction supplies the predicted values S4 selects on; 08b supplies them for the simulated hybrids (08b:13-17, 00:183-191). It answers the two gaps 03 and 07 left (03's callout leaves the prediction gap to it, 07's Finding 5 manufactured the error, 08b:19-25) and is one trait, one additive kernel, on a candidate subset.
- Establishes:
  - Assumptions of the whole chapter (08b:55-64): inbred parents, the complete $25 \times 25$ factorial ($N = 625$, $L = 50$, $m = 2000$, `book_cfg`, seed 1); purely additive trait (no dominance, no SCA); trait generated from the markers the models see (every causal locus genotyped, no marker-QTL gap, no LD, "a best case in that respect"); $\mathbf G$ centred on the hybrids' own frequencies; $h^2$ supplied, not estimated; accuracy = Pearson correlation among candidates with the true $g$ (`ctx$traits[, 1]`).
  - The generative model is GBLUP's own (@sec-genpred-truth, 08b:111-155): @eq-genpred-var-g $\mathrm{Var}(\mathbf g) = \mathbf G\sigma^2_u$, $\sigma^2_u = 2\sum_k p_k(1-p_k)\sigma^2_\beta$; asserted (`dev_G` < 1e-10, $\mathbf g$ in the column space of $\mathbf Z$ < 1e-8). The simulation is the infinitesimal model, so no model is misspecified and every accuracy is a best case. $\sigma^2_u \approx 1/\overline{G_{ii}} = 1.114$ ($\overline{G_{ii}} = 0.898$), so the supplied $\lambda = (1-h^2)/h^2$ is about 11% too large; the implied ratio is $\lambda\,\overline{G_{ii}}$ (08b:146-155, 245-257).
  - Additive identity (08b:156-195, asserted below 1e-10): an F1 of inbred lines has value = mean of the two parental line values for any additive effect vector (GCA, no SCA); consequences: $d_k$ ("dominance effects", @sec-heterosis) are absent from `ctx$traits`, so SCA is not in the data (08b:184-189), and fitted marker effects score any A x B combination from the two lines' genotype rows (`genpred-parent-score`, 08b:746, asserted). The saving is phenotyping, the cost genotyping the two lines; the memory saving @sec-stages attributes to S4 is a different one (08b:189-191). In a real hybrid the identity covers the additive part only (08b:192-195).
  - Mixed model equations (@sec-genpred-mme, 08b:210-268): $\mathbf y = \mathbf 1\mu + \mathbf W\mathbf u + \mathbf e$, $\mathbf W$ the $n_t \times N$ training incidence matrix ($\mathbf Z$ stays the centred marker matrix), @eq-genpred-mme with $\lambda = \sigma^2_e/\sigma^2_u$; $\hat\mu$ is the GLS intercept (`fit$mu`), not the training mean; $\mathbf u$ is defined over all $N$, so one solve predicts training and candidates; $\mathbf u$ and the simulation's $\mathbf g$ are the same quantity, $\hat{\mathbf u}$ its BLUP, $g$ the truth (@sec-choosing-error uses $u$ for the true index and $\hat u$ for a simulated prediction: same roles, different letters, 08b:239-247). The joint-density derivation is the chapter's own; attribution to Henderson (1975) unverified (08b:225-228).
  - Ridge blend (@sec-g-singular, 08b:269-384): $\mathbf G\mathbf 1 = \mathbf 0$ from @eq-g-sum-zero, so $\mathbf G$ is singular for any $N$, $m$ (when centred on its own frequencies; not for $\mathbf G_{0.5}$); @eq-genpred-rank $\mathrm{rank}(\mathbf G) \le \min(L - 2, m)$, a bound under the complete factorial, attained at $L$ = 20, 40, 50 (@tbl-genpred-rank, asserted, $m = 2000$); it is @eq-era-rank's argument with one dimension more for centring, cited and not rederived (08b:287-298). Every solve uses $\mathbf G^* = w_G\mathbf G + (1 - w_G)\mathbf I$, $w_G = 0.95$ (`w`).
  - `gblup()` agrees with the kernel form $\hat{\mathbf u} = \mathbf G^*_{\cdot t}(\mathbf G^*_{tt} + \mathbf I\lambda)^{-1}(\mathbf y_t - \hat\mu\mathbf 1)$ to ~4e-14 (asserted below 1e-8); GBLUP = marker ridge with $\lambda_\beta = \lambda\cdot 2\sum_k p_k(1-p_k)$, @eq-genpred-identity, asserted below 1e-10 on the raw $\mathbf G$ (the blend has no marker-space equivalent); the Gibbs ridge is a weaker, untoleranced check (08b:875-941).
  - Reliability (@sec-genpred-reliability, 08b:385-466): @eq-genpred-reliability $r_i^2 = 1 - \mathrm{PEV}_i/(\sigma^2_u G^*_{ii}) = 1 - \lambda[\mathbf C^{uu}]_{ii}/G^*_{ii}$ from the inverse of the coefficient matrix, needs no truth; "implied accuracy" is $\sqrt{\overline{r_i^2}}$ over the candidates, set against the realised correlation. Over 40 draws (noise 101-140, split $s + 1000$; @tbl-genpred-seeds): realised 0.939, implied 0.895, gap 0.044; the blend $w_G = 0.999$ gives 72% of it (+0.032), $\lambda\overline{G_{ii}}$ a further +0.007, 0.006 left and not attributed; the opening split (accuracy 0.960) is 1.4 s.d. above the 40-draw mean and not typical.
  - Which parents the training set had seen (@sec-hybrid-cv, 08b:467-659): the partition is Technow et al. (2014)'s (T2/T1/T0); CV2/CV1/CV0 are this book's reuse of the labels, with a meaning different from @sec-sparse-validation (08b:477-488; 90:381-385 equates them with T2/T1/T0). 20 replicates, 289 training hybrids, 8 held-out lines per pool, 272 CV1 and 64 CV0 candidates (@tbl-genpred-cv): accuracy 0.905 / 0.665 / 0.144 (s.d. 0.023 / 0.068 / 0.155), implied 0.873 / 0.644 / 0.127, gap (implied minus realised) -0.031 / -0.021 / -0.017 (s.e. 0.005 / 0.015 / 0.035): the gap is clear only in CV2; the model knows the mean accuracy of a scheme, not what one holdout returns. CV0 is 4.2 s.e. from zero while 20% of replicates are at or below zero. The rank of $\mathbf G_{tt}$ is $2(25 - 8) - 1 = 33$ (asserted), offered as the explanation of the collapse and "tested only weakly". The form $r = \sqrt{nh^2/(nh^2 + M_e)}$ is the chapter's own algebraic statement of Daetwyler et al. (2008)'s dependence, consistent with the lines unit ($n$ = training lines, $M_e$ = marker count) and not with the records unit at $k$ = 4, 8, 12, "consistent, not confirming" (@tbl-genpred-daetwyler, 08b:580-624). Callout "Report which parents the training set had seen" (08b:648-658).
  - BayesA, BayesB (08b:660-874): priors written out (@sec-genpred-bayes, 08b:685-757); the samplers get the uncentred dosages $\mathbf M^H$ (`M`), $\mathbf M^H\boldsymbol\beta = \mathbf Z\boldsymbol\beta + \mathbf 1(2\mathbf p'\boldsymbol\beta)$, the constant absorbed by the intercept (08b:687-694); `bWGR::BayesB()` takes `pi` as a fixed input and returns none (here 0.95, a choice; `df = 5`, `R2 = 0.5`). On the oligogenic trait (3 causal markers, effectively monogenic: one carries the larger share of the variance, nominal $\pi = 1 - 3/2000 = 0.9985$ understates its sparsity, one architecture) BayesB's inclusion probability at that marker is >= 0.990 in all 8 refits, median `d` 0.178 (sum 353 of 2000, prior 0.05); BayesA's variance there is above its median in all 8 chains by an unstable factor (1.4 to 31.7); which feature gives BayesB its advantage was not isolated (@tbl-genpred-bayes-seeds, 08b:758-874).
  - Which model wins (08b:943-1057): cached `genomic_prediction_accuracy.csv` and `genomic_prediction_pi_sensitivity.csv` are produced in this repository by `book/scripts/regenerate.R genpred` (the run reproduces both exactly; seeds $1000 + i$ and $2000 + $ replicate), unlike the other cached external files of the book. 8 paired contrasts (2 methods x 4 cells), paired $t$ on 15 splits, Bonferroni 0.05/8 = 0.00625: only BayesB oligogenic $h^2 = 0.5$ passes (+0.0224, s.e. 0.0017, $t$ = 12.86, $p = 3.8 \times 10^{-9}$, printed $< 0.0001$); BayesA oligogenic 0.2 ($p$ = 0.0068) and polygenic 0.2 ($p$ = 0.060) do not; the rest are exploratory, the largest $|t|$ among the three BayesB null cells is 0.88. The polygenic arm is @sec-genpred-truth restated (GBLUP's prior is the true one). `pi` sweep (@sec-genpred-pi, 8 paired splits per value, stops at 0.995, never reaches the nominal truth): accuracy rises with `pi` in a gradual way; BayesC$\pi$ [@habier2011] estimates $\hat\pi$ at the same value for both architectures, far from either truth, and estimating it is not a substitute for choosing it here.
  - Hand-off (@sec-genpred-handoff, 08b:1136-1181; chunk asserted): `stage1_build(ctx$X[cand, ], ctx$ped[cand, ], data.frame(trait1 = scale(predB)))` returns `hybrid, line_A, line_B, trait1`, ids renumbered $1, \dots, 250$ (line ids kept), $\mathbf f$ 250 x 250: $N$ becomes 250 for any chapter fed this object, the 375 training hybrids are not in it, and it is one trait, not the multi-trait matrix from which `build_ctx()` forms `ctx$index` (03). `ocs_quadprog(f, gebv, lambda, ...)` is not run; its `lambda` is the OCS penalty, not $\lambda = \sigma^2_e/\sigma^2_u$. The reliabilities should be stored next to every predicted value.
  - Back to Finding 5 (08b:1182-1235): 07's $r \in \{0.5, 0.7, 0.9, 1\}$ is a nominal accuracy against the three-trait index; 08b's are realised correlations with one trait, so the comparison is of orders of magnitude only (CV2 between 0.9 and 1, CV1 between 0.5 and 0.7, CV0 below the grid); the 0.960 of the opening split is not the CV2 figure. Published accuracies are higher for the hard schemes (0.91 / 0.85 / 0.77, T2/T1/T0, @sec-mating-genomic) and nothing says the low end of Finding 5's grid is the realistic one. Error structure (@tbl-genpred-errstructure): correlation of the product of two candidates' errors with their $f$ is reported for the opening split and over the 40 draws against a permutation null (500 relabellings; mean, sd and maximum printed in the table); a reference, not a formal test; not compared with Finding 5's generator.
  - Not modelled, declared (callout, 08b:1236-1281): marker panel = causal set (finite accuracy comes from training size, noise and the rank bound, not a marker-QTL gap), SCA, one architecture and one noise stream per cell, estimated variance components, multivariate prediction, forward validation, retraining across cycles, genotyping cost and marker density.
  - Notation (90:36-66 Matrices table: $\mathbf G^*$, $\mathbf W$, $\mathbf Z$ with the $\mathbf M^H$ relation; 90:336-385 Genomic prediction table): $\mathbf Z$ = centred dosage only; $\mathbf W$ = training incidence; $\mathbf G^*$, $w_G$ (not the index weight $w$); $\sigma^2_\beta$, $\sigma^2_k$, $\lambda_\beta$, $\mu$/$\hat\mu$ (with "not X" against the other uses of $\mu$ in the heterosis, selection and pipeline tables), $\mathbf V$ (defined in the prose, `V` in the oracle chunk), $r_i^2$, $M_e$, $\pi$, $\nu$, $S$; CV0/CV1/CV2 note.
- Assumes: the assumptions above, in particular: additive trait generated from the markers the models are given, so $\mathrm{Var}(\mathbf g) = \mathbf G\sigma^2_u$ is exact up to the scalar and no model is misspecified; inbred parents and the complete factorial for the rank bound and the line-score identity; $\mathbf G$ centred on its own frequencies for $\mathbf G\mathbf 1 = \mathbf 0$; $h^2$ known; $w_G = 0.95$ unless a sentence varies it; one population ($N = 625$, seed 1) and one draw of the trait, `book_cfg` scale; CV splits by line within one generation; oligogenic arm = one draw of three effects (effectively monogenic); 15 paired splits per sweep cell, 8 per `pi` value, 5 seeds for BayesC$\pi$, 20 replicates per CV scheme, 40 for the reliability draws; paired $t$ with normality unchecked, the 8 contrasts one Bonferroni family and the chapter's other tests not folded in; Gibbs chains seeded, BayesA's marker variance moves by an order of magnitude with the seed; production scale and real germplasm not run. Sources: read as reported in the text (Technow et al. 2014 by refcheck at full text, bWGR paper); not read or abstract-level: see Owed.
- Takes from: `00`: A2, A3, @sec-hybrid-programme (genomic prediction supplies the S4 values, @tbl-hp-stages; GCA under dominance at the opposite pool's frequencies). `01`: @sec-coancestry (the $\mathbf G$ of @eq-vanraden-g, GBLUP), @sec-why-not-g and @eq-g-sum-zero, `vanraden_G()`. `03`: @sec-simulating-crosses (`ctx$traits = scale(Z B)`, the callout on the optimum on known values, the `stage1_build()` contract, @tbl-stage1-contract, `build_ctx()`). `05`: @eq-era-rank, @sec-genomic-era (the rank argument, A5 marker panel = causal set). `05b`: @sec-heterosis, @sec-heterosis-gca-sca (dominance effects $d_k$, SCA). `07`: @sec-choosing-error (Finding 5: nominal $r$, $u$/$\hat u$, correlated error). `08`: @sec-stages (S4 taken as given; the memory saving). Forward pointers: @sec-ocs (`ocs_quadprog`), @sec-mating-genomic (`04e`, published T2/T1/T0), @sec-sparse-validation (`04f`, the other meaning of CV labels), @sec-cycles (`11b`), @sec-case-study (`12`, repeats the caveat; kept, 12:164-170).

## 09-optimal-contributions -- Optimal contributions and the $\alpha$ constraint
- Question: what optimisation problem the recommendation of 07 defines (put $\theta$ and $N_{e,\text{par}}$ in the optimiser, keep the expensive metrics for validation) once $\alpha$ is a budget, where that problem sits in the optimal-contribution literature, and what its continuous relaxation does and does not bound. It follows 08b only through the Metrics-and-prediction part's close: it opens from @sec-choosing-recommendation (09:11-13), defines $\alpha$ by @eq-ocs-alpha from @eq-catalogue-alpha / @sec-baseline, takes `ctx$index` from @sec-simulating-crosses and points to @sec-genomic-prediction only for how a real programme obtains the values to hand to `stage1_build()` (09:25-27). It opens the Optimisation part (09 -> 10 -> 10b).
- Establishes:
  - Assumptions of the whole chapter, as a numbered list (09:30-55): (1) inbred parents, A2, so $\mathbf f$ over hybrids is a function of $\mathbf f^L$; (2) no dominance, additive index, A7; (3) the index is the truth ($\bar u_S$ computed on `ctx$index`); for an *exact* optimiser the true index of the plan found on the truth is at least that of the plan found on predictions, so the values are an upper limit, "not extended" to the chapter's heuristics (09-M11); (4) marker panel = causal set, A5, dropped only in the panel sensitivity (index fixed, only $\mathbf f$ thinned); (5) the constraint pins one lens: $\theta$, $GD$, $\alpha$ are the homozygosity lens, $F_{\text{drift}}$ (anchored on `ctx$p0`) is not pinned, and with inbred parents $\alpha$ acts on $GD_T$ including the between-pool $GD_{BS}$ the programme wants preserved; (6) teaching scale, 25 + 25 lines, 2000 markers, $N = 625$, $n = 60$, seed 1, one founder draw.
  - The problem (09:57-70): @eq-ocs-alpha $\alpha(S) = (GD_{\text{ref}} - GD_S)/GD_{\text{ref}}$, $GD_S = 1 - \theta_S$, $\theta_S = \mathbf c'\mathbf f\mathbf c$ with $c_i = 1/n$ on $S$ (`theta_group()`), $GD_{\text{ref}}$ the mean $GD$ of random $n$-subsets (`gd_ref`, `null_distribution()`); @eq-ocs-problem $\max \bar u_S$ subject to $\alpha(S) \le \alpha_{\max}$; @eq-ocs-theta-max $\theta_{\max} = 1 - GD_{\text{ref}}(1 - \alpha_{\max})$. $\alpha_{\max}$ is the **budget** (what the programme accepts); $\alpha_{\text{trunc}}$ is the **ceiling**, the $\alpha$ of the truncation plan at which the constraint stops binding, not the largest $\alpha$ any $n$-subset reaches (09:433-445; as in 06:915-925 and `stage2_select`, R/11:95-101). A budget $\ge \alpha_{\text{trunc}}$ restricts nothing and `stage2_select()` warns. $\alpha_{\text{trunc}} = 0.0412$ ($B = 400$, seed 1; third decimal depends on $B$). 90 has rows for $\alpha_{\text{trunc}}$, $\theta_{\max}$, $V(\lambda)$, $\bar u_S$ (90:304-307, 09-M9 closed); 90:298 keeps $\lambda$ = OCS penalty.
  - Constraint against weighted sum (09:72-90): the optima of $\max \bar u_S - \lambda\theta_S$ over all $\lambda$ lie on the upper concave envelope of the pairs $(\theta_S, \bar u_S)$, so a budget between two vertices is the optimum of no $\lambda$; the multiplier is moved from input to output, the committee sets the budget, the analyst traces its cost (@fig-ocs-frontier) and reads the exchange rate off the trace ($\lambda^* = 68.1$ per unit $\theta$, 0.22 index per percentage point of $\alpha$ on this grid).
  - **Penalty units** (09:92-122, @tbl-ocs-penalty, asserted against `make_fitness()` on the truncation plan at a binding 1% and an inactive 5% budget): `viol = max(0, alpha - alpha_max)` is the **absolute** excess in units of $\alpha$, the fitness subtracts `penalty * viol` with `penalty = 1000` from $\bar u_S$; the price per unit of $\alpha$ is the same at every budget, so a 10% overshoot costs $1000 \times 0.1\,\alpha_{\max}$ (1 and 5 index units at 1% and 5%). The `max_use` term adds the parent slots used beyond the cap divided by $2n$; `max_use` is the largest number of the $n$ hybrids that may share one line as a parent (a count; enforced in @sec-combinatorial), default `NULL`, off here. The constraint is a linear penalty, not refused at any price: `alpha_loss()` of a returned plan must be checked; @eq-ocs-bound treats the budget as exact.
  - Lineage (09:125-161): Kempthorne & Nordskog (1959) pose the restricted-index problem (problem statement read in full text by refcheck, solution not read); the restricted index is 02b's matrix form, the book's own; the one fact used is that it holds its restricted direction at zero on 02b's two-trait example (asserted: $< 10^{-12}$, $-2.6 \times 10^{-17}$ against 0.1430 unrestricted; $\boldsymbol\Sigma_G = [[2, .3], [.3, .3]]$ and $\boldsymbol\Sigma_P - \boldsymbol\Sigma_G$ positive definite). What is specific here is the object restricted (a loss of molecular $GD$ against a resampling baseline), an inequality, a penalty in the heuristic and a constraint in the bound. Gomez-Romano et al. (2016) eq. 1a-1d is @eq-ocs-gr with their $\mathbf G$ written $\mathbf f$ (09:163-188); the constraint form is Meuwissen et al. (2020) Methods, via 02b, up to the constant $\tfrac12$; @eq-ocs-problem is that form restricted to $c_i \in \{0, 1/n\}$. Contributions attach to a hybrid here, not an individual or a line; "optimal cross selection" (Gorjanc et al. 2018) cited for the name only, not read (09:189-202).
  - **The valid bound** (@sec-relaxation, 09:204-266). @eq-ocs-V $V(\lambda) = \max \mathbf c'\mathbf u - \lambda \mathbf c'\mathbf f\mathbf c$ over $\sum c_i = 1$, $0 \le c_i \le 1/n$, attained at $\mathbf c_\lambda$ with $\bar u_\lambda$, $\theta_\lambda$, so $V = \bar u_\lambda - \lambda\theta_\lambda$ (`ocs_relaxation()`, projected gradient on the capped simplex). (1) $V(0)$ = truncation. (2) **@eq-ocs-weak** (weak duality): for every $S$ with $|S| = n$ and every $\lambda \ge 0$, $\bar u_S \le V(\lambda) + \lambda\theta_S = \bar u_\lambda + \lambda(\theta_S - \theta_\lambda)$; so a relaxation point bounds every discrete plan **at least as diverse as itself** ($\theta_S \le \theta_\lambda$), and at exactly equal diversity a plan cannot beat it. (3) What does not follow: a plan that spends the budget has $\theta_S$ near $\theta_{\max}$ and is *less* diverse than a relaxation point strictly inside the budget; @eq-ocs-weak is then the tangent of slope $\lambda$ at the point, which lies above it, so the height of "the best relaxation point inside the budget" is not an upper bound. **@eq-ocs-bound**: $\bar u_S \le \min_{\lambda\ge0}[V(\lambda) + \lambda\theta_{\max}]$ for every $S$ with $\theta_S \le \theta_{\max}$; every $\lambda$ gives a valid bound, so a coarse grid costs tightness not validity; by Lagrangian duality (Boyd & Vandenberghe 2004, 5.2.3; Slater: a feasible $\mathbf c$ with $\mathbf c'\mathbf f\mathbf c < \theta_{\max}$) the minimum over all $\lambda$ is the continuous frontier at the budget, and a discrete plan lies on or below it at its own $\alpha$. `ocs_bound()` returns the minimum over a $\lambda$ grid (16 values, 1 to 316), doubles the projected-gradient iterations until the bound value $V(\lambda) + \lambda\theta_{\max}$ changes by less than a relative `tol = 1e-3` and carries `converged`, a stalling criterion on the doubling, not a certificate; the count that converges at this scale does not at $N = 10{,}000$ (pointer to @sec-bound, carried, not re-measured).
  - Numbers at the 1% budget, teaching scale, seed 1 (@tbl-ocs-relaxation 09:288-296, chunk asserts the order, 09:269-285, 317-336): best relaxation point of the grid inside the budget 1.3473 < best greedy plan inside the budget 1.4227 (at $\alpha$ = 0.00685) < `ocs_bound()` 1.5532 ($\lambda^* = 68.13$, 8000 iterations = 4000 doubled once, `converged` TRUE); independent exact-QP check by the it2 verdict: 1.5399 at $\lambda$ = 80.8, so the grid is 0.013 loose. The greedy-to-bound gap, 0.13 index units, is an upper limit on what a better search could gain at this budget on this dataset, only if `converged`; closing it is @sec-advanced (09:337-349). The sweep is the greedy over the index weight $\omega$, 60 values logarithmic from $10^{-6}$ to $10^{-1}$: 39 distinct plans, 11 return truncation (asserted $\ge 20$ distinct); a grid in the wrong units collapses the frontier to one point (09:308-316, 477-489).
  - Under-convergence (callout 09:378-390, @tbl-ocs-convergence 09:392-432, asserted): projected gradient approaches $V(\lambda)$ from below, so an under-converged relaxation understates $V$ and the bound stops being one; the diagnostic is the penalised $V$, not the index (the index of a converged relaxation can lie below a discrete plan's). At $\lambda = 10$: $V$ = -5.1939 (50 it), -4.8790 (500), -4.8748 (4000), -4.874792 (16000); discrete truncation -4.8840; relative change 4000 to 16000 is 1.8e-6; weak duality asserted for all 63 discrete plans (3 selections + 60 greedy). The $10^{-3}$ is applied to $V$ at $\lambda = 10$ here and to the bound value in `ocs_bound()` (09-M8, closed). Larger $\lambda$ and $N$ not measured here.
  - Setting the budget (09:433-489): the budget is not chosen a priori; the frontier is the set of distinct greedy plans (a lower bound on the constrained optimum, not the optimum); secant slopes of the interpolated frontier 0.52, 0.31, 0.22, 0.15, 0.10 index per percentage point between $\alpha$ = -1, 0, 1, 2, 3% and $\alpha_{\text{trunc}}$: the cost of diversity is largest at low $\alpha$ and falls; no knee located or claimed.
  - **Budget invariance, 20 founders** (09:490-613, @tbl-ocs-panel-seed1, @tbl-ocs-panel-spread, @tbl-ocs-thin; A5 dropped: `ctx$traits` fixed, only $\mathbf f$, the null and $\alpha_{\text{trunc}}$ rebuilt under MAF $\ge$ 0, 0.01, 0.05, 0.10 and pool-A ascertainment): on seed 1, $GD_{\text{ref}}$ moves across the five panels and $\alpha_{\text{trunc}}$ by 4.65%; over 20 founder draws (simulation seeds 1-20) the movement $100(\max/\min - 1)$ is, for $GD_{\text{ref}}$, a mean of 19.13% (17.72-21.30, s.d. 1.13) and, for $\alpha_{\text{trunc}}$, a mean of 7.76% (s.d. 4.40, range 1.28-16.78, median 6.72), a ratio of 2.46; so the ceiling moves about 2.5 times less than the level it is computed from, **not zero**: a budget is more portable across panels than $GD_{\text{ref}}$, not invariant (the earlier "all three axes leave alpha_max where it was" was withdrawn, 09-D8). Thinning to 250/500/1000 markers (6 subsets each): bias of the mean thinned $\alpha_{\text{trunc}}$ against the full panel -0.0004 / 0.0008 / 0.0001, s.e. 0.0011 / 0.0009 / 0.0004, relative s.d. median 36.1 / 24.3 / 10.7% (range 9.8-81.0 at 250), within 1 s.d. in 20 / 19 / 19 of 20 draws: "no bias detected, resolution about 0.002 against a mean $\alpha_{\text{trunc}}$ of 0.037, a few percent not excluded", and a single thin-panel study has a relative spread of about a third (09-M10). $\alpha_{\text{trunc}}$ is a property of the germplasm and the panel; quote it with the panel (callout 09:635-644). Scope: this simulator at 25 + 25 lines and 2000 markers.
  - **Data and producer**: `book/data/ocs_panel_sweep.csv` (460 rows, 20 seeds x 23 panels) is produced by `Rscript book/scripts/regenerate.R ocs_panel` (`regenerate.R:894-`; ~35 s; deterministic: simulation seeds 1-20, `set.seed(100 + s)` before the thinning, `null_distribution()` default seed; the it2 verdict reran it twice, byte identical to the file); the chunk asserts that its seed-1 row equals the live @tbl-ocs-panel-seed1. The file is **untracked** (see Owed). It is reproducible in this repository, unlike the cached external files of 04e/04f.
  - `null_distribution()` reseeds (callout 09:614-634, chunk `ocs-reseed`, asserted): it calls `set.seed(seed)` (default 7) on entry, so a `sample()` after it inside a loop draws the same values on every pass; draw the randomisation up front or vary `seed`.
  - A caveat from the OCS literature (09:645-686, @eq-ocs-gr-delta): Gomez-Romano et al. (2016, read from the PDF) predict $E(\Delta f_{t+1}) = (\mathbf c_t'\mathbf G_{x,t}\mathbf c_t - f_t)/(1 - f_t)$ and find it biased downward for *regional* coancestry (CHR_ove): with a 0.1% restriction the realised rate is about 1.4, 0.3, 0.2% at $N$ = 20, 100, 200 (Fig. 1, ratios about 14, 3, 2), and about double the intended rate in one $N = 20$ resampling analysis (Fig. 2); "no single factor of two follows"; the mechanism is the authors' suggestion, not a derivation. It does not affect the $\theta_S$ computed in the book (no forward prediction); it would matter if a further generation is planned from a contribution vector, where $\mathbf c'\mathbf f\mathbf c$ underestimates and the margin is not measured.
  - Continuous QP (09:687-738, @tbl-ocs-qp, runs only where `quadprog` is installed): all 50 lines, $\mathbf f^L$, merit `line_merit` = mean index of the hybrids using the line as a parent (value against the opposite pool as tester, known index, deterministic); `upper` is a share of the pooled contribution; `upper` $= \texttt{max\_use}/(2n)$ puts the QP cap and the discrete cap on one scale (0.20 at $n = 60$ is `max_use` = 24); "same in kind, not the same constraint" (a share of a continuous vector against a count enforced in the decoder); the QP has a single margin (no per-pool sum) and `share_A` shows what that permits (0.60 at small $\lambda$); at most 5 lines at the 20% cap. Independent `solve.QP` check by the it2 verdict.
  - What belongs in the inner loop (09:739-757): this chapter times nothing and computes none of allelic richness, A-NE or Nei's $G_{st}$; it states the problem whose returned plan those would validate; the line route is slower per call at $N = 2{,}500$, $n = 50$, cheaper only for large $n$ and the only one that exists at $N = 22{,}500$ (07). The optimiser does $10^5$-$10^6$ evaluations (`NP` x `itermax`).
  - Cross-chapter facts fixed in this review: 07:1015 now says 09 computes none of the three; 10b:637 and 10b:744-752 now say the relaxation was once read off as a bound and that 09 now shows the bound; the `ocs_relaxation()` / `ocs_bound()` / `make_fitness()` roxygen in R/03 and R/12 (first paragraph) and the comment in R/01 `freq_metrics` were reworded, no code change (515 expectations pass).
- Assumes: the six assumptions above, in particular inbred lines, an additive index equal to the truth (an upper limit only for an exact optimiser), the homozygosity lens only, `book_cfg` teaching scale and one founder draw (seed 1, $B = 400$ for the null, 250 in the panel tables); an $\alpha$ against `gd_ref` from random subsets of the same size; $\mathbf f$ positive semi-definite (Gram matrix) for convexity and strong duality, with Slater's condition met at the 1% budget (relaxation $\alpha$ down to -1.15% at $\lambda$ = 316); the bound statements hold for $n$-subsets (equal weights $1/n$) and the cap $c_i \le 1/n$; `converged` TRUE for the numbers quoted, a stalling criterion; the greedy frontier is a heuristic (a lower bound, not the optimum); the panel sensitivity drops A5; the budget ceiling's spread is measured on 20 founder draws at one scale and one platform. Not measured: production scale, $N = 10{,}000$ convergence, hybrids planned a further generation (Gomez-Romano bias), dominance, estimated (predicted) values.
- Takes from: `00`: A2, A5, A7, A8 (@sec-era-programme), @sec-hybrid-programme ($GD_S = 1 - \theta_S$). `01`/`02`: @sec-coancestry ($\mathbf f$, $\theta$), `optimal_contributions()` of @sec-subdivided for the QP, @eq-gd-partition ($GD_T$ with $GD_{BS}$). `02b`: @sec-response (the restricted index, @tbl-response-restricted, the four differences from the $\alpha$ constraint, the lineage reported through Meuwissen et al. 2020 and Kempthorne & Nordskog 1959), @sec-response-genic. `03`: @sec-simulating-crosses (`ctx$index`, the generator, `build_ctx()`). `05`: @sec-genomic-era ($F_{\text{drift}}$, `ctx$p0`). `06`: @sec-baseline and @eq-catalogue-alpha ($\alpha$, $GD_{\text{ref}}$, the ceiling), @sec-catalogue (A-NE). `07`: @sec-choosing-recommendation, @sec-collapse (the line collapse, $\mathbf f^L$), @sec-choosing-cost, @sec-choosing-error (selection on predicted values, correlated error), `max_use` (07:531-538). `08b`: @sec-genomic-prediction (how a real programme obtains the values for `stage1_build()`). Forward pointers: @sec-combinatorial (`decode()`, `max_use` enforcement, the recipe), @sec-advanced and @sec-bound (closing the gap; the bound at scale), @sec-mating (`04e`, contributions assigned to crosses under a field limit). ## 10 -- combinatorial selection (OPEN after 3, user arbitration pending: 10-D19) -- PROVISIONAL
- Question (provisional): how the problem of 09 is searched in practice once it is a combinatorial problem on hybrids: the four baselines, the greedy sweep on a grid of $\omega$, the encoding problem and the repair bias, a warm-started differential evolution (`sel_de()`) and what its evaluation budget does to the comparison with a fine greedy sweep, the frontier and the strategy table (`.claude/review/10-combinatorial-selection/it3-verdict.md`, `it2-response.md`; book headings 10:180, 366, 609, 695, 964, 1361, 1480). It follows 09 because 09 states the constrained problem and its relaxation and points to @sec-combinatorial for `decode()` and the enforcement of `max_use`.
- Establishes (provisional; only what the it3 verdict closed): the evaluation budget `NP x itermax` is stated wherever a DE result is quoted, and the thesis "DE below greedy" holds at 30,000 evaluations and reverses at 120,000 and 600,000 for the 1% budget only (new subsection `sec-combinatorial-budget`: 0 / 10 / 10 of 10 seeds above the best feasible fine greedy, 1.42265; means 1.3343, 1.4384, 1.4814; one plan over budget at 120,000, alpha 1.000057%, still carried by 10-M19); $\omega = \Delta\theta/\Delta u$, coancestry per index s.d. (10-D18, closed), the reciprocal dimension of $\lambda$ in @eq-ocs-bound; the window between the $\omega = 0$ plan and truncation is a scale, not a threshold (10-D16, closed); `max_use` and `decode()` are defined and enforced there (the 09 keep-true item, 10 side). The strategy table (10:1480 on) is closed for the 1% budget only ("no recommendation of `sel_de()` over a fine greedy sweep" at the other four budgets).
- Assumes (provisional): the 09 assumption list unchanged (10:~11 and the assumption 3 that 10b cites at 10b:1832), `book_cfg` teaching scale, seed 1; one founder draw; cached `de_constraints.csv` is external and not reproducible here.
- Takes from: `09` (@sec-ocs: the problem, $\alpha$, the budget, @sec-relaxation), `07` (@sec-choosing: the restrictions table and the cached run), `06` (@sec-baseline), `02b`.
- Owed: -> **user** (10-D19, the only open disqualifying finding): 10:489-493 "a grid of round numbers such as {0.25, 0.5, ..., 30} -- the one `sel_de()` hard-codes for its warm start -- lies entirely above the weight from which the plan is truncation ... which makes greedy look an order of magnitude worse than it is". The grid `sel_de()` hard-codes is `c(0, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16)` (10:1036), contains $\omega = 0$ and returns two distinct plans; "an order of magnitude" has no number behind it. The user arbitrates in one sentence; until then this entry is not final and nothing here may be cited as established. Other open: 10-M5 (orphan figure files, stale `_freeze`/`docs`/`downloads`, render batch), 10-M19 (10:1262-1264 the "all 10 seeds" sentence omits the one over-budget plan). Dependants (10b's list, below) are owed by 11, 11b, 12, 07, 90; this entry does not settle them.

## 10b-advanced-selection -- Advanced selection: structure, bounds and scale
- Question: what the structure of the problem of 10 (a Gram matrix `f`, a max-sum objective, a partition matroid, the line collapse) buys, and what it does not; whether a swap-based local search, a line-encoded DE, a rounded line-space programme and an exact oracle say anything about how far the heuristics are from the optimum; what a valid bound is and why the construction 10's convergence check once used was not one; and what survives at 200 hybrids out of 10,000. It follows 10 because 10 stops at a warm-started DE treated as a black box ("@sec-combinatorial stops at a warm-started differential evolution. It is not [a black box]", 10b:50-55), takes its three objects and assumptions unchanged (10b:132-137) and says its own scales by name (10b:62-99); it spends the 09 pointers to @sec-advanced (closing the greedy-to-bound gap) and @sec-bound (the bound that does not converge at $N = 10{,}000$). It is the last chapter of the Optimisation part; its results are one dataset per scale and optimiser seeds 1-3, so they are measurements of routes, not of methods.
- Establishes:
  - **Scales, replication, provenance** (10b:62-130, asserted in the setup): "book scale" = the live 25 + 25 / 2000-marker instance ($N = 625$, $n = 60$, seed 1, $B = 400$ for `gd_ref`); `search_benchmark.csv` rows `book` and `production` are the *benchmark book scale* (same dataset, $B = 500$, cap 5) and the *benchmark production scale* ($N = 10{,}000$, $L = 200$, $n = 200$, cap 4: a 100 x 100 factorial, distinct from 10's "package production scale" of 71 + 71 lines, $N = 5041$). The unit of replication is the optimiser seed (1-3) on **one** dataset per scale; deterministic and capped rows have seed s.d. 0 (asserted), so a standard error of 0 means three equal differences, not a known difference. Files are written by `regenerate.R search` / `search_exact` (reproducible here; indices to the digit, timings one machine). The DE budget of the benchmark is `NP` = 100, `itermax` = 300 (30,000 nominal evaluations) against 30,000 proposed moves per local search; "DE below greedy" is the 30,000 reading, not a property of the method (10b:100-113).
  - **Three facts** (@sec-advanced-facts 10b:139-262): `f` is a Gram matrix exactly (stacked vectors $[\mathbf x_i; \mathbf 1 - \mathbf x_i]$; asserted, smallest eigenvalue zero to the eigensolver), so $\mathbf c'\mathbf f\mathbf c$ is convex and the budget a convex constraint; $\psi_{ij} = f_{ii} + f_{jj} - 2f_{ij}$ (squared Euclidean distance, negative type; **not** $d_{ij} = 1 - f_{ij}$ of E-NE/A-NE) with @eq-advanced-maxsum $\sum_{i<j \in S}\psi_{ij} = n\,\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf f_S) - n^2\theta_S$ (asserted, 1e-12): at a fixed $n$ maximising $GD$ is minimising the submatrix sum, the max-sum diversification of Borodin et al.; "not found named in the plant-breeding literature", no screen run; the per-line cap $k_{\max}$ (= `max_use`) is a partition matroid per pool, feasibility needs $n \le L_A k_{\max}$ and $n \le L_B k_{\max}$; the three properties and what each is worth (@tbl-advanced-structure: convexity and the line collapse large, the approximation guarantees small).
  - **Lazy greedy buys nothing** (10b:278-309): `sel_greedy()` already carries the running sum, every marginal gain is $O(1)$; $1 - 1/e$ and $1/(p+1)$ are proved for monotone non-negative submodular functions, which this objective is not in general (the index is standardised, $\bar u_S$ can be negative), so they are statements about the constraint class, not about a run.
  - **The move that was already paid for** (@sec-localsearch 10b:311-552): `theta_state()` / `theta_swap()` update $\theta$ in $O(L)$ (identity asserted against `theta_group()`, @tbl-advanced-eval-cost: swap 3.3 us at $N = 3{,}600$, 4.2 us at 10,000, hybrid fitness 40 / 83 us and 132 us, line-encoded 77 / 169 us, decode 3 / 159 us). The line encoding does **not** make an evaluation cheaper (it is slower, decode sorts $N$); it buys a smaller space; the order-of-magnitude saving is the swap, available only to a method that moves in the space of selections (as CLAUDE.md says). `sel_local_search()`: `start = NULL` builds the warm start (and needs the $N \times N$ `f` for it), cap tested not punished, self-calibrated temperature, objective $\bar u_S$ minus `penalty` x violation **divided by `alpha_max`** (a relative violation, unlike `make_fitness()`'s absolute one, 10b:419-427). Twenty seeds, 20,000 and 30,000 moves (@tbl-advanced-ls-modes): hill vs anneal $+0.93$ (s.e. 1.41) and $-0.92$ (s.e. 1.10) thousandths, not separated, no ordering that depends on the budget; tempering below both in 20 of 20 seeds at both budgets, a measurement of this implementation at default `n_chains` and ladder, the budget-splitting a candidate cause not tested (10b:537-552). Three checks before use: realised $\alpha$, `max_line`, $N_{e,\text{par}}$.
  - **Against DE at equal wall-clock** (@sec-advanced-lsvsde 10b:554-724, @fig-advanced-anytime, @tbl-advanced-benchmark-book, @tbl-advanced-ls-vs-de): at the 20,000-to-30,000 budget each local-search mode is ahead of the DE at the wall-clock of each of the six DE points (asserted); the local search is ahead of `sel_de()` by 0.1676 (s.e. 0.0106) at book and 0.3701 (0.0089) at production, and of the line-encoded DE by more, in less time; this is a comparison of **whole algorithms** (neighbourhood, acceptance rule, warm start together), explains time and not index, and is not a result about `sel_de()` at its default (10 finds the DE above the fine greedy at four times the budget). The greedy sweep is 9.8% below the leader on the benchmark 20-value grid and 6.9% on the 60-value grid of 10; at production greedy is 17% below `hill` and 1.5x cheaper, so its economy is a book-scale property.
  - **The encoding, `sel_de_fast()`** (10b:754-840, @tbl-advanced-de-pair): `sel_de()` minus `sel_de_fast()` $+0.3004$ (s.e. 0.0087, t 34.6 on 2 df) uncapped at book, $+0.1103$ (0.0322, t 3.4, p 0.08; not resolved) at production, capped $+0.0041$ (0) and $0$ (0); still differs in seeding (eight seeds along a line-diversity direction) and decoder, so it is not an isolated encoding effect. Capped rows are not a test of the search (the decoder satisfies the cap by construction). `sel_de_fast()`'s niche, narrowed (@sec-defast-restrictions 10b:842-988): **pool balance cannot bind** (every candidate is one A x B cross; share 0.5 for any such set, asserted; `pool_tol` vacuous); the **$N_{e,\text{par}}$ floor of 30 binds and the run did not meet it** (four runs, floors 20/25/30 at `itermax` 300 and floor 30 at 1,500: one index 1.1056, $N_{e,\text{par}}$ 25.71, same line usage, missed by 4.29); the capped greedy sweep has 5 plans with $\alpha \le 1\%$ and $N_{e,\text{par}} \ge 30$, best 0.673, penalised 0.815 against 0.391 for the DE plan; it also does not lead on index where a floor is slack (annealed local search at the same cap, @tbl-advanced-de-fast-vs-local); `sel_local_search()` lacks an `ne_par_min` argument, an implementation gap (@eq-advanced-ne-par-counts $N_{e,\text{par}} = (2n)^2/\sum_a k_a^2$, $p_a = k_a/2n$ in @eq-catalogue-ne-par, an $O(1)$ update of `cnt` per swap, asserted).
  - **The bound was not a bound** (@sec-bound 10b:990-1192, supersedes 09-M12b): by @eq-ocs-weak a relaxation point bounds every plan at least as diverse as itself and at exactly equal diversity a plan cannot beat it; what it does not bound is a plan that spends the budget when the point lies strictly inside it (tangent of slope $\lambda$, @sec-relaxation statement 3). The old construction (best relaxation point of the grid inside the budget) fell below a feasible plan at all four budgets (`old_valid` FALSE; four rows from a few distinct points, one dataset, best of eight plans, a statement about finite grids, @tbl-advanced-old-construction); a point moved onto the 1% budget by bisection (alpha 0.9921%, index 1.5373) sits above the plan 1.5202, consistent with @eq-ocs-weak and "one point against one plan" (@tbl-advanced-point-at-budget). The valid statement is @eq-ocs-bound, cited not re-derived. `ocs_bound()`: grid of 25 multipliers 0.1 to 1000, doubling to a relative `tol` 0.001, `converged` is a stalling criterion and **not a certificate**; 2 x 2 decomposition (@tbl-advanced-bound-grids): 12/2000 1.5355, 12/8000 1.5399, 25/2000 1.5468, 25/8000 (`ocs_bound`) 1.5532; iterations $+0.28\%$, grid $+0.74\%$ (the 25-point grid skips $\lambda$ 81.1 near the minimiser, 0.87% looser at equal iterations); $B = 400$ vs 500 changes the third decimal. A bound can serve as a stopping rule only conditional on convergence having been signalled. 10's convergence check was corrected to `ocs_bound()` (10b:1177-1192).
  - **Rounding the relaxation** (10b:1194-1320, @tbl-advanced-round-plan, -reconcile, -frontier): `ocs_round()` = line merit (a GCA, against the opposite pool as represented, @sec-heterosis-gca-sca) -> `ocs_quadprog()` at a $\lambda$ with each line capped at `max_use`/$2n$ and a single margin -> floor and largest remainders -> greedy b-matching of crosses -> $\lambda$ chosen by budget; index 1.4869, gap 4.27% against the converged bound; book vs cached benchmark differ only by `gd_ref` ($B$ 400 vs 500, third decimal); falls back to the least-loss row without warning if none is feasible; with a cap the bound goes loose (the relaxation carries no cap).
  - **Exact oracle** (@sec-oa 10b:1322-1460, @eq-advanced-oa-cut, @tbl-advanced-oracle): HiGHS cannot take MIQP; outer approximation on the convex `f`, the dual bound read from `mip_dual_bound`. Proved optimal on one $N = 36$ instance at three budgets (20 to 73 cuts, local search leaves nothing on the table there); at $N = 625$ it stops at 300 s after 4 cuts (interval against the Lagrangian bound 1.5543 is the tighter, 10.1% vs 1.7% for the same plan; neither called certified); no run with a cap, none at $N = 10{,}000$; why the loop stalls was not diagnosed. Not production (belongs with `ref_*`, @sec-verification).
  - **Scaling to 200 of 10,000** (@sec-scaling 10b:1462-1780, @fig-advanced-scaling, @tbl-advanced-scaling): memory (`f` is 0.745 GiB at $N = 10{,}000$, `fL` 0.31 MiB; fixed by the line route), search dimension (both DEs are 200-dimensional; not fixed by the encoding; what the collapse moves to $L$ is the contributions space), cost per evaluation (fixed only by not decoding). **Which routes run without `f`** (@sec-ffree 10b:1546-1653): only with an explicit `start =`, and `null_distribution()`, `alpha_loss()`, the greedy sweep and the rounding route need the matrix (five named failures, one unnamed: `sel_greedy()`); the matrix bought a starting point on this instance. **Head-to-head at production** (@sec-advanced-headtohead 10b:1655-1777): the cap binds and the budget does not ($2n/L = 2$ against a cap of 4); capped routes tie within 0.001 index units at production (spread 0.0002, deterministic on one dataset; the margin a convention fixed after the two differences were known, any value in 0.0002-0.0025 gives the same classification; 10b-M19) and do **not** tie at book scale (greedy_cap 1.1122 > de_hybrid_cap = tempering_cap 1.1097 > de_lines_cap = ocs_round_cap 1.1056, a 6.6-thousandth span); the bound did not converge at $N = 10{,}000$ in the file (4,000 and 8,000 iterations, 9 multipliers), the step-size explanation was not tested, and no gap is reported.
  - **What not to reach for** (@sec-notreach 10b:1799-1841): reinforcement learning (Younis 2024: a sequential process, a different question), Bayesian optimisation (argued from cost and dimension, not run), DPP sampling ($\log\det(\mathbf I + \beta\mathbf f_S)$ submodular by Chen 2018; monotone and non-negative the book's own argument, eigenvalues $\ge 1$ asserted; not the $\theta$ lens, so 09 assumption 5 / 10 assumption 3 applies, 10b:1829-1832), NSGA-II (not run). **Which method to use** (@tbl-advanced-which 10b:1843-1935, protocol 3-6 at 10b:1906-1935) and the closing statement (10b:1937-1975, epigraph 1960-1975): measured is the cost of the move and the matrix; **not** measured is how much of any index difference is due to the state; the one near-comparison (`sel_de()` vs `sel_de_fast()`) still differs in seeding and decoder; the guarantees, the Lagrangian bound (converged at book, not at production) and the oracle ($N = 36$ only) bound the distance to the optimum only where they apply.
  - Symbols (90:319, table "Advanced selection", each with its clash named): $\mathbf x$, $b$, $s$, $k_{\max}$, $p$, $\beta$, $\mathbf f_S$, $\mathrm{tr}$, $\psi_{ij}$ (the row for $k_{\max}$ is there).
- Assumes: the assumptions of 10 / 09 (inbred parent lines, so $\mathbf f$ is a function of $\mathbf f^L$ and the problem is $L$-dimensional; `f` is molecular coancestry, not centred, so a Gram matrix; additive index equal to the truth; one lens, $\theta$, chosen deliberately); a 25 + 25 / 2000-marker teaching scale and a 100 x 100 factorial at $N = 10{,}000$; one dataset per scale; optimiser seeds 1-3 (20 seeds on the one live dataset for the local-search panel); one budget (1%, $B$ 400 live, 500 in the file); `NP` 100, `itermax` 300; per-line cap 5 / 4; timings from one machine; the Lagrangian bound valid where `converged`, a stalling criterion not a proof; the oracle at $N = 36$, no cap. Not measured: other datasets, other budgets, the local search against the DE at larger budgets, the encoding effect isolated from seeding and decoder, the share of any index difference due to the state, tempering at tuned settings, the oracle with a cap or at large $N$, the step-size explanation of the production non-convergence, Bayesian optimisation, NSGA-II.
- Takes from: `00`: A2, @sec-era-step7 (Gram form). `03`: @sec-sim-assumptions (Gram form of `f`). `06`: @eq-catalogue-theta, @eq-catalogue-ne-par, @sec-baseline (`gd_ref` resampled at the size used). `07`: @sec-collapse ($\theta = \mathbf w'\mathbf f^L\mathbf w$), @sec-choosing (the cost benchmark refusing to form the $22{,}500^2$ matrix, the `$` partial-matching callout, 07:226). `09`: @sec-ocs (assumption 5, `line_merit`, the cap as a share of $2n$), @sec-relaxation (@eq-ocs-weak, @eq-ocs-bound, @eq-ocs-V, @eq-ocs-theta-max), 09's penalty units (the absolute violation of `make_fitness()`). `10` (OPEN, provisional): @sec-combinatorial (the three objects, assumptions, `decode()`, `max_use`, the 60-value fine grid, @sec-combinatorial-budget, @tbl-combinatorial-de-lines, assumption 3). `05b`: @sec-heterosis-gca-sca (line merit is a GCA against a tester pool). `92`: @sec-verification. External: Borodin et al. 2017, Nemhauser 1978, Cevallos 2016, Chen 2018, Kulesza 2012, De Beukelaer 2018, Yadav 2025 (abstract + full text read; "OPV" is the source's acronym, not expanded), Ahadi 2024 (Nature PDF read: "could make", "may consider", "likely settle for"), Waldmann 2025, Duran & Grossmann 1986, Minoux 1978, Fisher et al. 1978 (the last three not read in full).

Struck from the 09 entry.
